<summary>This is the title page of Robert Fludd's 1629 defense against Marin Mersenne, listing dozens of famous scholars, theologians, and philosophers whom Mersenne supposedly insulted. Fludd frames the book as a battle between true Wisdom (Sophia) and Mersenne's Folly (Moria).</summary>

<meta>This page continues the polemic against Marin Mersenne initiated on the title page. While the previous page listed the many scholars Mersenne attacked in his "Questions on Genesis," this page contains two satirical poems written by English scholars (from Oxford and Cambridge) in defense of Robert Fludd. The catchword "Prioris" at the bottom of the previous leaf indicates continuity with the preceding section of the book.</meta>

<margin>On Marin Mersenne, least of the Minims: A Sow rushing against Minerva:</margin>
<margin>A Sow rushing against Minerva: to Marin Mersenne and his *Questions on Genesis*:</margin>

# TO MARIN MERSENNE,
## To the most brotherly of brothers,
### The least of the very least of the Minims; <note>Mersenne belonged to the Order of Minims, whose name means "the least." The author sarcastically uses superlatives to mock his humility.</note>

-> TO KEEP SILENT. <note>original Greek: "ΕΥΦΗΜΕΙΝ" (Euphemein). Often used as a command for ritual silence.</note> <-

> *Why do you paint FLUDD with black charcoal, Mersenne?*
> *Is that color truly suited to the Floods?* <note>A pun on Robert Fludd's name and the Latin word "fluctibus" meaning waves or floods.</note>
> *Is this how Francis of Paola taught you to paint?* <note>St. Francis of Paola was the founder of the Minim order.</note>
> *Or did Satan rather teach you to devise such things?*
> *Why do you tear at the Rosy Brothers <note>The Rosicrucians</note> (you Gargantua) with a*
> *Backbiting tooth? Do curses become a monk?*
> *I warn you: hereafter, spare the paper that is destined to perish;*
> *And submit your stiff neck to the Fluddean yoke.*
> *Believe: open your ear to the Angelic speeches,*
> *Which the sacred Prophet pours from his Rosy mouth.*
> *Here, let the thirsty soul drench itself in eternal gold;*
> *Let the hungry soul live on Nectar and Ambrosia.*

-> Poured out off-hand <note>original Greek: "Αὐτοχεδὶς" (Autoschedis)</note> <-

James Aretius of Oxford.

# MARIN MERSENNE; <note>original: "MAVRINVS MERSENNIVS"</note>
-> rearranged into the anagram: <-
-> **A Sow, rushing against Minerva.** <note>The Latin name "Maurinus Mersennius" is an anagram for "Sus, in Minervam ruens." This was a classical proverb describing an ignorant person challenging a goddess of wisdom.</note> <-

<image-desc>A decorative rectangular woodcut headpiece featuring a central human mask or face surrounded by symmetrical arrangements of scrolling foliage, acanthus leaves, and floral motifs.</image-desc>

# THE CONTENTS OF
# THIS TREATISE.

<margin>This treatise, uncovering and refuting the errors of Mersenne, is divided into 4 Books. Of which:</margin>

The First Book's
> *First Chapter:* Defends Fludd’s Physics concerning the Subject of the Macrocosm. <term>Macrocosm: The "great world" or the entire universe, which Fludd believed was structured like a giant human being.</term>
> *Second Chapter:* [Defends its] Harmony. } He defends its
> *Third Chapter:* [Defends its] Consonances. } cosmic proportions. <note>original: "mundanas." In this context, it refers to the musical and mathematical ratios Fludd believed governed the physical world.</note>
> *Fourth Chapter:* Shows that Fludd’s opinions are not new, and that his doctrine is very far removed from Atheism.

*The Second Book:* Discusses that famous question: whether there is a World Soul <term>World Soul: The "Anima Mundi," an ancient philosophical concept that the universe is a living being with its own spirit.</term>; and finally, it clearly demonstrates that there is a World Soul, by which the world itself is said to live.

The Third Book's
> *First Chapter:* Removes all suspicion of Evil Magic <note>original: "Cacomagiæ," a Greek-derived term for "bad magic" or sorcery, as opposed to "Theurgy" or "Natural Magic."</note> from Fludd. Similarly, it assigns a triple genius <note>A "genius" was considered a guiding spirit or attendant intelligence assigned to a person.</note> to every human being. And it most clearly demonstrates that names can be attributed to Angels based on their properties without any error or sin.
> *Second Chapter:* Shows that the soul can, in a certain way, be called mortal.
> *Third Chapter:* Confirms the certainty of Fludd’s Microcosmic <term>Microcosm: The human being, viewed as a "little world" that perfectly mirrors the structure and laws of the universe.</term> Music.
> *Fourth Chapter:* Responds to the Chiroscopic <note>original: "Chiroscopicam," referring to palmistry or the study of the hand's features.</note> question proposed by Mersenne, according to the requester’s merits and impudence.

The Fourth Book's
> *First Chapter:* Defends Fludd’s Kabbalah <note>The Jewish mystical tradition, which Fludd adapted into his own Christian occult philosophy.</note> and proves Mersenne’s ignorance in that subject.
> *Second Chapter:* Discusses the formal characters of the Kabbalah and examines Mersenne as if by a touchstone. <note>original: "Lapide Lydio," the Lydian stone used to test the purity of gold; a metaphor for a true test of character or knowledge.</note>

<meta>This page continues the introductory matter of Robert Fludd's defense against Marin Mersenne. The previous page outlined the book's structure; this page begins the formal invocation and Fludd’s personal defense against the specific charges of atheism, heresy, and sorcery.</meta>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut headpiece consisting of a horizontal frieze of repeating interlocking circular patterns and flourishes.</image-desc>

# OF SOPHIA OR ETERNAL WISDOM,

->*To the Soul of the world, the salvation of lives, the searcher of hearts and kidneys, and the highest judge of judges, be praise, honor, and glory forever.*<-

<margin>
a Wisdom 7.
b 1 John 5.
c Colossians 3.
d Colossians 1.
e Hebrews 1.
f Psalm 3:5.
</margin>
O Eternal Wisdom [a], you brightness of eternal light, Mirror without blemish of the majesty of God, and image of His goodness; you who, though you are one, can do all things, and remaining in yourself, you make all things new, and through every age you pass into holy souls, making them friends of God and prophets. O [b] the one and only Truth (I say); O [c] Christ, the fountain of life, who are all in all; [d] in whom are hidden all the treasures of Wisdom and Science; [e] since you uphold all things by the word of your power. You (I say), [f] who are a shield for the righteous and a glory, <margin>g Psalm 28:7. h Psalm 7:12.</margin> lifting up his head; O my strength, O my shield and my God, who, since you are a just and strong judge, are indeed [h] a fair searcher of hearts and kidneys <note>original: "animorum & renum." This is a literal translation of a biblical idiom (found in Psalm 7:9) referring to a person’s innermost thoughts and secret emotions.</note>. Behold, I, a man most unworthy of all, humbly beseech you, that [i] <margin>i Psalm 18:34.</margin> you might deign to enter into and look upon this cause of mine, as one who is a just judge, a rock, a cliff, a bulwark, a shield, and a horn of salvation for those who [l] <margin>l Psalm 9:5. m Psalm 7:15.</margin> take refuge in you and trust in you. Consider this cause of mine, O just judge [l], who sit on the highest tribunal, and deliver me from the judgments of the perverse and iniquitous men of this glorious world, who are puffed up with vain pride; [m] since it is their way to labor with injustice, to conceive sorrow, and to bring forth iniquity. Let this cause of mine (I say) be conducted before you, who are accustomed to repay each person according to [n] <margin>n Psalm 72:7.</margin> their merits. For [n] you will judge the poor of the people, and you will save the son of the poor, and you will humble the slanderer who attempts to either impose the crime of Atheism upon him, or at least to feign him its supporter, and to defile his name with the stain of heresy, and even to smear him before the world with the poison of impious Magic.

How far the edge of my mind has always been from the crime of atheism, and how remote even the suspicion of it has been among my countrymen, you alone—from whom the imaginations of men cannot be hidden—are a faithful witness. For from you it cannot be concealed how great my zeal toward you, my God, has always been, and with what devotion I have celebrated your most worthy praises in my labors and midnight studies, according to that talent of intellect which you granted me by your grace [Gen. 1. Ps. 22:4. Ps. 68:34. Col. 3.]. In these works, I have acknowledged, preached, and proclaimed to the world you alone as God and <term>Jehovah</term>, who made heaven and earth; you as the sacrosanct, abiding, and perfect praise of Israel; you as the God of Gods, sitting upon the heaven of heavens; you as the animator and life-giver of all virtues; and indeed, you as all in all. I have sounded this forth everywhere against the insane assertion of the slanderer. Therefore, be a witness for me [Ps. 13:1.], whether I have said in my heart, like the fool, "There is no God."

Furthermore, I should not be considered a heretic rather than a Catholic simply because I am not Roman. It is yours primarily (O most holy spirit of discipline) to examine and debate; it is yours (I say) to judge, since to you alone does this pertain. For you are that <term>Catholic</term> <note>Fludd uses "Catholic" in its original Greek sense of "Universal," arguing that the true Church is the entire world inhabited by God's spirit, not just the institution of Rome.</note> spirit of God filling all things, inhabiting that universal temple or Catholic Church—and not solely the Roman one—insofar as it is not assigned to or contracted within any specific place.

As for what concerns a disposition toward Magic in me: you, whose role it is to search the hearts and kidneys of men, you alone (I say, O just God) be witness and judge, whether I have not always held any member of <term>Cacomagia</term> <note>Evil magic or sorcery, often involving deals with demons.</note> or commerce with the worst and malignant demons in horror and hatred. But truly, if because your testimonies are my meditation, and your com—

<summary>In this opening invocation, Robert Fludd appeals to "Eternal Wisdom" (Christ/the Soul of the World) to judge his work fairly against the accusations of Marin Mersenne. He explicitly denies the charges of atheism and "evil magic," asserting that his philosophy is rooted in a universal, non-sectarian Christianity.</summary>
<keywords>Eternal Wisdom, Sophia, Anima Mundi, Atheism, Magic, Cacomagia, Catholic vs Roman, Robert Fludd, Divine Justice</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the formal invocation to Wisdom (Sophia) from the previous page. The previous page ended mid-sentence with a reference to those who trust in God; this page completes that thought and pivots into a direct defense of "True Magic" against accusations of devilry.</meta>

# A Supplicatory Letter to Wisdom.

<margin>
Psalm 119:99.
Proverbs 2:3.
Job 28:18.
Proverbs 4:6.
</margin>

I strive to guard the gifts you have given me. If indeed I have called upon you (O sacred Wisdom) and inclined my heart to understanding—as the acquisition of such understanding is more valuable than rubies—and if I extol you with due praise (since you are accustomed to lifting up those who trust in you and making them friends of God); if because I venerate, follow, and embrace that <term>True Magic</term> <note>Fludd defines "True Magic" not as sorcery, but as the study of the divine laws of nature and the hidden workings of God in the world.</note> of which you (O blessed rock) are the firm base and foundation; if (I say) because I long for your divine laws and desire the living waters from your rock—should I therefore be pursued by such great malice of the wicked, and by their injuries and curses? Therefore, be my help (O powerful virtue), be to me a rock or a firm crag and a bulwark. I implore you to make me confident and courageous so that I may vigorously and bravely lead this cause of mine—seeing as it is also yours—and so that I may deservedly hurl back the marks of blasphemy upon the head of the blasphemer himself. For he, having despised your majesty and neglected that power which is stronger than all forces, seems to attribute the entire capacity of <term>True Magic</term> to the devil, making no exception. He acts as if you, decreasing in your ancient strength and languishing more and more in power through every age, no longer know how to perform deeds much greater than the devil even today through the mediation of your creatures, whether they be the Angels or others living in heaven and on earth. For to you alone, and not to the devil, belongs the science of True Magic, since you alone are the worker of all wonders. Therefore, O most holy Wisdom, let yours be the victory and triumph over <term>Folly</term> <note>original: "Moriam." This is a personification of Folly, likely a stinging reference to his opponent's lack of divine insight.</note>, that sorceress; to you be perpetual praise, honor, and glory. Amen.

->*Your creature, under the shadow of your wings, trumpeting your praises to the world,*<-

### ->I, the Man.<-
<note>original: "Ego Homo." This was Robert Fludd's preferred way of identifying himself as a humble representative of humanity before the Divine.</note>

<sig>a 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: Doctrine</meta>

<summary>In this concluding section of his invocation, Robert Fludd appeals to Divine Wisdom to defend him against accusations of sorcery. He argues that "True Magic" is a divine science belonging to God alone, and he accuses his detractor (Mersenne) of blasphemy for attributing such power to the devil.</summary>
<keywords>True Magic, Sophia, Robert Fludd, Divine Wisdom, Folly, defense against heresy, natural philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the author's defense of "True Magic" on the previous page to a broader philosophical justification for his work. He moves from defending himself against personal attacks to explaining that his five volumes are intended to reflect the divine "Universal Harmony" of the world, contrasting his peaceful scholarly intent with the "viperous tongues" of his critics.</meta>

Letter

<image-desc>A horizontal ornamental woodcut headpiece featuring symmetrical scrolling foliage, floral patterns, and circular motifs.</image-desc>

## To the sons of doctrine and those raised on the wings of justice, we pray from the heart for Concord, Symphony, and immunity from viperous tongues through Jesus Christ.

As it is my nature's constitution (most distinguished men, most desired judges) to lead a life that is, first and foremost, pleasing to my God, and then to men—a life tranquil and full of charity—I follow the theme of the <term>Mosaic Decalogue</term> <note>The Ten Commandments given to Moses.</note> which was contracted into two by our Savior: namely, honor and fear God with all your heart, and love your neighbor as yourself. Thus (as I hope), in those five volumes of mine now published with the assistance of divine grace, I have conducted myself before both God and men in such a way that no place can be found in them where I have uttered a biting insult <note>original: "dicaculæ probrum"</note> against anyone. Nor, puffed up by vain self-love <note>original: "philautia vana"</note> or led by a foolish and impious desire to disparage others, have I ever thought that because certain corrupt opinions have been trumpeted <note>original: "ebucinatis"</note> to the world, the truths found in the harvests of others should be considered lies—simply because they are perhaps rare to hear, seem crude at first sight, or appear difficult and beyond the grasp of common men’s thoughts.

Without harm to other writers (I say), or the degradation of their dignity—my pen not armed with a viper's tooth, but governed by a peaceful mind with all bitterness set aside, and with hatred and envy banished to <term>Orcus</term> <note>A name for the underworld or Hell in classical mythology.</note>, accompanied only by honest reason—I desired to sing of that harmonic matter in the world, both great and small <note>Refers to the Macrocosm (the universe) and the Microcosm (the human being).</note>, for the praise of God and the comfort of men. I did this so that, through the harmony I began, I might make the condition of my mind conform to its laws; for the mind is, in and of itself, quiet, and exists as a rich symphony of its supercelestial homeland, full of divine splendor. Hence, my mind being sincerely occupied in the sweet contemplation of the consonances of true harmony, it delighted in filling these volumes not with quarrels, brawls, calumnies, or lies, but with a real subject supported and confirmed by reason, authority, and visual demonstration.

For what, I ask, can be imagined more futile or more foolish among mortals than to explain peace through the means of war, or <term>Sympathy</term> through the noise of <term>Antipathy</term>, or to contaminate the peaceful proportions of God and nature herself with the discords of Strife <note>original: "Litigii"</note> and the impious and unjust dissonances of the Devil?

No one (as Christ witnessed) can rightly serve two masters. What weight, therefore, can be expected in a true explanation of worldly music where the mind (which worldly harmony fundamentally adorned) is now infected by the stain of Strife and defiled by the filth of dissension because of the pollution of the malignant body? For the more human nature is prone to litigation, the further we judge it to be from the meditation of divine harmony—by which the joints of the world and its elements are arranged toward one another in peace and concord—and consequently, we suspect such a soul to be plunged deeper into the sad valley of darkness. Solomon certainly seems to support this opinion, breaking out into these words: *The corruptible body weighs down the soul, and the earthly dwelling oppresses the mind full of many cares, so that we scarcely conceive of the things on earth, and find with labor those things at hand; but who shall investigate the things in heaven? And who shall know the counsel of God unless God gives him wisdom? Thus the paths of those on earth were corrected, and men were taught the things pleasing to God, and so were saved by Wisdom.* <note>A quotation from the Wisdom of Solomon 9:15-18.</note> *From this, therefore, we see that the holy Wisdom of God is the foundation of universal harmony; with her leading the paths in true concord, we perceive those things pleasing to God (the source of harmony), and we are led to know the harmonic mystery of the world.* This is also what the aforementioned wise man seems to confess in this thread of discourse: *Wisdom (he says) has given me certain knowledge, so that I may know the constitution of the world, and the power of the elements; the beginning, end, and middle of times; the changes of the solstices and the varieties of seasons; the cycles of the year and the positions of the stars; the natures of living creatures, and the tempers or rages of wild beasts; the forces of winds or spirits, and the thoughts of men; the differences of plants, and the powers of roots; even to know whatever things are...* <note>A quotation from the Wisdom of Solomon 7:17-20.</note>

<vocab>Concord, Symphony, Harmony, Wisdom, Solomon, Mosaic Decalogue, Strife, Antipathy, Sympathy, the world</vocab>

<summary>In this preface, Fludd argues that his work on universal harmony is intended to reflect divine order and peace, rather than the "viperous" academic strife of his critics. He cites the Wisdom of Solomon to show that understanding the structure of the universe (the macrocosm) and the human mind (the microcosm) is a gift of divine Wisdom.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the prefatory matter of Robert Fludd's work. While the previous page was a direct prayer to Wisdom (Sophia) for protection against critics, this page transitions into a formal Epistle (letter) addressed to "the sons of doctrine"—his fellow scholars and seekers of truth. He establishes his intent: to prove that the universe is governed by a divine, musical harmony rather than the discord of his detractors.</meta>

<header>Epistle</header>

<image-desc>A horizontal ornamental border composed of interlaced oval and chain-link motifs.</image-desc>

### *To the sons of doctrine and those raised on the wings of justice, we pray from the heart for Concordant Symphony and immunity from viperous tongues through Jesus*
->*Christ.*<-

**A**s it is my nature (most distinguished men and most esteemed judges) to lead a life that is first of all pleasing to my God, and thereafter to men—a life tranquil and full of charity, according to that essence of the <term>Mosaic Decalogue</term> <note>The Ten Commandments given to Moses.</note> contracted into two parts by our Savior: namely, honor and fear God with all your heart, and love your neighbor as yourself—so indeed (as I hope) have I conducted myself in these five volumes of mine now published (by the assistance of divine grace). I have behaved both before God and men such that no place can be found in them where I have spoken a reproach against anyone with a biting tongue. Nor, puffed up by vain <term>philautia</term> <note>From the Greek 'philautia,' meaning self-love or conceit.</note> or foolish and impious desire, led by a craving to disparage anyone, have I ever thought that because I trumpeted my opinions to the world, the truths found in the harvest of others should be considered lies. This remains true even if my findings seem rare to the ear, crude at first sight, or arduous and beyond the grasp of common men's thoughts. Without detriment to other writers (I say), or the depravation of their dignity (my pen being armed not with a viper's tooth, but governed by a peaceful mind with all bitterness set aside, and with hatred and envy banished to <term>Orcus</term> <note>A name for the underworld or hell in classical mythology.</note>, accompanied only by honest reason), I was eager to sing of that harmonic matter in the world—both the great <note>The Macrocosm, or the universe.</note> and the small <note>The Microcosm, or the human being.</note>—to the praise of God and the comfort of men. I did this so that, by the harmony begun by me, I might render the state of my mind conformable to its own laws; for the mind, in and of itself, is quiet and exists as a most rich Symphony and full of divine splendor in its super-celestial homeland. Hence, my soul being sincerely occupied in the sweet contemplation of the consonances of true harmony, it has delighted in filling these volumes—not with quarrels, brawls, calumnies, lies, or cavils—but with a real subject, supported and confirmed by reason, authority, and <term>ocular demonstration</term> <note>Evidence provided through visual diagrams and illustrations, for which Fludd’s works are famous.</note>.

For what, I ask, can be devised that is more futile or more foolish among mortals than to explain peace through the means of war, or <term>Sympathy</term> <note>The occult principle that things in the universe are naturally drawn to or influence one another.</note> through the noise of <term>Antipathy</term>, or to contaminate the peaceful proportions of God and nature itself with the impious and unjust dissonances of discordant Litigation and the Devil?

No one (as Christ witnessed) can rightly serve two masters. What weight, therefore, is to be expected in a true explanation of the world's music when the mind (which worldly harmony fundamentally adorned) is now, on account of the pollution of the malignant body, infected by the stain of Litigation and defiled by the filth of dissension? For the more human nature is prone to litigation, the further we judge it to be from the thought of divine harmony (by which the frames of the world and its elements are arranged toward one another in peace and concord), and consequently we suspect its soul to be plunged more deeply into the sad valley of shadows. Solomon certainly seems to support this opinion, breaking out into these words:

> For the corruptible body presses down upon the soul, and the earthly habitation weighs down the mind that is full of many cares, so that we hardly conceive of the things that are upon the earth, and we find with labor the things that are at hand; but who shall search out the things that are in the heavens, and who shall know the counsel of God, unless God gives him wisdom? Thus the paths of those who dwell on the earth were corrected, and men were taught the things that please God, and so they were saved by Wisdom. <note>original: "Corpus infestum corruptione adgrauat animam..." quoting Wisdom of Solomon 9:15-18.</note>

From this, therefore, we see that the holy Wisdom of God is the foundation of universal harmony. With her leading the paths in true concord, we perceive those things pleasing to God (the source of harmony) and are led to know the harmonic mystery of the world. The aforementioned wise man [Solomon] also seems to confess this in a similar thread of discourse: Wisdom (he says) gave me certain knowledge, so that I might know the constitution of the world, and the power of the elements; the beginning, the end, and the middle of times; the changes of the solstices and the varieties of seasons or tempests; the circuits of the year and the positions of the stars: the natures of living creatures, and the tempers or rages of beasts; the forces of winds or spirits, and the thoughts of men; the differences of plants, and the virtues of roots; and to know whatever things 

<meta>The catchword "sunt" (are) indicates the sentence continues on the next page.</meta>

<summary>In this preface, Fludd defends his work against critics, arguing that his study of universal harmony is a peaceful, divine pursuit based on the Wisdom of Solomon. He asserts that the world is built on musical proportions and that his "ocular demonstrations" (diagrams) prove this divine order.</summary>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s vigorous defense against the attacks of Marin Mersenne. Having previously established his peaceful intent, Fludd now uses the authority of King Solomon and the personification of Wisdom (Sophia) to argue that true knowledge of "Universal Music"—the divine harmony of the world—can only reside in a peaceful, pure heart. He contrasts his own pursuit of this wisdom with Mersenne’s "worldly" and contentious spirit.</meta>

### To the Reader.

<margin>Wisdom 1.</margin>
<margin>Proverbs 20:3.</margin>
<margin>Proverbs 17:14.</margin>
<margin>Proverbs 17:19.</margin>
<margin>Proverbs 25:8.</margin>
<margin>Proverbs 3:30.</margin>
<margin>Proverbs 22:10.</margin>
...things are both hidden and manifest: for Wisdom, the artisan of all things, taught me <note>A quotation from the Wisdom of Solomon 7:21.</note> etc. By these words it is clearly enough declared that Solomon himself, with Wisdom alone as his guide, possessed perfect knowledge in universal and truly formal Music. Without her assistance, it is impossible to reach such great understanding in things so hidden. But that spirit is accustomed to descend only into a clean heart, immune to contention; for we read that Wisdom shall not enter into a malevolent soul <note>Wisdom of Solomon 1:4.</note>. Hence, for this reason, Solomon prayed for it, as he held contention and quarreling in the greatest hatred above all other vices, as he seems to assert in many places of his teaching. Indeed, in one he says: *It is an honor for a man to cease from strife.* In another: *He who begins a quarrel is like one who lets out water.* And elsewhere: *He who loves a quarrel loves transgression.* And again: *Do not go out hastily to strive.* 

But someone will perhaps ask for the reason why I now prepare to hasten into the public field or arena of contention, since I find the Wise Man himself to be averse to such business? To this I respond: that this is permitted by the Wise Man himself in such a case, for he falls into this sentiment elsewhere: *Do not strive with any man without cause, if he has done you no harm* etc. <note>Proverbs 3:30.</note>. Therefore, I would never have wished to be involved in a Subject of this kind, unless the reason of defending my good name and reputation—which has remained immaculate until this day (thanks be to God)—had provoked me to it, willing or unwilling. *By casting out the scorner* (says the Wise Man) *contention shall go out; when the strife ceases, the ignominy shall also cease.* <note>Proverbs 22:10.</note>. 

It shall therefore be my task to delete with the edge of truth the brands of the slanderer, falsely impressed upon the minds of men; to cast out the fiction of the scorner like an evil demon using arguments of piety; so that at last, once the envy of that cynical Momus <note>Momus: The Greek personification of mockery and unfair criticism.</note> is detected, his deceptive skin stripped away, and the naked truth revealed and opened to the world, the ignominy falsely imposed upon me by that least deserving Monk <note>Referring to Marin Mersenne, who was a monk of the Order of Minims.</note> may be banished from human imagination into exile beyond the Hyperborean mountains <note>A mythical land in the far north; here used to mean the most remote place on earth.</note>. In this way, once discord is utterly defeated by these instruments, symphonic concord may freely exercise its power and virtue in the world.

<margin>As in Wisdom 7.</margin>
From these things, therefore, it is clearly enough declared that the sacred and simple laws of worldly harmony cannot descend into a breast contaminated by strife and envy, as it is destitute of Wisdom. What more, then, is to be expected or drawn from an empty breast regarding true and formal music than good wine from an empty cask? Or why should we seek *Wisdom* (which is the sincere foundation of true harmony) among those who are ignorant and blind to the "husk" <note>original: "cortice" (bark/outer layer), contrasting with the "nucleus" or inner truth.</note>, when her splendid tabernacle is only to be found in the core or the very center of a thing? For this reason, she is sought by many but found by few, because nearly all things are full of fools—that is, the greater part of men hunt and venerate the Wisdom of this world with such devotion (which, by the witness of the *Apostle* in one place is foolish, and in another is foolishness before God) that they neglect and remain ignorant of that true Wisdom, which is the power and splendor of the *Almighty*—that mystical Wisdom (I say) of the prophets and *Apostles*—and they pursue those who seek her with derision, reproach, and contempt.

<margin>Colossians 1.</margin>
Friar Marin Mersenne seems to be a man of this sort of flour <note>A phrase meaning "a man of this ilk" or "this kind of person."</note>, an egregious adversary not only to me but to truth itself. By deforming his own "Harmony of the World" with the stains of antipathy and discord, he has (so to speak) so choked and obscured his crops and his good grain with tares and weeds <note>original: "lolio & zizania"</note> that he can bring nothing of use either to himself or indeed to the world. For what benefit of harmony, I ask, is to be expected from him who does not cease to pursue his brothers with hatred and malice? He considers not at all that the unique foundation or cornerstone of the harmony of both worlds—namely *Our Lord Jesus Christ*—pacified through the blood of his cross both those things which are in heaven and those on earth. 

But in truth, Marin Mersenne (whatever he seems to preach about the harmony of the world) has not yet been appeased, but continues to accuse many of his brothers of atheism, blasphemy, heresy, magic, ignorance, and stupidity; he condemns them with rash judgment, and delights in chastising them with injurious words and insults, which are least becoming or decent for such a man. Yet he does not even think of the beam confirmed in his own eye. As for what pertains to me, for a reason of no moment he has cast suspicion of atheism upon me; in one place he says I am defiled by <term>cacomagia</term> <note>Evil or "black" magic, as opposed to "natural magic."</note>; in another he feigns that I am a patron of the same; elsewhere he calls me a heretic and sometimes a heretic-magician; and sometimes he writes of *Robert's* stupidity and...

<meta>The catchword "infamiam" (infamy/disgrace) indicates the sentence continues on the next page with further accusations from Mersenne.</meta>

<summary>Fludd justifies his entry into public controversy as a necessary defense of his reputation against Marin Mersenne, arguing that Mersenne's contentious spirit disqualifies him from understanding the "Universal Harmony" he claims to write about. He contrasts Mersenne's aggressive "worldly wisdom" with the peaceful, divine Wisdom of Solomon.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page, the author, Robert Fludd, transitions from a general declaration of his peaceful intent to a specific defense of his character. He frames his upcoming counter-argument not as a desire for conflict, but as a necessary defense of his reputation against the attacks of Marin Mersenne, using the biblical figure of Solomon as his model for divine wisdom and harmony.</meta>

->To the Reader.<-

...are things hidden and things manifest: for **Wisdom**, the artisan of all things, taught me, etc. By these words it is clearly enough declared that **Solomon** himself, with Wisdom alone as his guide, possessed perfect knowledge in universal and truly <term>formal music</term> <note>In this context, "formal" refers to the "Form" or the underlying spiritual blueprint of the universe, rather than just the technical rules of sound.</note>; without whose assistance, it is impossible to reach such great cognition in matters so occult. But that spirit is not accustomed to descend except into a clean heart, immune from contention; for we read that **Wisdom** will not enter into a malevolent soul. <margin>Wisdom 1:4</margin> Hence, because he held contention and brawling in the greatest hatred above all vices, **Solomon** prays—as he seems to assert in many places of his doctrine. <margin>Proverbs 20:3</margin> Indeed, in one of these he says: *It is an honor for a man to cease from strife.* <margin>Proverbs 17:14</margin> In another: *He who begins a contention is like one who lets out water;* <margin>Proverbs 17:19</margin> and elsewhere: *He who loves brawling loves transgression;* <margin>Proverbs 25:8</margin> and elsewhere: *Do not go out hastily to strive.* 

But someone perhaps will ask the reason why I now prepare myself to hasten into the field or public arena of contention, when I find the **Wise Man** himself averse to this business? To him I respond: this is granted by the **Wise Man** himself in such a case, since he elsewhere falls upon this sentiment: *Do not strive with any man without cause, unless he has done you harm,* etc. <margin>Proverbs 3:30</margin> Therefore, I would never have wished to be engaged in a subject of this kind, had not the reason of defending my good name and reputation (thanks be to God), which has been immaculate until this day, provoked me to it, willing or unwilling. <margin>Proverbs 22:10</margin> *By casting out the scorner,* (says the **Wise Man**) *contention shall cease; when the brawling ceases, the ignominy shall also cease.* It shall be my task, therefore, to erase the marks of the calumniator—falsely impressed upon the minds of men—with the edge of truth; to cast out the figment of the scorner like a malignant demon with arguments of piety. Thus, at last, when the envy of that cynical <term>Momus</term> <note>Momus was the Greek god of mockery and unwarranted criticism.</note> is detected, his deceptive skin pulled away, and the naked truth revealed and opened to the world, the ignominy—falsely imposed upon me by that **Monk** <note>Referring to Marin Mersenne, a French Minim friar and philosopher.</note> without my deserving it—may be banished from human imagination into exile beyond the <term>Hyperborean mountains</term> <note>In Greek mythology, the far north; used here to mean "the ends of the earth."</note>. In this way, once the discord of the instruments is utterly defeated, symphonic concord may freely exercise its power and virtue in the world.

From these things, therefore, it is clearly enough declared that the sacred and simple laws of worldly harmony cannot descend into a breast contaminated by strife and envy, seeing as it is destitute of **Wisdom**. What, therefore, is to be expected or drawn from an empty breast regarding true and formal music, any more than good wine from an empty cask? Or why should we seek **Wisdom** (which is the true foundation of sincere harmony) among the ignorant and those blind to the "bark" <note>The outer shell or literal meaning.</note>, when her splendid tabernacle is to be found only in the "kernel" or center of the thing? For this reason, she is sought by many but found by few, because nearly all things are full of fools—that is, the greater part of men hunt and venerate the wisdom of this world with such devotion (which, by the testimony of the Apostle in one place is foolish, and in another is foolishness before God). <margin>as in Wisdom 7</margin> Thus they neglect and ignore that true **Wisdom**, which is the virtue and splendor of the **Omnipotent**, that mystical wisdom (I say) of the prophets and Apostles, and they pursue its seekers with derision, reproach, and contempt.

Of this sort of "flour" <note>A metaphor meaning "cut from this cloth" or "of this character."</note> seems to be the man **Friar Marin Mersenne**, an eminent adversary not only to me, but to truth itself. By deforming his own harmony of the world with the stains of antipathy and discord, he has (so to speak) so choked and obscured his crops and good grain with cockle and tares <note>Weeds mentioned in biblical parables.</note> that he can bring nothing of utility either to himself or indeed to the world. For what benefit of harmony, I ask, is to be expected from him who does not cease to pursue his brothers with hatred and malice? He considers not at all that the unique foundation or cornerstone of the harmony of both worlds—namely, **Our Lord Jesus Christ**—has, by the blood of his cross, reconciled both the things in heaven and the things on earth. <margin>Colossians 1</margin> But truly, **Marin Mersenne** (whatever he seems to preach about the world's harmony) is not yet reconciled; he proceeds to accuse not a few of his brothers of atheism, blasphemy, heresy, magic, ignorance, and folly. He condemns them with rash judgment and desires to chastise them with injurious words and insults, which are least fitting or decent for such a man. Yet, nevertheless, he does not even think of the beam fixed in his own eye. As for what pertains to me, for a cause of no moment he has cast a suspicion of atheism upon me; in one place he says I am defiled by <term>cacomagia</term> <note>original: "cacomagia"; evil or black magic.</note>; in another he feigns that I am a patron of the same; elsewhere he calls me a heretic and sometimes a heretic-mage; and occasionally he speaks of **Robert's** folly and

insanity <note>Catchword for the next page: "infaniam".</note>

He characterizes Mersenne's accusations of atheism and "black magic" as the result of envy and a lack of spiritual insight.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense against Marin Mersenne. Fludd has just argued that true wisdom requires a peaceful heart; here, he contrasts his own intentions with Mersenne’s aggressive, mocking behavior, citing the Epistle of James to distinguish between heavenly and "diabolical" wisdom.</meta>

# Letter

...to the world, he seems to proclaim his madness triumphantly in the manner of the braggart Thraso <note>original: "Thrasonis gloriosi." Thraso is a stock character of a "braggart soldier" from Roman comedy, specifically Terence's play *The Eunuch*.</note>; nay rather, he pursues me with many other insults and hisses. Indeed, just as this behavior is contrary to the custom of one enriched by the essential Spirit of music and true Wisdom, so it proves that this disposition of Mersenne <note>original: "Marini." Referring to Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), the French polymath and Fludd's chief critic.</note> is derived from evil—namely, from the spring of worldly wisdom and diabolical strife. The Apostle seems to support this thread of our opinion with these words: 

<margin>James 3:17.</margin>
Wisdom (he says) that contradicts the truth does not descend from above from the Father of Lights, but is earthly, sensual, and diabolical, etc. He seems to explain the reason for this in this series of words: The wisdom (he says) which is from above is first pure, then peaceful, modest, easy to be entreated, not judging, without rivalry, etc. From these things you are able to discern more clearly than the midday light (most distinguished sirs) whether the spirit of that Monk <note>Mersenne was a member of the Order of Minims, hence "that Monk."</note> is a partaker of earthly wisdom, or at least of that which is heavenly and divine; for it was never, nor shall it ever be, my intention to speak reproaches against anyone, or to judge and condemn him (however guilty he may be). 

But now, so that you are not seized by any wonder hereafter that I, in descending into this cause of mine and also of others, have used the authority of the Holy Scriptures alone—and not that of others, whether Theosophers, or Cabalists, or Philosophers—I would have you know first of all that I did so partly compelled by necessity, and partly by the admonition and instruction of the Holy Scriptures themselves. 

Compelled by necessity (I say), because I realized that this adversary of ours, who regards the doctrine of almost all who philosophize and "theosophize" in a mystical and Cabalistic manner (both Christian and Heathen) as worth nothing, would certainly repudiate their authorities or testimonies if I alleged them. Nay, indeed, he is observed to deal so subtly with me and others (whom he has falsely afflicted with slanders), that he even attempts to forbid the text of the most holy page—offered by divine command to everyone in the whole world without exception—not only to me, but also to others under the pretext of heresy. These are his words: 

"Let the heretics only take heed that no passage of Scripture can be understood as it ought to be, unless the authority of the Church be added, which is the judge of all controversies that can be moved concerning religion. Unless you recur to her, what will you answer me when I say that a passage of Scripture must be understood differently than you and others commonly understand it?" etc. 

To this man, led by the truth, I respond: the true Church is not the assembly of Roman ecclesiastics, but the very rock or stone which the Patriarch called the House of God <note>A reference to Jacob’s ladder and the stone he anointed at Bethel in Genesis 28.</note> and consequently the Church, inasmuch as she is the truest spiritual rock and the catholic <note>In the sense of "universal."</note> mansion of Christ, who is himself God. Therefore, without asking the permission of Marin Mersenne, the use of the literal Scriptures is granted by God to me as much as to him, or to any other Christian—nay, even to the Gentile—so that the spiritual mystery or the secret of the spiritual stone may be gathered from them. For indeed, since this is the mystical wisdom of the Prophets and Apostles, or that catholic and universal rock, she has denied entrance and access to no one at this, her fountain, which overflows with all abundance. 

This, therefore, is the reason why we follow the authority of the Holy Page through the whole thread of this our treatise, and why I have been forced to apply it to this our purpose, and not those testimonies of others, whether Philosophers, or Cabalists, or Theosophers; for these, or at least the greater part of them, have been repudiated and condemned by that Chattering Babbler of Trifles <note>original: "Nugipolyloquide." A mocking compound word Fludd invented to describe Mersenne, combining "trifles" (nugae), "many" (poly), and "talker" (loquus).</note>.

Finally, why I did not come forward sooner, and before this time, into the field against this public enemy of truth and true Wisdom—since he himself published that volume of his in the year 1623—I will show briefly and in a word. 

On the 8th day of July, in the year 1626, I received these admonitory letters from a certain friend of mine, to whom I certainly confess I owe great thanks for his remarkable benevolence toward me and his diligence in defending and preserving my good reputation. You have here a copy of these letters, transcribed faithfully and word-for-word, just as they were truly written to me:

"Your Excellency (most learned Sir) will perhaps wonder why I have not written sooner, since permission to write was granted by your Excellency, or why I do so now at last. I should certainly have wished to write sooner, but until now, not experiencing that bright ray illuminating my darkness regarding the contemplation of Macrocosmic principles <note>The "Macrocosm" refers to the universe as a whole, which Fludd believed mirrored the "Microcosm" of the human being.</note>—I say, not experiencing it sufficiently abundantly in my mind—I preferred to remain silent rather than [write] against my will..."

<meta>The catchword at the bottom of the page is "quod" (that/because).</meta>

<summary>Fludd defends his use of the Bible as his primary authority, arguing that Marin Mersenne’s aggressive behavior proves he lacks divine wisdom. He rejects Mersenne's claim that only the Roman Church can interpret Scripture and begins sharing a letter from a friend that spurred him to finally publish this defense.</summary>
<keywords>Marin Mersenne, Robert Fludd, biblical authority, true Church, spiritual rock, Thraso, chattering babbler of trifles, Macrocosm, polemic</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page follows the introduction of Fludd’s grievances against Marin Mersenne. It transitions from Fludd’s general philosophical stance to a specific piece of correspondence he received from an anonymous supporter in Oxford, which first alerted him to Mersenne’s attacks.</meta>

# To the Reader.

<meta>Signature: b. Catchword: Momo (a reference to Momus, the Greek god of mockery and criticism, likely the first word of the following page).</meta>

...to do anything, as they say, "against the will of Minerva" <note>original: "invita Minerva." A classical proverb meaning to attempt something without natural talent or against one's better judgment; Minerva was the goddess of wisdom.</note>. Now indeed, although that light does not shine upon me, nevertheless, having found the occasion of writing to Your Excellence, I shall explain the cause: While perusing theological books, as is the custom at Oxford, I happened upon a certain author who seems to me to rail too sharply against the person and writings of Your Excellence. Therefore, out of my affection for Your Excellence and your writings, I thought it worthwhile—in case this perhaps remains hidden from Your Excellence—to note down that author’s name, the book, and the places in which he makes mention of Your Excellence. For I am not unmindful of that Apology <note>Fludd refers to his 1622 work, *Monochordum Mundi*, which defended his theories against the criticisms of the astronomer Johannes Kepler.</note> which Your Excellence published against Kepler by reason of his calumny; so that now, if Your Excellence deems it advisable and if the business (which I indeed think it altogether does) requires it, you may defend your person and writings against this man's provocations. The author’s name and the title of the book are as follows: **MARIN MERSENNE’S Most Celebrated Questions on Genesis**, folio, Paris, 1623. The principal places, as far as I could observe by skimming the whole book according to the marginal headings and here and there, in which MERSENNE (not MARSENNUS, as I had written above) mentions Your Excellence, are the following: The first place is column (for he divides the page into two columns and assigns numbers to each) 714; the second 716; the third 1208; the fourth 1561; the fifth 1838; the sixth 1696; the seventh 1743; the eighth 1750. Among these places, the most important of all is the one I have underlined. Nor indeed should Your Excellence neglect this under the pretext that it is not fitting for a grave man to lend an ear to any slanders whatsoever—especially those of common men and the literary "rabble" of wiseacres—for this man is of much reading and in many things, as may be seen, not superficially learned. Whence it is to be feared, if his provocations (for he provokes Your Excellence) are not answered as soon as possible, it is to be feared (I say) lest he sing a song of triumph and the dignity of Your Excellence’s person and fame be traduced among foreigners.

These things I had to indicate to Your Excellence for the present, Reverend Sir; I ask one thing: that you take this in good part, and do not disdain to respond to me through him by whose effort these letters of mine are delivered to Your Excellence.

->Given at Oxford, July 11, 1626.<-
->Yours in the Lord, A. B.<-

Therefore, before the time attached to the end of those letters, I had never heard of that man’s name, and consequently of the various calumnies employed by him not only against me, but even against others.

> *Meanwhile, however, during this time I pray you (benevolent readers and most desired judges), that all favor and partiality being set aside, you look into this little book of mine with sincere eyes directed by the virtue of justice, so that from there you may be able to judge correctly whether my ignorance or rather the malice of that Monk <note>Mersenne was a member of the Order of Minims, a Catholic religious order.</note> is the cause of such great virulence and just madness in me, with which he pursues me through almost the entire thread of his immense volume with such ardor. Farewell and judge rightly; and I implore you to forgive me for any errors, if any should meet your eyes in this our inquiry.*

->Your most observant,<-

->Robert Fludd.<-

<summary>A letter from an anonymous correspondent in Oxford informs Robert Fludd of Marin Mersenne's extensive attacks in his 1623 commentary on Genesis, prompting Fludd to publish this defense. Fludd appeals to the reader's sense of justice to determine if he or his "Monk" critic is in the wrong.</summary>

# An Admonitory Letter to the Calumniator.

->*To Momus <note>The Greek god of mockery and personification of criticism.</note> and the Slanderer, we wish not a curse, but health and an increase in morality.*<-

Look here, Mersenne: see and behold Robert Fludd—whom you have so sharply provoked and challenged into the public arena of competition with slanders and insults—now coming forth. You deigned, first of all, to pore over his volumes strictly and by an analytical path, even with (as they say) the eyes of a Lynx <note>original: "oculis... Lynceis." A classical reference to Lynceus the Argonaut, who was said to have sight so keen he could see through stone or into the earth.</note>. By this diligence of yours, you have done him a very welcome service; for you have accurately inspected their method, and like an index, you have corrected and refuted errors: you have detected the marks of atheism, heresy, and magic within them with unheard-of caution. You have done well, if it was well enough, and as becomes a just and religious man—if it was done justly and religiously enough. 

If, therefore, by returning like for like <note>original: "par pari referendo."</note> and compensating you with a similar measure of favor and diligence, Robert should lightly run through that volume of yours (all incivility set aside); if he should seek out certain errors in it and, with your leave, correct those he finds; if he should perhaps detect the stigmas of atheism, or at least of a true and not imaginary heresy; and if he should then afterwards revisit your observations on his own works—would anyone suppose that you, Marin, a man so Catholic and religious, indeed an imitator of Christ, and consequently by office humble, mild, and gentle, would rage beyond measure with unjust indignation? We call that man ungrateful who does not return a favor to one who deserves it; therefore, if it goes well enough, it will go well with you; but if ill, malice too will seek its own reward. Meanwhile, be wise and be well.

->*I am he whom you so eagerly seek.*<-

*Rob. Fludd.*

<meta>catchword: OF WISDOM [original: SOPHIAE]</meta>

Fludd frames his forthcoming rebuttal as a "favor" returned in kind, challenging Mersenne to maintain his Christian humility when the roles are reversed.</summary>

# THE STRUGGLE OF
# WISDOM WITH
->FOLLY<-

## BOOK ONE.

> The wrath of God is revealed from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who hold the truth of God in unrighteousness. Romans 1:18.

---

### CHAPTER I.

->*In which the malice of Marin Mersenne toward Robert Fludd is explained; specifically, how he attributed those things pertaining to Robert’s physical subject to an uncertain multitude, while omitting Robert's name.*<-

After Marin Mersenne had made mention of the opinions of Paracelsus and Campanella <note>Tommaso Campanella (1568–1639), an Italian philosopher known for his work "The City of the Sun."</note> by name in Columns 707 and 708 regarding the structure of the world, he then fell upon the Macrocosmic subject of Robert Fludd in Columns 708, 709, and 710. But he is so far from attributing to Robert the honor of his own opinion that, in his entire recapitulation of the history of Robert's worldly structure, he did not even deign to mention him—perhaps thinking that by doing so he would bring too much honor to him. Instead, in the margin after the mention of Campanella, he puts *The opinion of others* <note>original: "Aliorum sententia."</note>, when in truth whatever he wrote at the end of Column 708 and throughout the whole of 709 and 710, he took almost word for word from my *Physics of the History of the Macrocosm*. We thought it worthwhile to transcribe all of this from Mersenne's aforementioned columns in this place, so that benevolent readers may more rightly understand the malevolence—not to say envy—of that man toward me. Inasmuch as it is his way, on the one hand, for the sake of disparaging me as much as he can, to publish things which are truly mine under an uncertain plurality <note>i.e., referring to Fludd as "others" rather than by name.</note> while suppressing my name; and on the other hand, instead of a refutation, to afflict me by name with injuries and slanders for no evident reason; as will be shown more clearly in the following passages.

->1. Marin's Text, from Column 708.<-

<margin>Col. 708.</margin>
*Others confess that matter was created in the likeness of darkness, which the Chemists <note>Alchemists</note> sometimes signify by the name of the black crow <note>original: "corui nigri," a reference to the 'nigredo' or initial stage of the alchemical process involving decomposition and darkness.</note>. But they believe that whatever we have seen afterward was enclosed within it, which the Sun brought forth by its most powerful heat; to explain this by force, they use certain examples and believe that if the heat of the sun ceased, all things would return to their original state. Moreover, some of them think that the light which God made constituted the empyrean heaven, and then communicated itself to all things, especially the sun; others think the light was placed in that matter, which afterward produced our fire, by the power of which the elements were distinguished. Truly, they call the Fiery Spirit the origin of forms and the most lively effect of light. Thus he [Mersenne] says.*

<sig>b 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: Robert's</meta>

Fludd provides the specific text from Mersenne's work to prove that his ideas were labeled vaguely as the "opinions of others."</summary>

# WISDOM WITH FOLLY / ROBERT’S EXAMINATION.

From these things, one may see how secretly and cunningly that Monk <note>Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), the French friar Fludd is accusing of plagiarism.</note> acts toward me—namely, by taking those matters which pertain to me, and which he extracted totally and word-for-word from my own volume, and striving to assign them to various uncertain and unnamed persons. But I shall point out the particular places in my *Physics* from which he stole all these things. In Chapter 4, Book 1 of my *Physics of the History of the Macrocosm*, we described the <term>prime matter<gloss>Latin: *materia prima*. The fundamental, formless substance from which all physical things were created.</gloss></term> or the subject of darkness (in whose lap that greatest Creator of the Macrocosm arranged the structure of the world). In Chapter 3 of the same, we discussed whether that matter was created or uncreated. In Chapter 5, it is noted that the <term>Chemists<gloss>referring to Alchemists</gloss></term> call this mass the <term>crow’s head<gloss>Latin: *caput corvi*. An alchemical term for the 'nigredo' or initial state of chaotic, dark matter.</gloss></term>. In Chapter 6, we indicated the manner in which the light-virtue, sent forth by the divine breath, arranged that potential matter into the actual world—and not the Sun, as he falsely conjectures. And where he says that it is "their opinion" that if the sun's heat ceased, all things would return to their original state, in this he is also mistaken and misleads his readers; for at the end of that chapter, you will read this heading: *Demonstrations by which it results that by the cessation of the action of the formal light (that is, by the absence of divine light and not the sun), the entire matter of the world would return to the first abyss, the dark and formless void, etc.*

Similarly, we treated the creation and nature of light, and its operation in the production of the <term>Empyrean heaven<gloss>The highest part of the heavens, believed in ancient cosmology to be composed of pure fire or light.</gloss></term>, in Chapters 4 and 5 of the same second book. Then, in Chapters 11, 12, and 13, we discussed the nature or virtue of the Elements—especially of Fire—and the production of the Light-Spirit. Finally, in Chapter 15 of the same book, we established that fiery Spirit as the origin of the form of the Elements. By these references, it is declared clearly enough that although he secretly and falsely attributed the logic of constructing such a worldly fabric to others (which belongs to me alone), he did not deign to show me the kindness of ascribing those things to me by name—things which his own conscience discerns in my writings to be good and approved. Thus, he has clearly displayed his dog-like envy toward me to the world. He perhaps suspects that his own labors would not receive much favor throughout all Europe unless he stained mine with calumnies and infamy before the world, seeing as both our works deal with one and the same subject. Those even moderately instructed in Philosophy will very easily see the truth of this from the evidence. For without a doubt, if he had truly found so many and such great errors in my work, he would—in the manner of a Philosopher—refute them with an open pen, all envy set aside, and deal with me on a fundamental level, rather than with brawls, quarrels, and similar old wives' tales that have nothing to do with the matter. Thus, he proceeds in his recapitulation of my physical subject:

### 2. Marin’s text from Column 109.

<margin>Col. 109.</margin>
> *That fire penetrates dense things even to their center, and it opens, dilates, dissolves, thins, and makes them volatile so that they may be raised upward; it gathers homogeneous things, and digests, matures, fixes, and perfects them. It cuts into, scatters, drives away, and beats back heterogeneous things. They say this fire was produced by the Holy Spirit; whence they contend that the <term>Chaldean Oracles<gloss>A collection of mystical verses from the 2nd century AD, highly influential on Neoplatonists and Renaissance occultists.</gloss></term> called the Holy Spirit a fiery love, the God of fire and the deity of the Spirit, an intellectual and fiery Spirit, having no form, but transforming itself into whatever form it wishes and making itself equal to all things. And Zoroaster and Heraclitus said that all things were birthed from a single fire. Thus he [Mersenne] says.*

<meta>catchword: Robert's</meta>

# Book I of the Struggle.
b 3
<meta>catchword: Robert’s</meta>

->Robert’s Examination.<-

In this, we differ: for he uses the name of "fire," whereas I use that of "heat," which I have called the effect of the <term>fiery spirit<gloss>Latin: *volucris igneæ*. Fludd often uses the image of a "fiery bird" or winged spirit to represent the divine light of the Creator.</gloss></term>. He has taken the entire operation of this heat, word-for-word, from Chapter 7, Book 1 of my *History of the Macrocosm*. From there, he also transcribed the <term>Chaldean Oracles<gloss>A collection of philosophical and mystical poems from the 2nd century AD, which Fludd used to support his theories of divine light.</gloss></term>, etc., to the end of his text, in the same sequence of words from Chapter 2 of my second book.

<margin>Column 109.</margin>
### 3. Marin’s text from the same Column.

*Democritus indeed, and Orpheus, and the Pythagoreans thought that all things were full of gods; however, they referred these back to the one and only Jove <note>original: "Iouem". In this context, referring to the supreme Godhead.</note>, because they perceived that the power of his divine nature was present in all lower creatures according to a greater or lesser degree.*

->Robert’s Examination.<-

He took all of this from Chapter 2, Book 2 of my same *History*. Then he proceeds thus:

### 4. Marin’s text from the same.

*They call the first three days of the world's condition "Spiritual" because (they say) the Holy Spirit itself, in the manner of the sun, circled the entire mass for three days, so that on the first day it illuminated the highest part, which is called the <term>Empyrean heaven<gloss>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe, made of pure light and fire.</gloss></term>; on the second day, the celestial part; and on the third, this elemental part. By this, the whole world was divided into three different regions, just as the beauty of the world was perfected in another set of three days by the power of the sun. But the first set of three <note>original: "ternarius". A grouping of three, referring to the Trinity.</note>, representing the Blessed Trinity, applied its hand to the <term>Megacosm<gloss>The "great world" or the universe.</gloss></term>, while the second set applied its hand to the <term>Microcosm<gloss>The "little world," usually referring to Man.</gloss></term>.*

->Robert’s Examination.<-

Regarding the first three days of creation according to the tenor of Marin’s words, my same *History* treats this throughout Chapter 3, Book 2, including the "three Spiritual days." Examples of how the Empyrean heaven was made by the operation of divine light are found in Chapters 5 and 6; how the Ethereal heaven was made; and finally, how that heaven of the elements was formed, are contained in Chapters 11, 12, and 13.

<margin>Column 109.</margin>
### 5. Marin’s text in the same place.

*They explain the production of the elements and the sublunary world by a chemical example. For they extract <term>spirit<gloss>Refers to alcohol or the volatile essence.</gloss></term> from wine, then oil, which floats upon the spirit; thirdly, <term>phlegm<gloss>The watery, tasteless part of a distillate.</gloss></term>, after which the dregs remain, which they call the <term>dead head<gloss>Latin: *caput mortuum*. The useless solid residue left after distillation or sublimation.</gloss></term>. When they have enclosed these in a round glass vessel, <term>hermetically sealed<gloss>A seal made by melting the glass of the container's neck, named after Hermes Trismegistus.</gloss></term>, and with the vessel resting through an entire night, five regions appear. The lowest of these is black and veiled in darkness, upon which a portion of phlegm resides. This is covered by the oil extracted from the dregs. Next follows the spirit of wine, from which the extracted oil occupies the fifth rank. By this reasoning, they claim that the density of the spirit of darkness was driven out from the Empyrean heaven, from which the element of fire was constituted; and when these were cast out from the middle region—that is, the <term>ether<gloss>The pure, celestial air above the clouds.</gloss></term>—they were converted into the substance of air. Finally, being driven from the lowest region, they produced the element of water. The <term>dung-heap<gloss>original: "sterquilinium". Refers to the waste or bottom-most matter.</gloss></term> and "dead head" of all these must be established as the earth. Water, like the lowest part of the air, follows the earth's impurity; then air, fire, and the <term>fifth essence<gloss>The "quintessence" or most refined form of matter.</gloss></term>, which corresponds to the most subtle part of the wine: "burning water" <note>original: "aqua ardens". An early name for alcohol.</note>, which contains the parts of the fifth essence and a single part of phlegm as fire; that part which is called common "water of life" <note>original: "aqua vitæ".</note>, having two parts of wine and as many of water as air; and finally, the phlegmatic part, containing almost no spirit, as water. The "dead head" residing at the bottom of the <term>cucurbit<gloss>The bottom part of a distilling apparatus, shaped like a gourd.</gloss></term> represents the earth. From this, it is established that the earth is the dregs of every heaven and a heap of darkness; that water is the spirit of the darkness of the lowest heaven, almost void of light; and that these, according to a greater or lesser participation in light, are converted into various metals.*

<summary>Robert Fludd continues his accusation of plagiarism against Marin Mersenne, citing specific chapters from his own work on the Macrocosm. He explains the creation of the universe and the elements through an alchemical analogy involving the distillation of wine.</summary>

# Wisdom with Folly / Robert’s Examination.

He extracted all of this from Chapter 15 of that same book of ours, at the end of which you can find that experiment involving wine which he mentions. But now he approaches the description of my "Mundane Harmony" <note>original: *Harmoniæ meæ mundanæ*. Fludd’s theory that the universe is tuned like a musical instrument.</note> under the guise of a "plurality" <note>i.e., referring to "them" or "they" rather than Fludd specifically</note> and not by Fludd’s name.

### 6. Marin’s text in the same place, and Column 110.

<margin>Columns 109 and 110.</margin>
Indeed, they compare the whole world to a harmonic <term>monochord<gloss>A musical instrument with a single string and a movable bridge, used since antiquity to demonstrate the mathematical ratios of musical intervals.</gloss></term>, in which the spirit or soul of the world—or rather God himself, positioned outside the world—produces all the consonances. He creates sharper <note>higher pitched</note> voices in those creatures that are nearer to him, just as a person playing a flute produces sharper notes through the holes closer to his mouth, and deeper notes through those further away. Thus, the deepest and most ignoble voice, approaching more toward silence, darkness, and the horror of matter, can represent the earth because of the excessive density of the air and the presence of the spirit. For they claim that Light acts upon the matter of the world in the same way the human spirit acts upon the air; they say the string is the matter of the world, but the instrument itself is the monochord of the <term>Macrocosm<gloss>The "Great World" or the universe as a whole, which Fludd believed mirrored the "Microcosm" of the human body.</gloss></term>, in which the scale of musical intervals is arranged. The first of these is <term>Gamma<gloss>Represented by the Greek letter Γ, this was the lowest note in the medieval musical scale.</gloss></term>, then A, and so on, as is held in the musical scale. They extend the monochord from the summit of the <term>Empyrean heaven<gloss>The highest, most spiritual realm of the universe, composed of pure light and fire.</gloss></term> down to the base of the earth; if the middle part of this string is pressed, an octave <note>original: *diapason*</note> is produced.

### Robert’s Examination.

**H**e says "they" extend the monochord from the summit of the Empyrean heaven to the base. And who are "they," my dear Marin? Does a single Robert have a "plurality" in his belly? For I am certain that you have never received this from anyone except Robert alone. Why then do you pretend and offer false Latin in this place, saying "they extend" instead of "he extends"? For I will say this boldly: you received this division of the monochord from Robert alone and no one else. Why then, oh hypocrite, do you pretend and—against your own conscience—conduct yourself against Robert in this whole article? But I shall prepare myself for his next point, which is as follows:

> *In the earth they have placed coldness, thickness, and weight, which relate to the cold and matter of the lowest region extending up to the Moon in a ratio of 4 to 3. This is the <term>sesquitertia<gloss>The mathematical ratio 4:3, which corresponds to the musical interval of a perfect fourth.</gloss></term> proportion, because in this region only a single fourth part of light and heat is present; thus, from water, air, and fire, a <term>fourth<gloss>original: *Diatessaron*</gloss></term> is made, in which the earth is like the Gamma in music, or like the number one in arithmetic, or a point in geometry. For it is the base of the material pyramid, which they claim reaches all the way to the Empyrean.*

### Robert’s Examination.

<margin>2.</margin>
**B**ut tell me, Marin, who are "they" who want the base of the material pyramid to reach all the way to the Empyrean? Have you ever heard of this kind of "formal and material pyramid" from anyone besides Robert alone? Why then did you not write "he wants," but "they want"? Does this not prove your outstanding envy toward Robert? Yet elsewhere, in the full force of your oratory, you attribute these pyramids to Fludd, asserting—though quite thoughtlessly—<margin>Col. 1556.</margin> <margin>Col. 1744.</margin> <margin>Col. 1750.</margin> *that Fludd’s formal and material pyramids are devoid of all reason (Col. 1556).* And elsewhere, you say you "challenge Fludd with all his waves <note>A pun on the name "Fludd" and the Latin word *fluctus* (waves).</note> and his formal and material pyramids." And in another place, you do not blush to assert that "Fludd has more than enough betrayed his own ignorance with his upright and inverted pyramids." Yet now, you assign these to a "plurality." The reason for this is obvious: namely, because you have nothing with which you can either refute Fludd’s pyramidal demonstrations or truly contradict them in any matter. But to the point; he continues thus:

> *Just as they imagine a formal pyramid of light from the Empyrean to the center of the earth, which is like an emanation and representation of divine light: from this it happens that water occupies the place of one whole tone, as does air, but fire (as the summit of the air) obtains only the place of a semitone. The intervals follow up to the Sun <note>original: ☉</note>, namely the intervals of the Moon <note>original: ☾</note>, Venus <note>original: ♀</note>, and Mercury <note>original: ☿</note>, and the middle part of the solar heaven, which enjoys a semitone; this, with the tones of the Moon, Venus, and Mercury, creates a <term>fifth<gloss>original: *Diapente*</gloss></term>, from which both the aforementioned...*

<meta>catchword: aforementioned</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Robert Fludd defends his "World Monochord" and "Pyramid" theories, accusing Marin Mersenne of plagiarizing his work while using the plural "they" to avoid crediting Fludd. Fludd highlights the mathematical and musical ratios (like the fourth and fifth) that he uses to map the structure of the universe from the Earth to the Heavens.</summary>

<keywords>World Monochord, Macrocosm, Empyrean, musical intervals, Diapason, Diatessaron, Diapente, Pyramid of Light, material pyramid, Marin Mersenne, plagiarism, celestial harmony</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s detailed rebuttal against Marin Mersenne. Fludd is explaining the harmonic structure of the universe, specifically how musical intervals correspond to the celestial spheres, and criticizing Mersenne for crediting Fludd only with the illustrations rather than the philosophical concepts themselves.</meta>

# Contest, Book 1.

<meta>catchword: CHAPTER</meta>

From the aforementioned <term>Fourth<gloss>original: Diatessaron; a musical interval spanning four notes of the scale</gloss></term> is born the <term>Octave<gloss>original: Diapason</gloss></term>, which ends in the middle of the sun’s sphere. This is called the "sphere of equality" because it is where the two pyramids <note>Fludd’s famous diagrams of the "Pyramid of Light" descending from God and the "Pyramid of Matter" ascending from the Earth</note> intersect one another, and that equal bond between matter and form is created.

They conceive of a spiritual Octave rising upward from the very center of the sun, which is far more noble than the lower one. Its foundation is a perfect <term>Fifth<gloss>original: Diapente; an interval spanning five notes</gloss></term>, just as the elemental, lower Octave was based on an imperfect Fourth. They place the remaining part of the sun at a <term>semitone<gloss>a half-step in music</gloss></term>, so that the lower part of the sun completes the minor semitone needed to finish the lower Octave's Fifth, while the upper part provides the minor semitone where the upper Octave's Fifth begins. Since these two semitones do not quite make a full <term>tone<gloss>a whole step</gloss></term>, they attribute the two remaining <term>commas<gloss>tiny musical intervals, the minute difference between two nearly identical notes in different tuning systems</gloss></term> to the sphere of equality and the sun's equilibrium. Meanwhile, the spheres of Jupiter, Mars, and Saturn are filled out to complete a new Fifth. Upon this, they place a spiritual Fourth composed from the lowest part of the empyrean heaven <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the sky</note>, which produces a whole tone, as does the middle part, while the highest part corresponds to a semitone. The hand or power of the divine deity loosens or tightens the strings of that <term>monochord<gloss>a single-stringed instrument used to demonstrate the mathematical ratios of music</gloss></term> at the whim of his will. By this means, he brings forth wondrous consonances and composes the sweetest harmony, by which the celestial inhabitants and the minds of the blessed, as well as mortals, may easily be carried away.

->*Robert’s Examination.*<-

He has even extracted these words verbatim from Book 3, Tract 1 of our *History of the Physics of the Macrocosm*, in the book titled *On Mundane Music*. I refer you, just Readers, to that work so that you may draw out the full intention of our Music. I ask that your eyes be fixed not only on those external figures of ours, but also on the internal and formal speculations of our mind. By doing so, you may more correctly understand the true intention of both my pyramids and the harmony created from their proportions. So that this may be more easily accomplished according to your wishes, we shall provide a broader and clearer discussion concerning this knowledge further below.

And because this "good Inquisitor" of ours <note>A sarcastic reference to Marin Mersenne</note> attributes our entire system of world-building and harmonic arrangement to "others unknown"—claiming it belongs to me alone only when it suits him—he speaks always in the plural. This clearly proves that there is actually no fault to be found in the work itself. For this reason, out of malice toward me, he refused to attribute to me the good things he observed in that work. However, at the end, by his "good grace," he finally deigned to name me by my proper name as follows:

### 7. Marin’s text, Column 110.

<margin>Column 110.</margin>*Whose figure Robert Fludd displays in Tract 1, Book 3, on Mundane Music.*

->*Robert’s Examination.*<-

So, while Marin attributes the entire description of my Macrocosmic substance or subject to "unknown and unnamed others," he leaves me with only the credit for one illustration. It is as if I, Robert, following Marin’s own habit, had usurped the inventions of others and made them my own, or accepted what belongs to others as my own work! Truly, Marin, it would befit men who are truly Philosophical and Christian to act more seriously in their corrections and refutations, and to conduct themselves with greater modesty and justice. This means bringing things that truly deserve to be rejected and refuted out into the open, before the eyes of men, along with the author’s name, and then proceeding to correct what is worthy of correction. This is how men driven by justice and piety usually act. If it happens otherwise, the mark of scandal or ill-will in the critic will be apparent not only to the wise man, but even to the ignorant. But now let us see with what wisdom and subtle genius this "Herculean man" <note>Another sarcastic jab at Mersenne's self-importance</note> is able to refute these opinions in Fludd regarding the fabric of the world and its harmony, so that the world may see if he truly is the kind of man he proclaims himself to be in his writings.
