# Wisdom with Folly

## CHAPTER II.

### In which the strength of Mersenne <note>Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), the French philosopher and mathematician who was Fludd's primary intellectual rival</note> shines forth in his attempt to refute the arguments of Robert <note>Robert Fludd, the author</note>, who establishes the structure of the world and its harmony in his own unique way.

<margin>Column 712.</margin>
Now that the opinions of Fludd regarding the structure and harmony of the world have been reviewed by Mersenne, he moves to his next article. In that section, after he had rejected the opinion of Paracelsus <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus, a famous physician and occultist whose ideas Fludd often blended with his own</note> regarding the fabric of the world—dismissing it as (in his words) "monstrous, vain, impious, and foolish"—and after he had examined the minds of others on this matter, he finally seems to approach our own opinion in Column 712. From this, I clearly perceive the reason why, in his preceding article, he dealt with my opinions on the world's fabric and its harmony in such a guarded and secretive manner, without even mentioning my name. He did so, evidently, because he was guided by a better conscience and realized he could not justly bring any argument against my view without manifesting a contradiction of the truth. Indeed, in this very article, he seems to confess and even argue in my favor: for it appears that he is not refuting my ideas here, but rather confirming them, as may be concluded from his own context.

->1. Mersenne’s text. Column 712.<-

> *It is clear that the heaven and the earth were created by God in the beginning, and that on each specific day described by Moses, some new work was produced by God. Whether you call the production of each day a "creation" or use some other term, it matters little. However, I do not in the least call into doubt whether God could, by the power of some light or the Sun created in the beginning, form all things out of the earth and chaos and establish them in their places. For indeed, it has long been established by me that God can accomplish whatever He wishes through any means or causes He chooses. Yet, whether He actually brought the water, the heavens, and their ornaments into their places from that chaos gradually by the power of heat—using the sun or that primary light of the first three days—is another matter. In that regard, we must not devise anything that we have not weighed against the sacred history of Moses (which are the words of God) as if against a <term>Lydian Stone<gloss>A touchstone used to test the purity of gold or silver; metaphorically, a standard of truth</gloss></term>.*

->Robert’s examination.<-

Here, he seems to refute nothing. He acknowledges both the "chaos" and the "power of light" in this passage, and I do not yet perceive from these words that he denies the works of creation as I have described them. He does not deny that God could form all things from the earth or chaos by the power of some light. He even acknowledges that God acts through certain means as He wills, and yet, at the end, he seems to doubt whether God, through the primary light of the first three days, moved the water, the air, the heavens, and their ornaments into their places from that chaos by the power of heat.

In this matter, since this man—so boastful in his own learning—hesitates in doubt, I shall attempt to resolve it for him in very few words. Thus, neither he nor the world will have any further reason to doubt this opinion of mine in the future.

Concerning the confirmation of <term>Chaos<gloss>The formless state of the universe before creation</gloss></term>, or <term>Hyle<gloss>original: hylæ; a Greek term for "matter" used by philosophers to describe the raw substance of the universe</gloss></term> at the world’s beginning: if that "void earth" mentioned before the creation of light, or the "darkness" which was over the face of the abyss, is not enough for you, I would have you look to the opinion of Solomon on this matter. He brilliantly explains the matter or subject from which the world was created, and he does so in express words. According to the translation by Tremellius <note>Immanuel Tremellius, a 16th-century Hebraist and Bible translator</note>, they sound like this: <margin>Wisdom 11:18.</margin> *"Your hand created the world out of unformed matter."* Or as Pagninus <note>Santes Pagninus, a Dominican friar and scholar of the Bible</note> has it: *"The hand of the Almighty made the world from unformed matter."* But Jerome <note>Saint Jerome, the 4th-century translator of the Latin Vulgate</note> says: *"Your hand created the orb of the lands from invisible matter."* By these citations, it is declared clearly enough that this matter, insofar as it was unformed, can be considered as nothing other than that original "Hyle," from whose depths the world and everything in it were created once the separation was made. Do you still hesitate?

<meta>The text ends mid-argument as Fludd continues to challenge Mersenne’s skepticism regarding the specific mechanism of creation.</meta>

# On the Contest, Book I.

Mersenne? Since you have the words of Moses expressly confirmed by the Sage <note>Fludd frequently refers to King Solomon as "the Sage" or "the Wise Man," attributing the Book of Wisdom and Proverbs to him.</note>, do you not see? But if these are not sufficient for you, listen to this speech uttered by a man of patience <note>A reference to Job.</note>:

<margin>Job 12:22.</margin>
> He reveals the foundations from the darkness, and brings the shadow of death into the light. And elsewhere: God stretches out the North over the empty and void, and hangs the earth upon nothing. <note>Job 26:7.</note>

Therefore, if you wish to give any place to reason: this word is for the Wise man regarding that subject—namely, concerning the matter out of which God created or made the world—namely, that it is formless matter and darkness, as frequently stated by both Moses and Job. But now that the "matter from which" has been produced, you seem finally to hesitate and doubt concerning the Agent <note>The "Agent" refers to the active force or power that performs the work of creation.</note>, based on those words of yours. The text is as follows: *But indeed, whether by means of the sun or any primary light of the three days, he brought the water, air, heavens, and their ornaments little by little by the force of heat out of that chaos into their places, is another matter; nor should we devise anything in that regard which we have not compared to the sacred History of Moses (which are the words of God) as if to a touchstone.* <note>original: "lapis Lydius" — literally a Lydian stone, used to test the purity of gold, here meaning a standard of truth.</note>

These are your own words.

Therefore, since you seem to doubt the immediate agent in this business, I will show you without great difficulty (if you will give any faith to the Sacred Writings) what that agent is. It is indeed, for certain, that **uncreated light**, which shone forth immediately from the Father for the creation of the world: that Word (I say) sustaining and nourishing all things by its goodness. I do not speak of that literal word whose property is (as Paul testifies) to kill, but that word whose property is to give life, to create all things, and without which nothing exists; for in it is life. **That Light** (I say) **which shines in the darkness and the darkness did not comprehend it, by means of which the world was made.** And we have all of this confirmed by St.

<margin>John 1.</margin>
John. But if you still hesitate further, it was that holy Spirit of Wisdom, whom the Wise man calls the pure emanation of the glory of Almighty God and the brightness of eternal light: concerning whose power in the fabric of the world the Holy Bi-

<margin>Jeremiah 51:15.</margin>
ble writes thus: *He who made the earth by his power, and prepared the world by his wisdom, and by his prudence stretched out the heavens. When he gives his voice, the waters are multiplied in the heaven; when he raises the clouds from the ends of the earth, he makes lightnings and rain, and brings forth the winds from his treasures.* Do you not yet grasp (Mersenne) who that luminous spirit is, by whose power the world was created

<margin>Job 28:23.</margin>
from nothing, or from formless matter? *God* (says Job) *in his wisdom fits the weight to the air, and weighs out the waters by measure; he makes statutes for the rain, and a path for the lightning of the thunders.* But we also have this most excellently confirmed by the Royal Prophet <note>King David, traditional author of the Psalms.</note>, who happens upon this 

<margin>Psalm 104:24.</margin>
sentiment: *The works of God are wonderful and vast, which he made in Wisdom.* <note>In the Latin Vulgate used by Fludd, this is Psalm 103.</note> What need is there for more? Does the Prophet not seem to confirm this most clearly elsewhere? *By the word of the Lord the heavens were made firm, and by the breath of his mouth all their power.* Furthermore,

<margin>Proverbs 8.</margin>
Solomon speaks thus regarding this Word, or holy Wisdom: *Jehovah possessed Wisdom in the beginning of his way, before his works, before any time, before the ages; when he prepared the heavens, I was there (says Wisdom) with him, arranging all things, etc.*

Was Solomon not also taught by means of this Luminous Spirit, which works all things, how the world was made? But right now I seem to see Mersenne smiling with a twisted mouth, because I seem to take the creature for the Creator. But far be it from me to either assert or even think that the divine essence was ever created. But I will never cease to boldly assert that this **uncreated Light** is the internal form of **created light** and to call it its life. Were this not so, why would we give any faith to King David, who says that *God has placed his tabernacle in the Sun*? For the sun was nothing else than that light created on the first day by divine shaping <note>original: "informatione" — in the Aristotelian sense of giving "form" to matter.</note>, and then on the fourth day gathered and contracted into a single pure mass. Before that time, it was said of this crea-

<margin>Psalm 104:2.</margin>
ted spirit: *Clothed with light as with a garment, stretching out the heaven like a skin; you who cover its upper parts with waters, and make the cloud your ascent; you who walk upon the wings of the winds; who make your angels spirits, and your ministers a burning fire; who founded the earth upon its stability, etc.* All these things that uncreated light made and created within the created light, not otherwise than if clothed in a garment. Finally, what else...

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s rebuttal of Marin Mersenne. Fludd is defending his "Mosaic Philosophy," arguing that the light created on the first day of Genesis is not a mere quality but the very "Spirit of Wisdom" or "Soul of the World" that gives life to all things.</meta>

### On the Contest of Wisdom with Folly

Besides that spirit of Wisdom, Moses understood that which moved over the waters, imparting a fiery vigor to them, as Augustine says along with others. Therefore, we do not say, Mersenne, that some created light performed such wonderful works, but rather that uncreated light—the pure emanation (I say) of Almighty God—put on the supreme spirit of the world as if it were a cloak. From this, that spirit, thus animated by divine splendor, was called "created light" and the "Empyrean or fiery spirit." And this spirit (against your stubborn mind and opinion) we call the soul of the world <term>Anima Mundi; the vital force that Fludd believed permeated the entire universe</term> without any hesitation, whose internal act is GOD, as is evident from what has been said; but this will be explained more clearly *in the following sections* where we deal with the soul of the world. 

These things, therefore (my Lord), seem to differ entirely from your Peripatetic philosophy <note>The philosophy of Aristotle and his followers, which dominated medieval and Renaissance universities.</note>, inasmuch as it affirms in a somewhat profane manner that light is an accident <term>accident; in Aristotelian terms, a non-essential property (like color) rather than a fundamental substance</term> and by no means a substance. You yourself are a philosopher of the same flour <note>original: "eiusdem farinæ" — an idiom meaning "cut from the same cloth" or "of the same sort."</note>, since you hold the same sentiment as the heathens against the sacred scriptures. This we shall show more clearly in what follows—not in your manner, with cavils and slanders, but with infallible arguments resting upon the sacred page [scripture]—where we shall declare the subject of that Aristotelian philosophy to be most opposed to the intention of Holy Scripture.

From these things, then, unless you are entirely blinded by vain opinions, you might lay aside all that doubt which you seemed to have in your text; and consequently it appears clearly that my foundations in the method of constructing the world are not as futile and ridiculous as you vainly imagine, and therefore not so empty and weak that they can be refuted by such uncertain and doubtful reasons.

I could produce very many testimonies here of authors famous both in Philosophy and Cabala <note>The Jewish mystical tradition, which Fludd and other "Christian Cabalists" used to interpret the secrets of creation.</note> to confirm these foundations of ours, had I not perceived that you (led by I know not what madness) have rejected and discarded the opinions of almost all of them, holding them as nothing in many of your writings. Wherefore I am forced to bring forward the authority of Scripture to prove these things; and if you, in an impious manner, deny it or attempt to twist it into a contrary meaning, the title of *Atheist* or at least *heretic* will deservedly fall not upon me, but upon yourself.

<margin>Column 712.</margin>
-> 2. *The text of Mersenne in the same place.* <-

> *Therefore, if the aforesaid opinions—which hold that the light of the first three days afterwards gathered itself into the sun, or fashioned the Empyrean [heaven], or was diffused in each individual creature according to its measure and aptitude, and that by the power of the sun the water was separated from the earth, and the air and heavens from both—can be accommodated to the sacred text, I do not so reject them as not to admit them by another reasoning.*

-> *Robert’s Examination.* <-

<margin>Wisdom 7.</margin>
We find and hold in the resolution of the theologians that the Light of the three days subsequently contracted itself into the substance of the sun. Similarly, it is likely that it fashioned the Empyrean or fiery heaven by its presence, since the supreme spirit of the world is informed by divine light; it must necessarily be most luminous and exceedingly fiery in condition, because that holy spirit of wisdom, informing this created Spirit, is clothed in ineffable splendor, which the Sage <note>Solomon</note> also seems to assert in these words: *The spirit of Wisdom,* he says, *is the brightness of eternal light, more beautiful than the sun, and compared to the light, it is found before every arrangement of the stars.* <note>Quoting the Wisdom of Solomon 7:26-29.</note>

<margin>Wisdom 1.</margin>
Moreover, that this Luminous spirit is diffused to each according to its measure and aptitude is taught by holy testimony, by which we are instructed that *this incorruptible Spirit is in all things.* <note>Quoting the Wisdom of Solomon 12:1.</note>

<meta>catchword: Deni- (Furthermore)</meta>

<summary>Fludd argues that the light of the first three days of creation was not a physical accident but the "Spirit of Wisdom" or "Soul of the World," which eventually formed the sun and the Empyrean heaven. He accuses Mersenne of following pagan Aristotelianism rather than the truth of the Holy Scriptures and the Cabala.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s rebuttal of Marin Mersenne. Having previously discussed the "Soul of the World" and the nature of uncreated light, Fludd now addresses how this divine spirit organizes the elements and clarifies his stance on the role of the sun in creation, contrasting his "Mosaic" view with Mersenne’s Aristotelian (Peripatetic) framework.</meta>

->Of the Contest, Book 1.<-

<margin>Jeremiah 51:15.</margin>
Finally, that which arranges everything and separates all the elements in the world from one another, and distinguishes the waters from the waters by means of the firmament, is to be gathered from these words of the Prophet: *He who made the earth by his power, and prepared the world in his wisdom, and by his prudence stretched out the heavens.* And elsewhere: *God by his wisdom fits weight to the air, and weighs out the waters by measure, etc.* <note>A reference to Job 28:25: "To make the weight for the winds; and he weigheth the waters by measure."</note> From these things it is clear that all proportions in the worldly spirit <term>spiritu mundano; the subtle substance Fludd believed acted as a bridge between God and the material world</term>, from which the elements arise, are arranged by this divine spirit. Hence, you see that all these things are most aptly reconciled with the Sacred Scriptures, and consequently, they are not to be rejected or mocked by Mersenne. But he proceeds thus:

### 3. Mersenne's text, in the same place.

> For I know that God confers such great power upon the sun that it could have drawn all bodies which are related to the five days <note>The first five days of creation before the making of man.</note> out of the womb of matter, if you except man, whose soul, since it is immortal and spiritual, is created by God alone. But this neither ought to be said nor can be said, because the sacred text cannot endure that sense, since on both days <note>Meaning the various days of the Genesis narrative.</note> God is said to have produced, or if you prefer, created this and that; nor do we read even a single word that suggests He used the power of the sun in that first production of all things.

### Robert’s examination.

<margin>2 Peter 3:5.</margin>
But indeed, as all good Christians confess, God did this by His Word: which the Apostle also declares in these express words: *For they are ignorant (he says) that the heavens were of old, and the earth consisting of water and through water by the word of God.* By these words we are taught that even that "formless matter" of Solomon, or the "waste and void earth" of Moses (so called because the Divinity which fills all things had not yet shone forth from the darkness for the creation of the world), or the "darkness" or "nothingness" of Job, was the "prime matter" <term>materia prima; the fundamental, formless "stuff" of the universe before God gave it shape</term> from which the world was created. Thus, the waters (which are nothing other than the dark abyss reduced into the appearance of waters by the shining of the divine act) are the "secondary matter" of things, out of which the heaven and the earth, and consequently the whole world, were forged. Here, therefore, are those two true Mosaic matters—namely the first and the second—which the Peripatetic philosophers <note>Followers of Aristotle, whom Fludd accuses of stealing and renaming biblical concepts.</note> falsely attributed to themselves, having changed only the names of the "waste and void earth" or "darkness" into "prime matter," inasmuch as it was not yet led into actuality by the flash and emanation of divine brightness. From this, the Peripatetics acknowledge that this potentiality must be reduced into act, which happens immediately and, as it were, in the blink of an eye by the appearance of the Radiant Spirit of Jehovah. 

As for what pertains to the solar power, I would have you understand first of all that by that word "Sun" commonly used, I understood the "Eternal Sun," which the Prophet teaches us has placed its tabernacle in the "temporal sun," according to the interpretation of Jerome.

<margin>Isaiah 44:24.</margin>
<margin>Ephesians 4.</margin>
<margin>Wisdom 1.</margin>
Has it not also been demonstrated by us *in the preceding sections* that that most bright spirit, existing from eternity, was clothed in light? That is, it was clothed in the worldly spirit, illustrated and illuminated by His presence. This spirit, while it was expanded everywhere through the space of the first three days, on the fourth day contracted itself along with its garment into the mass of the sun. Therefore, although it is common speech to say that the sun acts upon lower things by motion, light, and influence, nevertheless in truth it is that "Central Sun" which dwells in the material sun, which is also said to fill all things and consequently to admit no vacuum in the world. This also the Prophet, speaking in the person of God, seems to declare best in these words: *I am the Lord making all things, stretching out the heavens alone, establishing the earth, and no one is with me.* And elsewhere it is said, *that Christ fills all things.* And elsewhere, *the holy spirit of discipline fills the world, etc.* From which I say to you (O vain Peripatetic!), that all subordinate formal causes <term>formal causes; the Aristotelian concept of the internal "blueprint" or nature that makes a thing what it is</term> are to be deleted and banished into exile from the Christian world, since every action—both mediated and immediate—is to be referred to the one and only God, who is indeed the Act of Acts. For these things (says the Prophet), *says God creating the heavens, stretching them out, alone establishing—*

<sig>C 2</sig>
<meta>The text cuts off mid-sentence; the catchword "mans" suggests the next word is "mansions" or "manifesting" (Latin: firmans).</meta>

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that the creative power usually attributed to the sun is actually the Divine Spirit dwelling within it, and he equates the biblical "darkness" with "prime matter" to discredit Aristotelian philosophy.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s argument against Marin Mersenne, specifically defending the idea that God is the primary mover behind all natural phenomena. Fludd uses a series of biblical citations to argue that the "Sun" in the creation narrative acts as a divine instrument rather than just a physical body with limited power.</meta>

# On Wisdom versus Folly

<margin>Isaiah 42:5.</margin> ...establishing the earth and those things that spring forth from it; giving breath to the people upon it, and spirit to those who walk upon it. From these words it is clear that God performs all things within Himself and through Himself, moving from the First Cause <note>Latin: *à Priori*, referring to the ultimate source</note> to the subsequent effects. This is also expressly confirmed by the Apostle in these words: <margin>Romans 11:32. 1 Timothy 6. Ephesians 4:6. 2 Corinthians 5. 1 Corinthians 12.</margin> *From Him, in Him, and through Him are all things:* <note>Original: *Ex eo, in eo, & per eum sunt res omnes*</note> And elsewhere, *God (he says) gives life to all things in the Word:* And elsewhere, *God is the Father of all, who is over all and through all, and in us all.* And elsewhere, all things are from God, *for God is one.* But he seems to assert this even more clearly in another discourse, saying: *God works all things in all,* and in another place, stripping away all power of acting independently from both the Angels and the stars, and attributing all the acts of the world solely and uniquely to God, he breaks out into this speech: <margin>1 Corinthians 8.</margin> *There are indeed those who are called gods in heaven and on earth, yet for us there is one God the Father from whom are all things and we in Him, and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom are all things, and we through Him.* <margin>Acts 17:28.</margin> Whence also elsewhere: *In God we live, move, and have our being.* <note>Original: *In Deo viuimus mouemur & sumus*</note> This means that every other acting cause is set aside and excluded, as nothing is done in this world without Him. For this reason <margin>Acts 17.</margin> in another passage of the same book it is said that *God, lacking nothing, gives life and inspiration and all things to all.* By these things we are clearly taught that it is not created nature itself which truly performs these works, but God Himself, putting on the image or likeness of the creature and existing at the center of all creatures, using them as if they were His instruments <note>Latin: *organis*, meaning tools or organs of a body</note>, so that through their mediation He may execute His will both in the heavens and on earth. <margin>Baruch 6.</margin> But the Prophet Baruch also seems to testify to this in this sense: *The Sun, Moon, Stars, lightning, spirits, clouds, and fire, etc., all diligently obey God in all things.* For it cannot be otherwise than that all creatures are rendered obedient to the Creator, insofar as they received life and form from the Word or the Spirit of Wisdom at their creation, as Saint John testifies. Since this life is joined to the Divinity and consequently preserved by it—and since that Divinity is most closely joined in an indivisible bond with life and form—it follows that the creature is rendered obedient to the will of the Creator in the blink of an eye.

<margin>Job 38:35.</margin> Hence we find it written of the lightning, His creature: *Will you send out lightnings, and they will go, and returning say to you, "Here we are"?* And of fire elsewhere thus: *God makes the fire grow mild lest it should consume; yet sometimes God makes fire burn in the midst of the waters, so that He might destroy the offspring of an unjust land.* <note>This likely refers to the plague of hail and fire in Egypt described in Exodus</note> Therefore, that conclusion which you make, Marinus <note>Marin Mersenne</note>, was most poor, since your text is as follows:

->Mersenne’s text at the bottom of the column.<-

<margin>Column 7.12.</margin> *Although we have established that the light of the sun existed on the first day, it must by no means be granted that by its power the water was separated from the earth and the air from the water; for experience shows that in the middle of summer the sun can scarcely dry out a single sewer in the space of eight days; how then could it have dried out the whole earth, which we see uncovered, in one or two days?*

->Robert’s examination.<-

Here, readers, you can see how filthy and foul his comparison is, where such holy and divine matters are concerned. It is as if he could not have placed his comparison in mud, clay, or moist slime, but instead had to descend into sewers and dung heaps. Truly, this is not surprising, since the delight of swine is in sewers and filth; but to the point. If it is in God’s will for the Sun to rest fixed in its place, as in the time of Joshua; if He commands it to move backward, as in the days of King Hezekiah; if He intensifies or slackens the heat of fire at His pleasure, as is held in the aforementioned passage and as happened to those three Israelite youths in Nebuchadnezzar's fiery furnace; and if a vehement East wind, at the command of Jehovah, can dry up the sea itself in a single night—then how foolish and filthy was that question of Marinus: whether <term>GOD</term> <note>Printed in all caps to emphasize divine sovereignty</note> would wish, for the sake of a single sewer, to intensify the action of the sun so much that it would destroy the fruits of the earth by drying them out, or change the benevolent season of the year in a prodigious manner?

<summary>Fludd argues that God is the direct, central force acting through all created things, dismissing Mersenne's skeptical comparison of the Sun's creative power to the mundane task of drying a sewer.</summary>

# On the Contest, Book 1.

C 3
<meta>catchword: letters</meta>

But He <note>referring to God</note> reduced and extended the solar fire only for the comfort of living things into a moderate and harmonic proportion, by which the seasons of the year are arranged for the benefit of creatures; nor do these seem to be altered in this age except by miraculous means, namely for the punishment of the wicked—as in that Phaethontic heat <note>A reference to the Greek myth of Phaethon, who lost control of the Sun's chariot and nearly burned the Earth</note> by which Africa is said to have once been set ablaze, of which Ovid sings abundantly in his poetry, and furthermore in the sulfurous rain which He poured down upon Sodom and Gomorrah. But we have this confirmed by the Holy Bible, where the Wise Man speaks thus concerning the fiery creature: <margin>Wisdom 16:24 <note>The Latin text cites 16:14, but the quote corresponds to verse 24</note></margin> *For the creature that serves Thee, who made all things, exerts its effort for the punishment of the unrighteous, and it is slackened for the benefit of those who trust in Thee.* We conclude, therefore, that it is not the Sun that has done these things, but that divine essence, which placed *His Tabernacle in the Sun* <note>Original: *Tabernaculum suum in Sole*; a reference to Psalm 19:4</note> in the same way that He made the mud-built house of man into His temple. Consequently, we say that He who *is clothed with light as with a garment* made those separations of the world by means of the vehicle He uses. Thus, in the Empyrean heaven <note>The highest, fiery heaven of God's residence</note> He is clothed with light *as with a garment*; in the Ethereal heaven He placed *His Tabernacle in the Sun*; in the Elementary heaven, the *thick cloud* is called His hut, and the Prophet says: *The earth shall be opened, and shall bring forth a Savior.* We say, then, that the Word, or the Holy Spirit of Wisdom and discipline, existing everywhere (for as the Wise Man witnesses, *it fills the world*), has *made all things* according to the discourse of Saint John. Consequently, <margin>Wisdom 1</margin> He is the sole author in the world not only of creation, but also of generation, multiplication, and preservation. From Him—to conclude with the axiom of Saint Peter as I began with him—*both the earth and the heavens were created*, for he adds: *Consisting by the word*; wherefore he himself recognizes that the Word is the general agent in the world, as is most clearly declared *from the places cited above*. But truly, *just as all Solar rays*, according to Hermes Trismegistus <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure associated with the Hermetic corpus</note>, are continuous and indivisible from the Sun: *so indeed all internal forms have received their central ray from this eternal source of life.* In Him, Saint John says, *was life: And elsewhere. He gives life to all and breath and all things.* <margin>Acts 17:25.</margin> And the Prophet says: *God creating the heavens, extending them, establishing the earth and what germinates in it, giving breath to the people who are upon it, and Spirit to those walking upon it.* <margin>Isaiah 42:5.</margin> And the Wise Man, understanding this Spirit, says: *The incorruptible Spirit is in all things.* <margin>Wisdom 12:1.</margin> Hence, let Marinus <note>Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), Fludd's intellectual rival</note> see that the opinion of Orpheus and the Pythagoreans does not differ much from that of the Holy Scriptures, since it is their opinion that *all things are full of gods*; yet they referred all these "gods" to a single Jupiter. Similarly, every portion of the incorruptible Spirit is present in every creature for its existence. But truly, all these portions of divine light belong to the one full, eternal, and infinite Word or Spirit of eternal Wisdom; they are no less continuous and annexed in essence to the infinite fountain of divinity than all the solar rays are to the solar mass, or parts to their whole: for the divine essence is indivisible. But now, since your "ethnic" <note>Pagan or secular</note> philosophy seems to disagree with this sacred philosophy of mine, do not on that account (O Marinus) judge it foolish and ridiculous, since it is true. For I wish, by the urging of the Apostle, *to avoid vain and false philosophy*, which ours certainly cannot be, since it is in every way consistent with the holy scriptures—indeed, it is uttered by the mouth of the divine oracle itself. For who among Christians has ever dared to deny that the world was made from formless matter, as if from a *materia prima* <term>First Matter: The fundamental, undifferentiated substance from which all things were created</term>, and that *the earth was void and empty, or that darkness existed before the creation of the world, or that the heaven and earth were formed out of waters as if from a secondary matter*, according to the doctrine of Saint Peter? And that the *Word of God, or the Spirit of the Lord, which moved over the waters, or Wisdom, which was from the beginning and before any creation with God*, stood forth as the sole and unique operator and effecter in such a marvelous fabric? These are the foundations of the Philosophy of Fludd, so rejected by the ignorant; these are the bases of his physical doctrine. Come then (just readers), have you found any trace of atheism in this discourse of mine? Or from these things I have brought forth in this place, can anyone justly condemn me for heresy or wicked magic? In these foundations which I have produced...

# On Wisdom and Folly

You have heard the sacred scriptures speaking on my behalf. But let us proceed to the remainder of his refutation. And let us see whether he does not plunge himself even deeper into the "Sewer of Ignorance" <note>original: *ignorantiæ Cloacam*; a harsh rhetorical flourish typical of 17th-century intellectual debates</note> (to use his own word) while he attempts to falsely accuse others.

->Marin’s text from the same place.<-

<margin>Column 712.</margin>
*I would wish that by the name "Earth" the entire mass of earth and water be understood, upon which darkness was carried—that is, privation.*

->Robert’s examination.<-

Certainly, he wanders far from the truth here, for the primary darkness <note>The darkness present at the very beginning of Genesis</note> can in no way be called "privation," since before it, there was no "position" <note>meaning a positive state of existence or form</note> or formed thing; but "privation," properly speaking, is the removal of some preceding form. <note>Fludd is using logic to argue that you cannot have a "lack" (privation) of something unless that thing existed previously. Therefore, the primordial darkness of creation is a substance or state of its own, not just a lack of light.</note>

->4. Marin’s text. Column 714.<-

<margin>Column 714.</margin>
*If anyone should ask, however, whether it can be affirmed without prejudice to the faith that God produced all bodies at once in a certain mass which we call chaos—especially all the Elements, or even all the stars, which were afterwards excited by the power of heat, separated, and restored each to its own place—and whether the Sun produced grasses, plants, animals, and fish by the power bestowed upon it by God? I answer that this does not involve a contradiction, nor does it so fight against the faith that it cannot be brought into harmony; for it seems to be sufficient if the words of Sacred Scripture keep their truth safe and unharmed. For God is rightly said to have produced all things on individual days, even if light and heat converged to produce them; not only because He bestowed upon light, heat, and the Sun all the power they have, but also because He preserves them, or produces them continuously, and converges with them in every operation, and the Supreme Architect uses them like tools, not out of necessity, but by His will.*

->Robert’s examination.<-

It is most certain that this can be affirmed without prejudice to the faith, since (as was said before) the sacred scriptures testify that the hand of the Almighty created the world from formless matter. And if He created the world, why not also its parts, such as the elements and the stars, and then afterwards those things which are fashioned from them, such as animals and plants? All of these are distinguished by the power of the Sun—not the created Sun, but the eternal Sun <note>The "Eternal Sun" refers to the second person of the Trinity or Divine Wisdom</note> placing His throne within that created one (as was declared before)—namely, by the power of the <term>Eternal Wisdom</term>, and they are divided from one another by an ineffable harmony.

Similarly, it is certain that the external and visible Sun produced plants, herbs, animals, and fish by the power bestowed upon it by God—by the power, I say, bestowed upon it, that is, through the mediation of His Word or the Holy Spirit of Wisdom, who *placed His tabernacle in the Sun.* <note>Psalm 19:4</note> Is this same thing not also indicated in the passage cited above from the Prophet? <note>Isaiah 42:5</note> *God said these things, creating the heavens, stretching them out, making the earth firm and those things that sprout from it, giving breath to the people, and Spirit to those walking upon it.* From this it is clear that by His Spirit He made not only the heavens and what is in them, but also the earth and everything moving and living upon it, both animals and vegetables.

Where Marin speaks of heat, Light, and the Sun, he could have added (if he were sufficiently well-versed in the mysteries of God and nature) the *central Sun* within the Sun, who is *clothed with light as with a garment.* <note>Psalm 104:2</note> By a similar regard, we do not say that the visible and external man acts wisely or prudently, since he is nothing other than a "house of mud," but rather he who inhabits that house of clay, in whose center remains the Mind or divine Light. Hence Job says: <margin>Job 4.</margin> *Man inhabits a house of clay.* And of that internal man he speaks elsewhere: <margin>Job 33:4.</margin> *There is a Spirit in man, but the inspiration of the Almighty gives him understanding.* Thence he argues that the created human spirit is ruled and governed by the divine Spirit implanted within it, which is indeed to be understood both regarding his vital gift and his rational gift. Hence elsewhere: <margin>Job 33:4.</margin> *The Spirit of the mighty God made me, and the breath of the Almighty gave me life.* This same thing must also be said of the visible Sun: namely, that which is visible in the Sun is not

it is that which acts so powerfully upon the world; rather, it is that central light full of divinity, namely the Creator reigning within the creature. For the Creator is bound to His creature by such a proportion of Harmony, and that by an inviolable bond, that the creature could not exist or endure for even a moment of time without Him. And hence it is that, just as that eternal Light—which is the most general class of all forms—is quite simple in its nature and more eminent in its clear brightness than all things, so it is also most mobile in its disposition. Solomon testifies to this, affirming that *Wisdom is more mobile than all mobile things*. <margin>Wisdom 7</margin> <note>original: *Sap. 7*; refers to the Wisdom of Solomon 7:24: "For wisdom is more moving than any motion."</note> This divine Light, therefore, is first of all mobile; next, by its mobility, it excites heat; and by its heat, it acts by refining and separating according to the will of the agent. Moreover, the Sun was created as an Instrument chosen by God, so that from it He might liberally pour His power into the lower regions, according to that saying of the Wise Man: *The sun, giving light, has looked upon all things, and his work is full of the glory* <margin>Ecclesiasticus 42</margin> <note>Ecclesiasticus is also known as the Book of Sirach.</note> *of the Lord*. And elsewhere: *Great is the Lord who made the sun, at whose* <margin>Ecclesiasticus 43</margin> *word it hastened its journey*. However, that glory of the Lord, which fills the solar body, is not empty of operation, but is always mobile and ready to fulfill the will of God everywhere in the world according to the ordinary laws of nature. For it acts and moves in the "sermons," or rather the Word and will of God. Therefore, if Mersenne had wished to look at the matter a little more accurately, as it truly stands in the divine Scriptures, he would perceive no difference between my opinion on the fabric of the world and the sense of the Holy Bible.

Here indeed, in express words (fair Judges), you have the force of Mersenne’s refutation which he used against Fludd’s *History of the Macrocosm*. When well-considered, it seems to be such that any wise man would take it as confirmation rather than refutation. From this, it can clearly appear to you that he himself relies on quibbles and words heavy with slander, rather than on the science of refuting based on well-established, true opinion.

You have heard (kind Readers) how simple and empty were Mersenne’s arguments in his "Babylonian mass," <note>Fludd is likely mocking Mersenne's massive 1623 commentary on Genesis, *Quaestiones celeberrimae in Genesim*, comparing its density and confusion to the Tower of Babel.</note> with which he attacked Fludd’s opinion on the fabric of the world with such boasting. "The mountains are in labor, and a ridiculous mouse is born." <note>A famous Latin proverb from Horace (*Ars Poetica*), used to describe grand promises that lead to nothing.</note> Here indeed he seems to descend to a refutation of the harmonies themselves. But when the matter is well-weighed, in place of refuting them, he dared to say nothing—nor can he, for that matter—since he speaks with naked words thus: *Text: Concerning Fludd’s harmonies, there is no reason for us to add more here, because they do not pertain to the matter, etc.* In his preceding text, you certainly heard him speak more for me than for the opposing side, as is clearer from what came before. Yet he filled an entire Column <margin>Col. 110.</margin> with the repetition of it, under a mask of plurality; and nevertheless, when he arrives at the examination and correction of the same, he says *that it pertains very little to his matter*.

O, how strong is that reasoning of the giant's <note>Fludd sarcastically refers to his opponent, Marin Mersenne, as a "giant."</note> refutation? But he continues thus: Text: *Where (he says) the matter allows, we will discuss these things:* He certainly says this to excuse himself to the reader, perhaps thinking that this promise of his will be forgotten by the readers in such an immense and tedious reading. For as far as I am concerned, I have been unable to find any place in his volume that contradicts my harmony, except for Column 1556, where he spoke of my worldly harmony and its consonances—though his words were frivolous enough and did nothing to fundamentally refute my worldly music. Nevertheless, we have expressed his text in this manner:

<margin>Col. 1556.</margin>
## Mersenne’s Text, Column 1556.

*If indeed anyone is able to draw the true ratio of the harmony of virtues from the deep abyss of divinity, he has my permission: for now, I omit all those things, as well as the Metatron <note>original: "Mitattron"; a high-ranking angel in Jewish mystical tradition.</note> of the Rabbis, and the fire of the Pythagoreans which they called the "divine guard," <note>original: "θεὸν φυλακὴν" (theón phylakēn).</note> just as I omit Fludd’s formal, material, and worldly pyramids and harmonies, since all those things seem to lack reason and were devised for honorable leisure and delight rather than for truth. Thus, I now agree with Kepler, who says that Fludd's Worldly Music consists of poetic and oratorical symbolisms; otherwise, Robert would be devising many falsehoods, as Kepler sufficiently showed in the Appendix to the 5th book of his Harmonics—despite whatever that impotent squire <note>Mersenne uses "impotens armiger" (impotent squire) as a derogatory jab at Fludd’s status as a gentleman/physician rather than a "true" mathematician.</note> cries out in his "Analytical Discourse" in defense of his Harmony against Kepler's Appendix, Text 14, and other passages. And indeed, I wonder how Kepler, in the Apology for his Appendix on Harmonics, responds so coldly to Robert. Perhaps the heretic feared to be angry with a fellow heretic. <note>Mersenne, a Catholic, is mocking the fact that both Kepler (a Lutheran) and Fludd (an Anglican) were considered heretics by the Roman Church.</note> Fludd responds again, but his response has not yet reached me; let those who have the leisure look at it, for I dare to say it produces nothing but nonsense. I will only remark that those authors who believe, or rather pretend, that they possess the secrets of a more recondite doctrine, do not dare to speak more clearly, nor are they able to support their claims by even a single reason or demonstration. Unless they do so, let them keep their trifles to themselves, for I do not wish to waste time discussing their tangles. Therefore, having omitted these trifles of the Hermeticists, I proceed to Geometrical reasons, etc.*

## Robert’s Examination.

**Text.** *If indeed anyone [seeks] the true [ratio] of virtue, etc.* Here, out of his own grace, he has deigned to give permission to men to search for and draw the true ratio of the harmony of virtues from the deep abyss of divinity. If, therefore, I recognize that the divine emanation is that Universal Music in the world, whose Instrument <note>original: "Organon"; can mean both a musical organ and a logical tool.</note> is the worldly vessel resounding everywhere with the Spirit—its true string being forged from the mixture of both pyramids, the formal as well as the material—then the various harmonies arise from the varying immersion of the Spirit of Wisdom into the multitude of worldly matter. This occurs according to the degrees of its descent from unity into multiplicity, by which proportion the worldly spheres are said to differ from one another in position and nature. From this, qualities of diverse influences are rained down daily into the lower regions, and we call the mutual receptions and operations of these influences into the "matrices" of the elements the "effects of Worldly Harmony." If (I say) we say that deeper sounds result from a deeper penetration of the creating form into the spiritual mass of the world—that is, into the denser Spirit—is this to be considered against the laws of Sacred Scripture? Or is it not proven by common experience, since we observe that a dense and thick string produces deep sounds, just as a thinner one produces sharper ones? Hence, Pythagoras, speaking of this instrument, says: *The worldly Spirit is a great Divine Organ, or instrument, perpetually resounding in earth as in heaven, and holding the higher things with the lower in peace and concord.* In this speech, he does not seem to differ from the opinion of <margin>Job 28.</margin> the Holy Page, which expresses the sentiment: *God by His wisdom fits the weight to the air and weighs the waters by measure: He makes decrees for the rain, and a path for the lightning.* <note>Referencing Job 28:25-26.</note>

# Of the Contest, Book 1.

...of thunder, etc. Here, therefore, you have the Instrument <term>Organon: while often meaning a musical organ, Fludd uses it here to describe the world as a complex tool or musical mechanism</term> of the world, well-prepared by the true musician of the world, and arranged to emit the peaceful sounds of life: who also extends the world's monochord <term>Monochord: a single-stringed instrument used by ancient theorists to demonstrate the mathematical proportions of musical intervals; Fludd uses it as a metaphor for the structural harmony of the universe</term> to the true tones of Harmony, distinguishes it into its proper intervals, and adapts both heaven and earth according to His will. The Prophet seems to support this assertion of ours in these words:

<margin>Jeremiah 15:11</margin>
> Who made the earth by his power, and prepared the world by his Wisdom, and by his prudence stretched out the heavens.

<margin>Wisdom 7</margin>
And hence it was that Solomon confessed he had reached the true fabric of the world and the reasoning of its Harmony, with Wisdom as his teacher. This same Spirit is observed elsewhere to argue for the Harmony of the worldly spirit in these golden and clear words:

<margin>Wisdom 19:17</margin>
> For (he says) while the elements are changed among themselves—just as the sound of a quality is changed in an instrument <note>original: "Organo"</note> and all things keep their place—which can be surely estimated from the very sight. For wild things were changed into water-dwellers, and whatever were swimming things passed over onto the land; fire prevailed in water beyond its own power, and water forgot its extinguishing nature, etc.

These, then, are the true laws of worldly Harmony, by which all things, according to the moderation of divine Wisdom, are accustomed to give forth hidden sounds in the world, and to be preserved in concordant peace from the world's beginning even to this day—and that more or less according to the good pleasure of the creator. Solomon, therefore, was instructed by the reason of this Harmonic type, as was said, in how the world was made, and he had a perfect knowledge of its true Harmony. And since it is said that this divine Wisdom placed

<margin>Psalm 19:4; Ecclesiasticus 43</margin>
> His tabernacle in the Sun. Whence also elsewhere: The Sun, announcing as it appears, a vessel at its going forth, the wonderful work of the Most High.

And elsewhere:

<margin>Ecclesiasticus 43</margin>
> The Sun, illuminating all things, looked upon them, and his work is full of the glory of the Lord.

Therefore (I say), since Wisdom herself is the center of every thing, whose circumference is nowhere <note>Fludd invokes the famous Hermetic definition: "God is an infinite sphere whose center is everywhere and whose circumference is nowhere."</note>, and since she proceeds from the mouth of the eternal God (who, in the manner of a monochord not at all divided into intervals, but existing in a state of permanent unity), and emanating from the Father or Unity, she made the first division of the worldly monochord—not in the essence of the string, but in the property of proportion. But truly, because Wisdom is the image and likeness of the Father, it was necessary that this emanation, both in the perfection of Harmonic proportion and in likeness, should have an expressed relation to its luminous source.

For indeed, the closest and nearest relation is between the unison and the Octave <term>Diapason: the Greek term for the interval of an octave, which Fludd views as the most perfect mathematical relationship (2:1) reflecting the first step from God into creation</term>; since both are held in a double proportion to each other, namely, as 1 to 2. Whence if the monochord 1 is divided into two equal parts, with the compression in its middle or center, then two Octave consonances will result. By which facts it seems to be clearly declared that the greatest Harmonic force consists in the middle of the string of the world's monochord, and consequently, that the principal seat of the divinity, emanating and shining forth from the luminous source for the creation of the world, is in the central middle of the world. And since the visible Sun is said to be most full of divine glory and majesty, we have therefore not ineptly represented it in the center of the world's monochord.

But all this is indicated by the effect; for first, St. John says that in the Word is life, and we are taught by daily experience that the acts of life are governed annually by the sun, in vegetables as well as in animals, and indeed in minerals. But since we have spoken at sufficient length about the type of this mystery in our little book called *The Symphonic Monochord of the World*, we will only express the image of the demonstration of that Monochord in this place: so that from it you, benevolent readers, may see whether [Marin] Mersenne <note>original: "Marinus" - referring to Marin Mersenne, a French mathematician and musical theorist who was a fierce critic of Fludd’s mystical "occult" science</note>, along with others of his ilk, can find any palpable error in it, and whether or not Robert [Fludd] uttered trifles in his response to Kepler, as Mersenne pretends in his text to have heard. But he who chattered and babbled against the truth is like a dog barking vainly at the Sun; for despite the fault-finder <note>original: "momo" - referring to Momus, the Greek god of satire and mockery</note> and the envious, the truth shall enjoy the trophy which she herself has fixed.

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense of his cosmological models. Having established the "World Monochord" as a divine instrument, Fludd now addresses the specific geometric diagrams—his "formal and material pyramids"—which he uses to map the relationship between spirit and matter. He specifically responds to the harsh criticisms of the French mathematician and priest, Marin Mersenne.</meta>

### Concerning Wisdom with Folly

By this demonstration of the **World Monochord**, the logic of our **Pyramids**—namely the formal and the material—is most clearly explained. Indeed, these pyramids in turn reveal the hidden mystery or intention of that monochord. Therefore, the assertion made by Marinus <note>Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), a French mathematician and philosopher who was a fierce critic of Fludd’s mystical approach to science.</note> in this context is entirely false, where he says that Fludd’s formal and material pyramids lack any basis in logic or musical proportion. But because he attacks Fludd’s pyramids so sharply in many parts of his book—for example, in *Column 1556*, he says: *Fludd’s formal and material pyramids lack reason*; and in *Column 1744*: *I challenge that Fludd with all his waves <note>original: "fluctibus"—a Latin pun on the name "Fludd."</note> and his formal and material Pyramids*; and in *Column 1750*: *Fludd shows his ignorance more than enough with his inverted and reversed pyramids,* etc.—I have therefore resolved to dispute these claims briefly in this place. Marinus says that Fludd's pyramids "lack reason," as if he were saying they have no relationship to the **Harmonic fabric of the world**. Let us now examine whether this assertion of his agrees with the truth or not.

It can remain hidden to no one moderately versed in Philosophy—indeed, even to those not lacking in basic reason—that the earth itself is essentially dense water, just as water is dense air, and air is the thickness of fire itself, and fire is the thickness of the ether, and the ether is the thickness of the **Empyrean Spirit** <term>Empyrean Spirit: The highest, most refined spiritual substance, associated with the dwelling place of God and pure light</term>. All these dimensions, differing in their thickness and density, are considered from the perspective of worldly matter. <meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense of his "Pyramidal" cosmology against the criticisms of Marin Mersenne. # Contest, Book 1.
<note>original: "Certamine, Lib. 1." — Referring to Fludd's intellectual "contest" or polemic against Marin Mersenne.</note>

d 2

is made the base of this kind of universal matter ascending upward; and this matter, in its ascent, excels more in thinness the further it is exalted upward from its terrestrial base. For this reason, we have not unadvisedly compared it to a mathematical pyramid, which, in its ascent from the base toward the cone, gradually decreases and is diminished in the dimension of its bulk. In this way (I say), worldly matter <note>original: "materia mundana"</note> in ascending is always of a thinner dimension. And this is the reason why we have fashioned that material pyramid, for by observing it, we have the type of worldly matter described as exactly as possible. For if, starting from the earth and progressing toward the summit of the world, we take a parallelogram-shaped portion of worldly matter, and compress its upper part—by which the matter expanded above is reduced to an identity of bulk with the lower part—we shall see the material pyramid created from it. That you may understand this more easily, I would have you diligently weigh this pyramidal demonstration, taken from the *Book of Pyramids at the end of my technical History of the Microcosm* <note>Robert Fludd refers here to his own massive work, *Utriusque Cosmi Historia*, specifically the section on the Microcosm (the human being) as a reflection of the Macrocosm (the universe).</note>, which explains the disposition of worldly matter very well indeed.

From these things, one may see that this material triangle swells at its base, but in its true cone, it ends in a very acute angle beyond which nothing of matter is imagined to exist; and consequently, it is clear that the more one tends upward, the more the existence of matter decreases. But we shall render the reason for this thing in the world more clearly *below*. However, since we have already noticed that **Form** is, by nature and condition, most adverse to **Matter**, it is therefore necessary that its motion and position be also opposite to that matter. And we have this confirmed by the testimony of the Fathers of Theology themselves:
<margin>Psalm 68:34; Psalm 133; Psalm 122:1.</margin>
For in the first place, it is said, *The Spirit of the Lord was carried over the waters*, and therefore not in the center of the waters. And elsewhere: *God sat upon the heaven of heavens of old.* And elsewhere: *Jehovah God dwells in the highest height.* And elsewhere: *God's seat is in the heavens.* And elsewhere: *God is higher than heaven and is exalted above the vertex of the stars,*
<margin>Job 22.</margin>
by which it is certain that he understands another heaven besides that visible one. Here, therefore, you see the place originally assigned to God: the heaven of heavens, which is above the vertex of the stars. That its nature is fiery or lucid, as being illuminated by the divine presence, is to be gathered from these following things:
<margin>Psalm 75:4; Psalm 104; Deuteronomy 4.</margin>
*God (says the Prophet) illuminates wonderfully from the eternal mountains, etc. God is clothed with light as with a garment, stretching out the heavens like a skin.* And elsewhere: *He is called a consuming fire.* Furthermore, He is frequently called the **Father** and **Source of Light** by the sacred scriptures. From these, it is right to conclude that the lucid source and truly **formal** place has its position at the very highest summit of the world. Wherefore, some of the Fathers of Theology have called that portion of the created Spirit the **Empyrean Heaven**, or **Fiery Heaven**, in which the Angels and Saints, most filled with divine light, have their habitation, always beholding the face of God. Next, they called the middle heaven the **Aethereal**, as if it were a fiery air, or air adorned with fire or light in an equal and moderate proportion. But the lowest region of the world they called **Elemental**, where matter is more exuberant than form; and for this reason, we notice that it eagerly affects celestial form at every moment. From this, it is evident that a greater portion of matter is present in it than of the life-giving Light: and hence it is that this region is subject to so many mutations. But, being instructed by Philosophical reason, I proceed to prove it thus: Where there is a greater portion of divine Light, there is a greater power and act of motion and vitality; but motion is the offspring of heat itself, whose property is to make the dense rare and subtle, and by thinning a compact thing into most minute parts, to disperse it according to its strength.
<margin>Wisdom 7.</margin>
But the *Holy Spirit of Wisdom (says Solomon) is more mobile than all mobile things, and reaches everywhere because of its purity, and has all power in itself, insofar as it is the splendor of eternal Light, or an emanation of almighty brightness, far clearer than the Sun or the stars.* By which it may shine clearly enough to the world that the more matter is gross and opaque, the more it is destitute of light itself (which acts fundamentally in the world). Consequently, the thinner, clearer, and purer the worldly matter is, the greater portion of creating Light it claims for itself. From this, it is right to conclude that just as motion and the bulk of matter vary from bottom to top...

# On the Contest of Wisdom with Folly
<note>original: "De Sophiæ cum Moria [certamen]" — This refers to the title of Fludd's book, where Wisdom (Sophiæ) is Fludd's philosophy and Folly (Moria) represents his critic, Mersenne.</note>

[The material pyramid] relates to itself in the manner of a pyramid, reaching from the earth as its base upward toward the luminous source with its point <note>original: "cono" — the apex or cone of the pyramid</note>; it does so in such a proportion and exactly opposite the Light. From the luminous and purely formal source, which has its seat at the summit of the heaven of heavens, a pyramid also descends in the same manner, from a super-celestial base down to the very center of the earth. This pyramid is always in motion, diminishing in its power and vigor as it moves downward. This is most clearly shown because the more it stretches from the top toward the bottom, the more the matter is strengthened and thickened, as has been said. For this reason, we say that the **formal pyramid** <note>The "Formal Pyramid" represents the descent of Divine Light or Spirit into the world.</note> descends in exactly the same proportion as that **material pyramid** <note>The "Material Pyramid" represents the ascent of physical substance toward the divine.</note> ascends. 

We prove this by the fact that all the diversity of worldly matter arising from the darkness is due to the presence of the light; it is a result of the various actions of the light emanating from that luminous source. Just as we notice all parts of water to be of the same kind <note>original: "homogeneas"</note>, so too the Spirit or created matter of the world—which Saint Peter called "water"—behaved in its own nature. But by the presence of the divine light, that mass of water was divided into more noble and less noble parts: namely, into the heaven and the earth, which Saint Peter recognizes as consisting of water through that luminous Word. 

And then again, the heaven was divided into the **Empyrean**, the **Ethereal**, and the **Elementary** regions. The former excel the latter in dignity because of the greater or lesser abundance of light in their composition. Afterward, a subdivision was made in each of them: in the Empyrean, into various levels of Hierarchies and orders of Angelic creatures; in the Ethereal, into the stellar spheres; and in the Elementary, into the spheres of the Elements, of which fire is preferred in dignity over air, air over water, and water over earth. 

Our worldly music, therefore, does not consist of the distances or intervals of spaces numbered between the base and the point of the material pyramid, but rather from the various acts of divine light within the worldly Spirit. This light fills the material pyramid and makes it resonate in a symphonic manner <note>original: "more symphoniaco"</note>. 

Truly, just as the *Word, or Wisdom, or the Son proceeding from the mouth of the Father, who placed his Tabernacle in the Sun*, is called the *image of the Father*, so we have designated that place (indicated by the point where the two pyramids intersect) as the sphere of equality. We have assigned this sphere to that wonderful vessel most full of the glory of its creator—namely, the **Sun**. In this location on the **World Monochord** <term>Monochordum mundani: a single-stringed musical instrument Fludd used as a metaphor for the harmonious proportions of the entire universe.</term>, we observe that the **Diapason** <term>Diapason: the musical interval of an octave, considered the most perfect harmony.</term>, both spiritual and material, has its seat. This is the most perfect of consonances, giving forth its tone in the image of the Unison, or its Father. 

It is not without proven reason that we have established the **World Monochord** as a ray common to both pyramids. But so that you, readers, may understand this better, we have crafted it first by the logic of the formal pyramid, and then according to the nature, position, and adaptation of both pyramids. I do this so you may more easily understand my meaning, promising you by my Christian faith that if anyone in all of Europe, even moderately versed in philosophy, should doubt this matter, I could make him so certain and secure in it through visual demonstrations <note>original: "demonstrationib. ocularibus"</note> within the space of one hour that he could have no further doubt. Indeed, I assert with confidence and a clear conscience that I have demonstrated this to certain men far more learned than Marinus <note>Likely referring to a specific scholar or perhaps a misspelling of a name like Marini.</note> and deeper in true philosophy, and satisfied them with my demonstrations.

Therefore, I would not wish that in the future Mersenne should shout and proclaim these inventions of mine to be trifling, frivolous, and born from my own brain, since authors worthy of trust seem to happen upon this same opinion of mine. For in the second chapter of the book *The Gate of Light* <note>original: "Porta Lucis" — A significant Kabbalistic text by Joseph Gikatilla.</note>, we read these things: *All things having life tend upward by their instinct, that is toward perfection, which matter greedily desires; but all things infusing life always tend downward.* From this, therefore, the appetite of matter tending upward toward the "Palace of Form," and the office of the "Inflowing Form," is openly expressed. And the Archangel <note>Archangelus of Borgonovo, a Christian Kabbalist.</note> in his Kabbalistic dogma says: *Whatever is distant from God in its first and purest act, must necessarily enter into matter,* from which the naturalists, describing the latitudes of beings...

at its summit they create pure act without matter; at the bottom is matter without form. These are his words. By "matter without form," he therefore understands the earth, which is empty in itself <note>original: "inanem" — a reference to the "void" earth of Genesis 1:2.</note> and fertile only by accident, insofar as it receives the influences emitted from the luminous source above, to which it serves as a female womb.

Therefore, having considered these things well, you see (O just judges) that the Monk was led more by envy and malice than by any good and just cause when he loudly proclaimed to the world that these **Pyramids** published by me for the light of the public—along with the harmonies collected from their observation—lack any reason. And why? *Because he himself said so, namely Kepler.*

# On [the Contest of] Wisdom with Folly

he seems to boast vainly and idly to the world. Whose seditious and turbulent nature is manifested in the following part of his own text. **Text.** *And indeed I wonder how Kepler responds so coldly to Robert, etc.* To which I say: Certainly the reason is obvious, because namely, Kepler himself is a most learned man, and in mathematical matters a thousand times to be preferred to Mersenne, even though Mersenne seems to snarl at him and many others. Kepler (I say), as befits a Philosopher, acted with arguments and reasons, both philosophical and mathematical, and in these he satisfied the world, not with brawls and old wives' quarrels, as Marinus [Mersenne] does (in a manner of arguing hateful to both God and men). He [Kepler] (I say) dealt with me using sufficiently valid arguments, and likewise, when I threw back reasons that were by no means lacking, he was pacified and reconciled—not because he is a heretic, but because he was nurtured in the house of philosophy and educated in human morality. For I would rather endure the false title of "heretic" than, by falsely putting on the name of "Catholic" while plunging myself into the abyss of immorality, to attack someone with slanders without reason and speak ill of him, led by some sort of malevolence and envy.

### CHAPTER IV.

->*In which Robert Fludd proves that his opinions are not new, and that his doctrine is furthest removed from Atheism, and that Marin Mersenne himself, as a rash judge, has ignorantly cast himself into the abyss of Atheism.*<-

->Mersenne’s text, in the same place.<-

*I warn some in passing to beware of new opinions which idle heads <note>original: "male feriata... capita" — literally "poorly holidayed heads," meaning people with too much leisure time who invent nonsense.</note> generate daily, which lead to the subversion of Philosophy under the shadow of some small reasons, and introduce atheism; if you weigh these more accurately, they will manifest their own weakness, and you will judge immediately that they proceeded from weak brains, as I will demonstrate below in their proper places.*

->Robert’s examination.<-

<margin>Proverbs 8</margin>
Mersenne speaks well in this: "let them beware," he says, "of new opinions which idle heads generate daily," etc. But I will subjoin these words uttered by him: *If the sacred text does not bear their sense in the least.* But of such a kind is the philosophy of <term>Aristotle</term> <note>Fludd frequently attacks Aristotle as the source of "heathen" errors in contrast to his own "Mosaic" or biblical philosophy.</note>, which Mersenne, along with others of the same cloth, embraces like an oracle sent down from heaven, as we shall show more widely below. But what are those opinions which Marin considers new? Certainly if he understands ours, he is deceived and he deceives; for to speak with the scriptures, and use the words of Divine Wisdom herself (since she is the first principal actress of my principles): *she was the active principle of my principles in the beginning of the way of Jehovah, before his works, before any time, before any age anointed, ordained from eternity before the earth was made, when there were no abysses, before the mountains were founded; when he prepared the heavens, my acting principle was there, when he established the circuit of the surface of the deep, when he fortified the upper clouds above, when he set the sea its decree for the waters lest they should transgress their boundaries, when he established the foundation of the earth, this active principle of mine was with Jehovah (from whose mouth it went forth) composing all things,* as Solomon testifies.

<margin>Wisdom 11</margin>
Similarly, my first **passive principle** <note>In Fludd's system, the "passive principle" is the primordial matter or darkness (the "Waters" of Genesis) that receives the "active" light of God.</note> is of such antiquity that it existed even before the creation of Light, namely as the empty earth and darkness which were over the face of the abyss, or that unformed matter from which that wisdom, or our agent composing all things, made the world.

<margin>2 Peter 3</margin>
Finally, my secondary passive principle (called <term>secondary matter</term> <note>Materia secunda: matter that has already received some form, as opposed to the pure chaos of primary matter.</note> by the <term>Peripatetics</term> <note>The followers of Aristotle.</note>) is even prior in time to the creation of light, for the spirit of the Lord was moved over the waters before the appearance of created light. Furthermore, it is clear from the aforementioned passage of St. Peter that the heavens were before, and the earth [was made] of water, and through water.

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s vigorous rebuttal against Marin Mersenne. On the previous page, Fludd defended his character by comparing himself to Kepler and accusing Mersenne of being a "seditious" and "turbulent" critic. # On the Contest, Book 1.

What do you say, Mersenne? Are these Fludd’s principles, or are they new opinions, or do they disagree "by a hair's breadth" <note>original: "ad tranſuerſum vnguem" — a Latin idiom meaning "by a fingernail's width," or not at all.</note> from the sense of the Holy Bible? Certainly, I would think you were seized by madness if you spoke so vilely of me and my foundations from your heart. But, good readers, is this man to be considered a just judge, who, with the cause barely known, or the writings of others hardly understood, nevertheless hastens to judge and condemn them in such a reckless manner?

But because he continues, saying in his text: *“From opinions which lead to the subversion of true Philosophy under the shadow of some minor reasons and bring in atheism, etc.”* Certainly Mersenne speaks well, if he understands well enough, for he seems to fall into the sentiment of the Apostle, who warns us to beware of "vain philosophy" <note>A reference to Colossians 2:8: "Beware lest any man spoil you through philosophy and vain deceit."</note>. But here a question arises: what might that "true Philosophy" be? Is it that which is derived from the sacred fountain and supported and confirmed by its testimony, or rather that ethnic <note>original: "ethnica" — here meaning pagan or non-Christian.</note> or Aristotelian philosophy, which Mersenne and those like him venerate with such great devotion? Indeed, he does not blush to publish it in this place as "true philosophy" to the world, and consequently, he contends that the philosophy drawn from the sacred letters should be regarded as vain and a "new opinion." Since that Aristotelian philosophy is opposed to the sacred sense in almost everything, I will hold and esteem as the worst kind of Christian the one who, having neglected the Bible, follows Aristotle. For truly, if there be no truth except from the Spirit of Wisdom—seeing as that Spirit is the very same and only truth—that man will be seized by extreme madness who attempts to prefer a lying spirit to the truth. 

Nevertheless, we shall show that the Aristotelian spirit is lying and opposed to sacred philosophy. Therefore, he who (contrary to Mersenne's mind) approaches the subversion of Peripatetic <term>Peripatetic: The school of philosophy following the teachings of Aristotle.</term> Philosophy, and seeks to establish in the world that true Mosaic <note>Mosaic philosophy: A 17th-century movement, led by figures like Fludd, which sought to build a system of science and nature based strictly on the Book of Genesis and other biblical texts rather than Greek philosophy.</note> philosophy confirmed throughout the whole thread of sacred Scripture, will, I believe, rather drive out atheism from this Christian world. He will make true Christians according to the internal effect, rather than those who are Christians only in outward appearance, instead of introducing atheism into the Christian world. For the question can then arise: does the certainty of Philosophy exist greater in the writings of the Pagans (who never acknowledged either Jehovah or the word of Jehovah) or in the fountain of truth, full of the word and divine wisdom? Certainly, among true Christians, no doubt ought to be held in this matter. And yet, with unheard-of audacity, Marin [Mersenne] calls that Aristotelian philosophy "true"—for he cannot mean any other. He was, after all, educated in it along with others of his sect (as is the worst custom in Christian schools), which indeed he himself seems to verify in Column 365 in these words:

<margin>Column 365.</margin>
**Text.** *“But let us approach Aristotle, the disciple and nursling of true philosophy, whom the Holy Doctor <note>Mersenne likely refers to St. Thomas Aquinas, who integrated Aristotelianism into Catholic theology.</note> valued so much that he published extensive commentaries upon him, etc.”*

Furthermore, in Column 1385, he writes thus:

<margin>Column 1385.</margin>
**Text.** *“Aristotle—that is, the 'best end' <note>A pun on the Greek etymology of Aristotle's name: 'aristos' (best) and 'telos' (end/purpose).</note>—handed down to us those things which lead to happiness and the best end; for to Philosophers, of whom he was the prince, he provides a contempt for human things and a desire for heavenly things and an illumination <term>illumination: the spiritual or intellectual enlightenment of the mind.</term> of the mind, which also promote happiness, etc.”*

What further can be said in praise of the man [Aristotle], or what can we expect from the sacred fountain beyond happiness or the illumination of the mind? This, therefore, is the cause why Mersenne hastens to prefer that pagan philosophy to the Mosaic one, calling it "true," when in reality it is spurious and false, and contrary to the sense of the sacred letters. This will appear most clearly from the evidence we allege, and is declared even more clearly in our *Sacred Philosophy*. Not only the Philosophers of our age, but even Christian Theologians instructed by Aristotle, create a divide between Theology and "sacred Physics." By drawing away the rays of real divinity from created nature, they assert that God himself is essentially not at all present within his created nature—
