# Of the Contest, Book III.

From these points, it is declared clearly enough that Francesco Giorgio <note>original: "Georgius Venetus"; a Venetian Franciscan friar (1466–1540) known for his Christian Kabbalistic work *De Harmonia Mundi*.</note> deserves to be regarded as a man of far higher intellect and deeper learning in the true mysteries of nature and of God than any teacher who would attempt to "purge" or "correct" his works as if they were erroneous (as Mersenne <note>original: "Marinus"; referring to Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), a French priest and mathematician who was a fierce critic of alchemy and magic.</note> falsely reports). Indeed, the children of true Wisdom would rather praise and defend him, rather than following Mersenne's habit of scattering dung-like errors—unsupported by any sufficient reasons—among the precious pearls of a good author. Finally, it is unjust for a man engaged in Philosophy to stuff his pages with diabolic slanders and poorly applied facts, and consequently, for no other reason provided by Agrippa <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535), a famous Renaissance occult philosopher.</note> than the mere repetition of Angelic names, to rashly proclaim him a Sorcerer and a <term>Arch-magician</term> <note>original: "Archimago"; a chief or powerful practitioner of magic, often used as an insult by critics.</note>. For Agrippa himself said that the names of the Angels were revealed to Adam by the Angel Raziel <note>In Jewish mysticism, Raziel is the "Angel of Secrets" who gave a book of wisdom to Adam.</note>; and nevertheless, he was not so forgetful of himself as to not say that we know no names of Angels except by divine revelation—that is, by the mediation of divine Wisdom. 

But divine Wisdom was inscribed within the Angel Raziel, since without her, the angel himself is of no value or existence. The bright power of Wisdom was engraved, I say, within him, insofar as an angel is like a mirror receiving the divine radiance, which is Wisdom itself. And hence it was said by Jehovah:

> *Behold, I send my Angel before you to keep you in this way... he will not forgive your rebellion, for my name is in him,* <note>original: "Ecce mitto Angelum meum ante te ad seruandum te in hac via, non feret ipse defectionem vestram, quia nomen meum est in eo." This is a quote from Exodus 23:20-21.</note>

namely, the divine essence whose unique name is the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note>. From this it follows that even if this revelation was made by Raziel, it does not mean that it did not originate from divine illumination and revelation. Consequently, it is argued that Agrippa was not at all as deceived in this matter as Mersenne believed. Finally, if Mersenne had possessed as much and as profound a knowledge of the mysteries of God and nature as either Giorgio or Agrippa had, he would indeed be ashamed to descend into the field of such a useless controversy; instead, setting aside all suspicion of either <term>Evil Magic</term> <note>original: "Cacomagiæ"; from Greek "kakos" (bad) and "magia" (magic).</note> or Necromancy, he would have judged them worthy to be embraced with reverence and love, and to be regarded as Sages, or true and non-superstitious Magi.

Hitherto (kind readers), the necessity of the subject, which pertains most closely to my cause, warns me to measure and extend my discourse, so that I may truly unlock for you the mystery of the Angels. By this, you may more clearly perceive the notable errors of this "Cynic" author <note>A jab at Mersenne, comparing him to the Cynic philosophers who were known for their biting criticism.</note> and his superficial knowledge of divine mysteries.

But to gird myself more closely to my own specific cause: these things being so, what "evil magic," I ask, can be imputed to us in this discourse? Or what is that error in Robert <note>The author, Robert Fludd, refers to himself in the third person.</note> that is so remarkable? Is he to be sung of throughout the world as a Sorcerer by Mersenne simply because of the naming of Angels? Do there not show themselves here, with eyes wide open, three notable fabrications (not to say lies) brought forth by our "good monk" <note>Mersenne was a member of the Order of Minims.</note> in the preceding contexts? For first, where he says that I produced names of Angels of which the Sacred Scriptures make no mention, I make it known to all of you—indeed, it can be hidden from no one—that we have Michael, Gabriel, Raziel, Raphael, Uriel, Jeremiel, Zachariel, and many other names of Angels expressed in the Sacred Scriptures. In this, therefore, this author is false. Secondly, in that he affirms those names of Angels cited by me are not significant, he reveals the spurious and erroneous condition of a man poorly nurtured in Religion. And what is even more absurd in this, that passage which he condemns is contradictory to himself. And in the last place, this is an unheard-of lie, fashioned with great and impudent presumption: that Robert should be held infamous as a Sorcerer because he assigns certain Angels to every just and pious man—indeed, to every human being from birth—contrary to the intention of the scriptures. For we have the scriptures themselves (with "open mouth," as they say, and with a polished thread of discourse) confirming this very same thing. Indeed, even in the fourth place, he fabricates by saying that the Roman Catholic Church did not approve of such names of Angels being committed to writing. For many are found

<sig>n</sig>
<meta>catchword: among (inter)</meta>

<summary>Robert Fludd defends himself and other Hermetic philosophers like Agrippa and Francesco Giorgio against Marin Mersenne’s accusations of "evil magic," specifically justifying the use of angelic names through scripture and the role of Divine Wisdom.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense against the attacks of Marin Mersenne. Fludd is arguing that his interest in Angelic names and the Kabbalah is shared by esteemed Catholic scholars like Johannes Reuchlin and Johannes Trithemius, whose works received ecclesiastical or imperial approval.</meta>

# Of Wisdom's Contest with Folly

among the Catholic Roman writers who have committed to public writing the names of all the Angels we have recited—and indeed many more—and did so with the approval of the Roman Church. And first, I shall insist upon the most celebrated Doctor Johannes Reuchlin <note>A German humanist and scholar (1455–1522) who was a pioneer in bringing Hebrew studies and the Kabbalah to Christian Europe.</note>, who makes the dedication of his <term>Cabalistic art</term> <note>Christian Kabbalah was the Renaissance study of Jewish mystical texts to find hidden Christian truths.</note> in this manner:

->To the most holy Leo X, Sovereign Pontiff, Johannes<-
->Reuchlin commends himself as a suppliant.<-

<margin>Folio 607.</margin>
By this, therefore, it appears that the art of Reuchlin was approved and confirmed by the Pope; for otherwise, it would never have been published or inscribed to him. But indeed, he himself writes in Book 3 of his *Kabbalah*, folio 607: *"There are 72 sacred names (one of which is called the <term>Semhamophores</term> <note>original: "Semhamophores"; from the Hebrew *Shem HaMephorash*, the "Explanatory Name," a 72-syllable name of God derived from Exodus.</note>, that is, the explanatory name of the most holy Tetragrammaton) to be enunciated with fear and trembling through the invocations of Angels by men dedicated to God. These are Vehuiah, Lieliel, Sitael, Elemiah, Mahasiah, Lelahel, Achaiah, Cahetel, Haziel, etc."* I have not dared to recite the rest of their names here, lest I should offend the chaste eyes of Mersenne <note>original: "Marinus"; referring to Marin Mersenne.</note>, for in this place Reuchlin recites the names of 72 Angels.

<margin>
Folio 725.
Folio 727.
</margin>
And elsewhere he says, *"since they cannot pronounce the name of any Angel with its full meaning, they are accustomed to utter the whole together with the aid of some name of God which they join to it; and thus the name of God, 'El,' is added, as in Michael, Gabriel, Raphael."* So they also, attempting to imitate the Holy Scripture in other Angelic names, say, *"Raziel, Jophiel, Zadkiel, Peliel, Malthiel, etc. Thus the nation of the Jews calls upon Him as 'Jah' because of His benefits, and as 'El' because of His power and virtue; hence Vehuiah, Elemiah, Mahasiah, etc."* These are his words. Elsewhere he mentions <term>Metattron</term> <note>Metatron, an archangel in Jewish tradition often described as the celestial scribe or the "lesser YHWH."</note>, whom he says is the Spirit of the ministry of <term>Shaddai</term> <note>original: "Sadai"; a Hebrew name for God meaning "Almighty."</note>. And elsewhere, he names the Angels of the four winds. And elsewhere, in the army of God, he records four standard-bearers, namely, Michael, Gabriel, Uriel, and Raphael; likewise, he says that in the army of Satan, Samael, Azazel, Azael, and Mahazael carry the banners most widely. Finally, by a logic not dissimilar, Mersenne seems to conclude that this entire treatise of Reuchlin is plainly magical, despite Papal approval—as is that which Trithemius <note>Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516), a Benedictine abbot and polymath whose "Steganographia" was accused of being a book of black magic.</note> dedicated to the Emperor Maximilian; and that of Abbot Sigismund, who defended the *Steganography* of Trithemius; and finally that of the Kabbalist Archangelus <note>Archangelus of Borgonovo, a Franciscan who defended the Kabbalistic theses of Pico della Mirandola.</note>, etc.

<margin>Folio 727.</margin>
By similar reasoning, he seems to condemn as magical that entire treatise of Robert [Fludd] on the *Macrocosm* (as in text 1), especially when he produces Magical characters elsewhere, even if understood in his own way.

<margin>Col. 40.</margin>
Then afterward, at the end of his third context, he [Mersenne] sings out: *"This is the illustrious doctrine which Fluctibus <note>A pun on Fludd’s Latinized name, "Robertus de Fluctibus" (Robert of the Floods/Waves).</note> has propagated; unless he seriously repents, he will soon be submerged in eternal waves,"* etc. And what, Mersenne, is this so-called dangerous doctrine? Is it that which teaches that a specific Angel is assigned by God to every Patriarch, Prophet, Apostle, and even to every other person? O insane and ill-willed Monk, always prone and ready to judge rashly and condemn men of every condition! But because you only speak, and appear to prove nothing throughout your entire volume except to shout, bicker, slander, and speak evil—and to bring forth little or nothing to the purpose as befits a Philosopher—I shall say, by deservedly retorting your own words back at you, and that without any hesitation: this is indeed an illustrious doctrine! You would lead men into errors and false paths away from the true way and the right track; and Mersenne shall soon be submerged in the marine waves <note>Another pun, this time on Mersenne's name ("Marinus"), suggesting he will drown in the sea of his own ignorance.</note> of eternal ignorance and darkness unless he seriously repents. As for my doctrine, I have said nothing of the names of Angels, or of the attribution of an Angelic genius to every man, except what is confirmed by the Wisdom of the ancients and proved by sacred testimony. Since this is so, what is to be said hereafter of these old wives' cavils of Mersenne, except that they are the vain and insane dreams and phantasms of a sick brain, which will be reduced to nothing by themselves without any opposition and will gradually vanish. You have here, Marin Mersenne, the marrow of the Magic of Robert Fludd, whom you so falsely accuse; it proves as much vanity and falsehood in you as there has always been—and, with Christ as leader, shall always be—a horror of <term>Evil Magic</term> <note>original: "Cacomagia"; from Greek, meaning "bad magic" or sorcery.</note> and an eternal hatred of Necromancy in the soul of Robert.

<meta>This page marks the beginning of Book III of Fludd’s defense against Marin Mersenne. It continues the polemic by shifting focus from Fludd’s use of Kabbalah and angelic names to the more serious charge of atheism and the theological status of the soul.</meta>

# On the Contest, Book III.

<note>The title *Sophiae cum Moria Certamen* (The Contest of Wisdom with Folly) is Fludd's direct rebuttal to Marin Mersenne’s accusations. Here, Fludd positions himself as "Wisdom" and Mersenne as "Folly."</note>

## CHAPTER I.

### In which it is debated whether Robert Fludd is justly accused of atheism by Marin Mersenne, on the grounds that he himself seems to acknowledge, in a certain way, the mortality of the soul.

### The text of Marin, Columns 671 and 672.

The first way that atheism can be extinguished is if all books of that kind are removed, and none are kept except those that write what is true and conducive to good morals. I would have you understand that I do not mean only <term>pasquils</term> <note>Short, anonymous satirical poems or lampoons, often posted in public places.</note>, but even books that otherwise seem to embrace good things, unless they teem with atheism. Such are the books of Charron *On Wisdom* <note>Pierre Charron (1541–1603) was a French philosopher; his work was controversial for its skeptical tendencies.</note>, Machiavelli *On the Prince and the Republic*, Cardano *On Subtlety* and *The Judgment of the Stars*, Campanella, the *Dialogues* of Vanini <note>Lucilio Vanini was an Italian philosopher executed in 1619 for heresy and atheism.</note>, Fludd, and many others. It would be worthwhile for these books to either perish entirely or at least be carefully purged. For they more than once insinuate the mortality of the soul, or disseminate other errors that can lead to atheism. Thus says he.

### Robert’s Examination.

But Marin’s judgment should least of all be required for this matter—namely, whether books smell of atheism or not. For while he seems to hunt for the crimes of heresy and atheism in others, he himself many times, though unknowingly, plunges into the abyss of them, as we have clearly demonstrated in certain places expressed in my aforementioned book. Indeed, whatever he has in his own imagination is immediately decreed as atheism, heresy, or error by his own law. Nevertheless, when the matter is considered a little more accurately, truly wise men will perceive it to be far otherwise. 

For example, take that mark of atheism which he tries to brand upon me in this place, among others: *The books of atheism,* he says, *are those of Charron on Wisdom, Machiavelli on the Prince and Republic, Cardano on Subtlety and the Judgment of the Stars, Campanella, the Dialogues of Vanini, Fludd, and many others, etc.* Here, this "honest" author numbers my books among those of others in which, as he says, atheism is taught. But now he comes to the cause: why those books of Fludd, among others, seem to be stained with atheism. *Because,* he says, *they more than once insinuate the mortality of the soul, etc.* 

As for the others, I shall say nothing, nor is it my place to descend into the causes of others here. But on my own part, I confess that (in Treatise 1, Section 1, Book 8, Chapter 2 of the <term>Microcosm</term> <note>Fludd’s "History of the Microcosm" explores the human being as a miniature reflection of the universe.</note>, regarding the middle soul acting in the body) I said in express words that the soul can, in a certain way, be called mortal. I will here re-measure, word for word, what I wrote there:

### Text from Chapter 2 of the aforementioned place,

### How the middle soul is sometimes called immortal and sometimes mortal.

> *We spoke of the soul’s immortality in the last chapter of Book 4 of our Microcosm. Here, therefore, we have proposed to ourselves to deal with the reason for its mortality and immortality. The soul, indeed, is never truly said to die insofar as it is the image and likeness of the Creator; rather, it is the very spark of the Creator’s ray. Whence it is impossible that it be enclosed by the boundary of death, since, insofar as it is an act of God, it can never lose its life and being. However, according to a certain mobility of its own, by which it can become either better or worse, it is to be esteemed mortal. For it is thought to be subjected to death when it loses "living well," although even then it is called immortal insofar as it cannot lose "living miserably," etc. Thus I wrote.*

<sig>n 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: Hic (Here)</meta>

<summary>Robert Fludd defends himself against Marin Mersenne's accusation that his works promote atheism and the mortality of the soul. Fludd clarifies that when he called the soul "mortal," he was referring to spiritual or moral death ("losing the ability to live well") rather than the extinction of its essential divine essence.</summary>

->On the [Contest] of Wisdom with Folly<-

<margin>Ecclesiastes 1.</margin>
<margin>2 Peter 3:5.</margin>
<margin>Ecclesiastes 3.</margin>
<margin>Pimander 1. <note>Refers to the first book of the *Corpus Hermeticum*, attributed to Hermes Trismegistus.</note></margin>

Just as a bright emanation was produced from <term>Aleph</term> <note>The first letter of the Hebrew alphabet, which Fludd uses to represent the divine source of light and the beginning of creation.</note>: from the dark [source] or Alpha, and extends itself to Omega <note>The last letter of the Greek alphabet, representing the end or the most material part of creation.</note>—which is why it is called the "Bright Aleph," or the clear emanation and splendor of eternal Light—so indeed the ray of this emanation is like a string. Its source is the *Word of God in the highest*, who is the player of that string on the worldly <term>monochord</term> <note>A single-stringed instrument used by ancient and Renaissance philosophers to demonstrate the mathematical and musical ratios of the universe.</note>. He performs the will of the Father by means of that "bright Angel," so called because he is sent out first, before all things, for the creation of the world. Hence it is said: *By the Word of the Lord the heavens were established.* <note>original: "Verbo Domini firmati sunt cæli."</note> And elsewhere: *The heavens existed long ago and the earth was formed out of water and through water by the Word of God, etc.* <note>original: "Cæli prius & terra fuerunt ab aquis & per aquas consistens Verbo Dei."</note> Furthermore, the heavens and the earth which now exist have been stored up by the same Word. And elsewhere: *In the Word is life, and from Him, in Him, and through Him are all things.* <note>original: "in verbo est vita & ab eo, in eo, & per eum sunt omnia."</note> He, therefore, is the player of that string of the worldly monochord, whose middle point is the waters or the worldly Spirit; the instrument itself is the world. In the cavity [of the instrument], the sounds of that hidden Harmony are produced—sounds that are imperceptible [to the ears] but perceivable by their effects. The starting point from which this string proceeds by its vibration, giving life to the world, is from the Bright Source, namely from Aleph; the ending point toward which it tends is toward the source of the earth's darkness, namely the center point; and it circles back in Omega, according to the words of the Wise: *I, Wisdom, came out of the mouth of the Most High, and like a mist I covered the earth; I alone circled the vault of the heavens and walked in the depth of the abyss.* <note>original: "Ego Sapientia ex ore Altissimi prodij & tanquam nebula cooperui terram rotunditatem cælorum circuiui sola in profunditate abyssi ambulaui." A quote from the Book of Sirach (Ecclesiasticus) 24.</note> Here, then, we have the extension of the string constituting the Diameter of the single spherical world, made as it is in the image of the creator; for just as the Word is the image of God, so indeed the world is the image of the Word.

But truly, insofar as that unique and indivisible divine essence is divided into its three properties or various persons—the first of which is the source of life, for Christ, or the Word, or Wisdom, is called the *tree of life and the image of the Father* <note>original: "lignum vitæ & imago Patris."</note>—He is the source of all living things. To that extent, the source of the whole Harmony is the unique divinity, whose Harmony consists of a single and simple sound encompassing all consonances within itself: namely the <term>Diapason</term> <note>The Octave.</note>, the <term>Diapente</term> <note>The Fifth.</note>, and the <term>Diatesseron</term> <note>The Fourth.</note>. Therefore, the entire world is accustomed to echo and be filled by the arrangement of that sound, and it becomes a unity in the likeness of the unique creating Father. For by the striking of the entire string of the monochord, it emits a single but universal tone, and this integral striking of the string is the vivification of the one universal world. It is called a "unison," which is why the single world is called the image of the one God.

However, when this string is pressed down in the middle of the monochord, it is divided into two more perfect consonances which we call the Octave. Hence, we have asserted that the sphere passing through that point of division is the "orb of the middle soul," which we have also clearly enough demonstrated through the intersection made between our ascending material pyramid and that descending [pyramid of light]. This is why we called that sphere the "sphere of equality." And this was not done without consideration (even though this <term>Cynic</term> <note>A derogatory term Fludd uses for his opponent, Marin Mersenne, implying he is a "dog-like" or snarling critic.</note> author of ours speaks so superficially about my inverted and reversed pyramids in his text and judges them perversely). But truly, just as we have seen the Octave in every respect assimilated to the unison in its sound, standing in the ratio of 2 to 1, so indeed Wisdom, or the Word, or the Son in the Trinity, is the truest image and likeness of the Father. In the most perfect Harmony, He resonates with His Father—that is, He responds to His will—since without His mediation no one can approach the Father. *In Him was life* <note>original: "In eo erat vita."</note> (says Saint John), and this consonance was made by the pressing of the entire string of the monochord at its midpoint. From this it is clear that the entire string, resonating as a simple unison at the world's beginning—from which the whole world was forged before its division into regions—becomes in itself, and in secret, nothing other than the "Double Octave" <note>original: "disdiapason."</note> of God. Hence <term>Trismegistus</term> says that when <term>Pimander</term> revealed his face, all things were turned into a pleasant light. Once this unison was made in the world, it was necessary for other consonances to be produced for the perfection of the worldly symphony. Therefore, once the central division was made in the middle, it was necessary for a double Octave to be produced, creating a more perfect melody with the unison. Hence the emanation, or the Word, in which and from which there is life, is said to have placed a tabernacle in the heart of the heavens, or the midpoint of the monochordal string, from which place...

<summary>Fludd describes the creation of the universe as the tuning and playing of a "World Monochord" by the Divine Word, moving from the light of the Hebrew letter Aleph to the darkness of the center/Omega. He argues that the mathematical-musical ratios of the Octave (Diapason) represent the harmony between God and the created world, defending his "pyramid" diagrams against Mersenne's criticism.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s metaphysical defense of the "World Monochord." Having established the Word as the primary actor in creation, Fludd now explains how the musical intervals of the octave, fifth, and fourth correspond to the relationship between the Trinity, the Sun, and the human heart.</meta>

# On the Contest, Book III. 103

Whether by the Word or the Son himself in relation to the Father's manifestation, He is believed to strike the harmony of the <term>Diapason</term> <note>The interval of an octave, which Fludd considers the most perfect harmony, representing the union of extremes.</note>—both the higher and the lower, that is, of heaven and earth—no differently than the Father struck the entire fabric of the world in the manner of a <term>unison</term> <note>The fundamental note from which all intervals are measured; here representing God the Father as the source of all.</note>. And from this, it is said that the Diapason is the most exact offspring and image of the unison, just as the Son is of the Father. Finally, the power resulting from both these pulsations—namely, the Diapason of the higher and lower regions—is referred to the third person in the Trinity, proceeding from the Father and the Son: I mean that God-formed Wisdom by which and with which all things are formed, both in the heavenly and the earthly host. In the heavenly host, I say, it is by means of the Spiritual Diapason, which extends from the invisible, lucid fountain to that visible and solar fountain; in the earthly host, it is by means of that material Diapason, which extends from the visible sun down to the earth. 

And just as any harmony of the Diapason is composed of the <term>Diapente</term> <note>The musical interval of a fifth.</note>, which is a less perfect harmony, and the <term>Diatessaron</term> <note>The musical interval of a fourth.</note>, which is an imperfect harmony, so indeed we relate the proportion of the Spiritual Diapente to the super-celestial world, and the proportion of the Diatessaron to that part of heaven which lies between the solar tabernacle and the <term>primum mobile</term> <note>The "first moved" or outermost sphere of the heavens in the Ptolemaic system.</note>; and so it is in those harmonies contained within the natural Diapason. 

We see, therefore, the reason why the Sun is situated in the middle of the world-string, and the musical reason why Wisdom could place her tabernacle in the Sun. From this harmony—namely, the Diapason, which is the most perfect harmony—life rains down to the lower things and is cast like a javelin in every direction around the creatures by that Spirit which is the stream of life, gushing from the fountain of life called <term>En-soph</term> <note>A Kabbalistic term for the Infinite or the Divine Boundless, prior to any self-manifestation.</note>, who placed His Tabernacle in the Sun. 

Indeed, infinite mysteries are to be drawn from this kind of consideration: why the Sun is the heart of heaven; why the life of every creature is situated in its center or middle; why God is said to be the center of any thing whose circumference is nowhere; why virtue consists in the middle; how Wisdom was created before all things; how the incorruptible Spirit of God is in every thing; and how life comes from the Word, in whom alone life is present through the harmony of the first and highest perfection sent forth. Furthermore, we see how through the Diapente, contained within that most perfect harmony, the gifts of the <term>fifth essence</term> <note>The "quinta essentia" or ether, the celestial substance from which the heavens were thought to be made.</note>—namely, the rays of life—rain down, and through the Diatessaron the powers of the elements, of which the former lead primarily to the internal soul and the latter to its external part. We see also how the flow of life, beauty, and graces are infused into the sun from <term>Eloah</term> <note>A Hebrew name for God.</note> through <term>Tiphereth</term> <note>The Kabbalistic sphere (Sefirah) representing Beauty, often associated with the Sun and the balance of the universe.</note> of the Kabbalists, by means of the Spiritual Diapason: specifically, Angelic force through the spiritual Diapente, the power of the higher heaven through the Diatessaron, and other similar things which need not be recounted in this place.

From these things it is clear that the entire force and power of life consists in the Sun. For we, being taught, will find by ocular demonstration that this cannot be otherwise: namely, because when the middle or center of the string of an artificial <term>monochord</term> is pressed, we observe the Diapason to sound from both sides relative to its whole. Furthermore, we see life itself placed in the center of every thing, and that between two extremes it is necessary that there be a middle, which is the best of things; hence virtue or life is said to "consist in the middle." Is this not also clearly proven by the life-giving condition of that harmony consisting in the middle? For if a piece of straw is placed upon a single string of a lute, and another string tuned in unison with the one carrying the straw—or having itself in a double proportion <note>An octave.</note>—is struck, the straw will immediately jump up as if imbued [with life].

But this is also most excellently indicated in the constitution of man, who is said to be the most exact image of the world. For we find that the heart (as the holy page witnesses) is the seat of life; for with all watchfulness <margin>Proverbs ch. 23. <note>Fludd cites Ch. 23, but the quote is from Proverbs 4:23: "Keep your heart with all vigilance, for from it flow the springs of life."</note></margin>

> *keep your heart, because from it life proceeds*,

it is in the middle of the monochord of the <term>microcosm</term> <note>Man, viewed as a "little world" that mirrors the structure of the universe.</note>. For if you measure between the point of the member or pubic bone and the cranium or the crown of the head, you will find most exactly that the heart, situated under the left breast, occupies the middle of that space. Wherefore it is not possible that we should have a more exact example than this, since on account of the simili- <note>The text cuts off mid-word (similitudinem/similitude).</note>

<summary>Fludd explains the musical-theological structure of the universe, arguing that the Sun sits in the center of the "World Monochord" as a divine octave (Diapason), mirroring the human heart's central position in the body.</summary>

# On the Contest of Wisdom with Folly
<meta>catchword: and inciting</meta>

The similarity between man and the world is accepted by everyone; man is considered a "little world," hence the term <term>Microcosm</term> <note>From the Greek 'mikros' (small) and 'kosmos' (world), a central concept in Renaissance philosophy suggesting that the human body is a miniature map of the entire universe.</note>. Conversely, for the same reason, it is clearly indicated that the Sun is positioned in the middle of the heavens or the world, acting as its heart. Yet the reproductive parts in man are most directly related to the spheres of generation and fertility. For just as all the influences of life, generation, and vegetation descend from heaven to the earth—as if into a common womb—so too do all the generative influences of the entire "lesser world" (man) descend into the seminal vessels. They are retained in those spongy glands called the <term>Parastatae</term> <note>An archaic term for the epididymis or associated structures of the reproductive system.</note> until the act of emission. This is demonstrated by the fact that even when the testicles are removed, as happens to eunuchs, they can nevertheless be moved toward the act of generation one last time. 

Therefore, according to the preceding "world-string," judgment must be made at every point regarding human harmony. This is quite clearly explained in the illustration at the end of *History of the Physics of the Microcosm*, Book 13, Chapter 5, where all these proportions in man are expressed. But to refer you to an even more exact demonstration, I would like you to look closely at our "World Monochord." In it, you have all these things described with precision: namely, the geometric proportions of the harmony of the whole world. On its right side, it depicts similar proportions found in the letters of the <note>original: "Tetragrammatis יהוה"</note> <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH, which Fludd integrates into his musical and mathematical diagrams.</note>, in the human spirit, and even in the Ark of the Covenant and the Temple of Solomon with all its regions. On its left side, all the same arithmetic proportions are described according to the most exact intentions of Plato and Proclus. Although a model of that Monochord is expressed in the second book of this work, as well as in our "pyramids," for a better exposition of it and an explanation of the mysteries therein, I would refer you to the treatise on the "Symphonic World Monochord" at the end of our volume titled *The Amphitheater of Anatomy*. I also refer <note>original: "Marinum"</note> <term>Mersenne</term> <note>Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), a French mathematician and priest who was Fludd’s primary intellectual rival and a harsh critic of his "occult" harmonies.</note> to that same work, so he may see if he can find any error in it, and let him show, if he is strong enough, in what way our Harmony differs from that of Plato.

<margin>Text.</margin> These things having been considered, it will be evident to you, Readers—unless I am mistaken—to judge how unjustly and with what poor understanding our author has spoken here. He said in the preceding text: *How poorly Fludd understands the Platonic doctrine which he seems to follow, etc.* On the contrary, from his own words and writings, I am certain that he is far from the true intention of Plato and the other true philosophers. Among the Wise, there is only one true intention, whether it is expressed in the <term>Kabbalistic</term> manner, or the Philosophical, Magical, Chemical, or finally the Theosophical way. This is that "Highest Good" of the philosophers, the <term>Shining Aleph</term> <note>The first letter of the Hebrew alphabet, symbolizing the divine source or the unity of God.</note> of the Kabbalists, the Word, Wisdom, and the Christ of the theologians, who, by the testimony of the Apostle, *is all in* <margin>Col. 3</margin> *all*. He is the true Philosophers' Stone of the learned and wise Alchemists, and that musical "consonance of life," the <term>Diapason</term> <note>An octave; for Fludd, the symbol of perfect reconciliation between opposites.</note> of true, formal, and non-spurious musicians. This harmony is the whole, and through the whole; the Alpha and Omega, filling all things, comprehending all things, making all things, since without it there is nothing. For by the peaceful and most harmonious blood of Christ, God has reconciled all things, whether <margin>Col 1. Heb. 1.</margin> *those which are in heaven, or those which dwell on earth*, as the Apostle testifies. For she [Wisdom/Harmony] carries all things by the word of her truth and made peace between those two opposites in both worlds: heaven and earth, soul and body. By means of this admirable Harmony, He has made and will make both into one; He destroyed the middle wall of the fence, the enmities between the flesh and the law, nullifying the commandments in the decrees. He created the two, I say, in himself into <margin>Eph. 2.</margin> one new man, making peace with God. I say He reconciled both—namely, the external and internal man—in one body. And so He himself shall be *all in* <margin>Coloss. 3. Heb. 1.</margin> *all: the brightness of glory and the figure of His beauty*. He is one; and just as He departed from the Father (who is Unity in the beginning) in the number two, descending through the most perfect consonance of Harmony from the shining fountain, founding the earth, making...

He defends his mystical philosophy against Marin Mersenne, asserting that true wisdom—whether called Alchemy, Kabbalah, or Christianity—is the same universal harmony found in Christ.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s explanation of the "World Monochord." Having previously discussed how musical intervals represent the Trinity and the celestial spheres, he now applies these proportions to the human body (the Microcosm) and defends his interpretation of Platonic and Kabbalistic philosophy against his critic, Marin Mersenne.</meta>

# On the Contest of Wisdom with Folly ##

[...simi]litude between man and the world, man is held by all to be a little world, whence he is called the <term>Microcosm</term> <note>From the Greek 'mikros' (small) and 'kosmos' (world), the idea that the human body reflects the structure of the entire universe.</note>; and conversely, by the same reasoning, it is clearly indicated that the Sun is in the middle of the sky or world, placed there just like its heart. But the reproductive organs in man, being the sphere of generation and fertility, are most rapidly compared [to the earth]. For just as all influences of life, generation, and vegetation descend from heaven into the earth, as if into a common womb, so also all the generative influences of the entire lesser world descend into the seminal vessels and are terminated and retained in those spongy glands called the <term>Parastatae</term> <note>original: "Parastatæ"; an early anatomical term for the epididymis or prostate</note> until the act of emission. This is demonstrated by the fact that once the testicles are removed, as happens to Eunuchs, they can nonetheless be moved to the act of generation one last time. 

Whence, according to the preceding "worldly string," judgment must be made at every point in the human Harmony, just as it is in the figure at the *end of book 13, chapter 5 of the Physical History of the Microcosm*, in which all these proportions in man are expressed and explained clearly enough. But to refer you to an even more exact demonstration, I would wish for you to look closely at our "World Monochord," for in it you have all these things described with precision: namely, the geometric proportions of the harmony of the entire world. On its right side, it depicts likenesses of itself both in the letters of the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>original: "יהוה"; the sacred four-letter Hebrew name of God, often transliterated as YHWH</note> and in the human Spirit, as well as in the ark of the testimony and the temple of Solomon with all its regions. On its left side, all the arithmetic proportions of the same are described according to the most exact intentions of Plato and Proclus. And although a model of that Monochord is expressed in the second book of this work, and also our "pyramids" [of light and darkness], nevertheless, for a better exposition of it and an explanation of the mysteries within it, I would like you to be referred to the treatise on the *Symphonic World Monochord* at the tail end of that volume of ours entitled *The Amphitheatre of Anatomy*. I also remit <term>Marinus</term> <note>Refers to Fludd's intellectual rival, Marin Mersenne</note> to that same work, so that he may see if he can find any error in it, and let him show, if he is strong enough, in what way this Harmony of ours differs from that of Plato.

<margin>Text.</margin>
Therefore, having considered these things, it will be evident to you, Readers—unless I am mistaken—to judge how unjustly this author of ours has spoken, and with how little understanding, in what he said in the preceding text, namely: *How poorly Fludd understands the Platonic doctrine which he seems to follow, etc.* On the contrary, from his own words and writings, I am certain that he himself is far from the true intention of Plato and the other true Philosophers. Among the Wise, there is only one such intention, whether it be expressed in the Kabbalistic manner, or the Philosophical and Magical, or the Chemical, or finally the Theosophical. And this is that "highest good" of the Philosophers, the bright <term>Aleph</term> <note>The first letter of the Hebrew alphabet, representing unity and the divine source</note> of the Kabbalists; <margin>Colossians 3</margin> the Word, and Wisdom, and the Christ of the Theologians, who, as the Apostle witnesses, *is all in all*; the true Philosophers' Stone of the learned and wise Alchemists; and that musical consonance of the life-giving <term>Diapason</term> <note>An octave</note> of the true and formal (not the counterfeit) musicians. This harmony is the whole, and through the whole, the <term>Alpha and Omega</term> <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, signifying the beginning and the end</note> filling all things, comprehending all things, making all things, as that without which nothing exists. For by the peaceful and most harmonious blood of Christ, God reconciled all things to himself, whether <margin>Colossians 1, Hebrews 1</margin> *those which are in heaven, or those which dwell on the earth*, as the Apostle witnesses. For she [Wisdom/Harmony] carries all things by the word of her truth, and made peace between those two opposites in both worlds: heaven and earth, soul and body. This she did by means of an admirable Harmony, through which he has made and will make both one; <margin>Ephesians 2</margin> he dissolved the middle wall of the partition, the enmities between the flesh and the law, voiding the commandments in decrees; he created the two, I say, in himself into one new man, making peace with God. I say, he reconciled both—namely, the external and internal man—into one body, and so he himself shall be all in all: <margin>Colossians 3, Hebrews 1</margin> *the brightness of his glory and the figure of his beauty*, who is one. Thus, as he himself departed from the Father in the number two (who is unity in the beginning), descending through the most perfect consonance of Harmony from the fountain of light, founding the earth, and making...

He defends his "World Monochord" as a synthesis of Platonic, Kabbalistic, and Christian truths, identifying the ultimate harmony with Christ/Wisdom.</summary>

### On the Contest, Book III.

<margin>Ephesians 4</margin>
<margin>He goes to God</margin>

...and establishing the heavens, ordering the waters, and weighing the air in measure, turning the heavens in a circuit, and bringing forth all virtues in the world, both great and small, animating and giving them life in a wondrous way. So also through that same Harmony, he <note>Referring to the Creator or the Word.</note> ascends to the unity from which he emerged, completing the perfection of the Trinity. By this reasoning, he not only fills all things with his Harmony but also sustains them. Hence the Apostle says: *Christ descended, and it is he who ascended above all heavens, so that he might fill all things.* And elsewhere: *Christ came forth from God,* and from there he is the *Alpha and Omega* <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing the beginning and the end.</note>. And thus it is that wherever he is present in his perfect Harmony, there also life and goodness are present.

From these things, it is clear that although this Wisdom is unique and simple in itself, it is nevertheless called "manifold" with respect to the creatures it informs, as Solomon testifies, and as the Apostle also says: *Christ is one body, but having many members,* inasmuch as in him alone, and in no other, is true life. For as Saint John testifies, *Christ is the only truth*; the Spirit testifies to this, and therefore no life or existence of a creature consists of anything except this truth, nor is anything real unless it is this truth. Therefore the Apostle says: *In the beginning, Christ founded the earth, and the heavens are the works of his hands; they shall perish, but he will remain, and his years shall not fail.* This, therefore, is that only tested, chosen, and precious <term>cornerstone</term> <note>original: "lapis angularis." Fludd uses this architectural metaphor to bridge Christology with his geometric theories of the world's structure.</note> which the false builders rejected—a stone of stumbling and a rock of offense to those who stumble at the word and do not believe, in which they are placed. This is that spiritual stone which the Apostle calls Christ. Saint John and the Apostle admonish us to build a spiritual house upon this stone, Christ, as our foundation, just as we are "living stones." For without this luminous foundation, nothing can stand or resonate in a harmonic tone; instead, they will be suddenly reduced to nothingness by the original discord of darkness. 

Hence it is said of the <term>Macrocosm</term> <note>The "Great World" or the universe.</note>: *Upon what are the bases of the earth fastened?* Or, *Who laid its cornerstone,* by which the measures of the earth are arranged, and the line is stretched over it, its foundations are placed, and it is hung upon nothing? Is not this same stone required for the existence of the other members of the world? For this spiritual stone is the foundation of the solar creature, just as the soul is to the body. Indeed, the incorruptible Spirit—which is the "true gold" to be bought from Christ—is in all things. Even into the <term>Microcosm</term> <note>The "Little World," meaning Man.</note>, this cornerstone has been cast as a foundation; thus, speaking of the internal man, the Prophet says: *Look to the rock from which you were hewn.* And for this reason, man is called the "temple of the Holy Spirit," and the "Tabernacle of Christ," and Christ is the "Word and the light of men."

We conclude, therefore, that the entire Harmony in both worlds proceeds from the Word, from Wisdom, from Jesus Christ, and from the power of the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note>, who is the true cornerstone of every thing in the world. In him, as the firm foundation of any thing, life and being are established. We say that his "semi-conical tones" <note>original: "toni semiconij." This refers to Fludd's famous diagrams where two cones or pyramids—one of light/spirit and one of darkness/matter—interpenetrate to create the musical intervals of existence.</note> are the various degrees of his immersion into the multitude of matter. And although this true, essential light is unique, simple, and remains within itself, it is seen to work in various ways in the world, according to the greater or lesser appetite for receiving or resistance in suffering of the species or individual it informs; but especially according to the arrangement of the composition and the proportion of that divine light assigned to it by God in its specific creation or generation. For just as common fire, though it is one and the same in its nature, does not harm a dense subject, yet nevertheless devours another, thins another, produces a great noise in another, and even by its action raises one thing higher and depresses another, and works in infinite other ways in this world—so must we judge concerning the "Fire of fires," the "Stone of stones," and him who is "all in all."

You see here, (Just Readers), how "unfortunate" <note>Fludd is likely being sarcastic here, using a critic's insult (*infelix*) against them.</note> the foundation of my Harmony of both worlds is, and whether it was "unsuccessfully" produced (as he says): the foundation of the world, I say, namely the Word, Christ, and Wisdom. You also have the most perfect <term>Diapason</term> <note>original: "Diapason," the musical interval of an octave, which Fludd sees as the fundamental unit of cosmic harmony.</note> of concord, or the most perfect outflow of emanation into the world, sent forth like an Angel from the mouth of the Almighty, from which all things in the world...

...[in]citing and strengthening the heavens, adapting the waters, and weighing the air in measure, turning the heavens in a circuit, and bringing forth all powers in the world, both great and small, and animating and quickening them in a marvelous way; so also through the same Harmony it ascends to the unity from which it issued, making the perfection of the Trinity, and by this reasoning his Harmony not only fills all things, but also sustains them. Hence the Apostle says, *Christ descended, and it is he who ascended above all the heavens, that he might fill all things.* <note>Ephesians 4:10</note> And elsewhere, *Christ went out from God, and goes to God, whence he is both Alpha and Omega.* And hence it is that where Wisdom herself is present in her perfect Harmony, there also life and goodness are present. From these things it is clear that although this Wisdom is unique and simple in itself, it is nevertheless called "manifold" with respect to the creatures it informs, as Solomon testifies, whence also the Apostle says: *Christ is one body, but having many members, as in him alone and no other is true life.* For as St. John testifies, *Christ is the only truth*; the Spirit testifies to this, therefore no life or existence of a creature consists except in truth alone, nor is anything truly real, except this truth. Wherefore the Apostle says: *Christ in the beginning founded the earth, and the works of his hands are the heavens; they shall perish, but he shall remain, and his years shall not fail.* <note>Hebrews 1:10-12</note> This, therefore, is that single *tested, chosen, and precious cornerstone, which the spurious builders rejected, a stone of stumbling and a rock of offense to those who stumble at the word and do not believe, in which they are placed; that Spiritual stone which the Apostle calls Christ.* <note>1 Peter 2:6-8</note> To which stone, Christ, as a foundation, St. John and the Apostle advise us to build up a spiritual house as living stones. For without this luminous foundation, nothing can stand or resonate in a Harmonic tone: but by the original discord of darkness they will be suddenly reduced to nothing. Hence it is said of the <term>Macrocosm</term> <note>The "Great World" or the universe at large.</note>: *Upon what are the bases of the earth solidified? Or who let down its cornerstone by which the measures of the earth are arranged, and the line is stretched over it, its foundations are placed, and it is hung over nothing?* <note>Job 38:6 and Job 26:7</note> Is not the same stone required for the existence of the rest of the world’s members? For this Spiritual stone is the foundation of the solar creature, just as the soul is to its body. Indeed, the incorruptible Spirit, which is the true gold to be bought from Christ, is in all things. Even into the <term>Microcosm</term> <note>The "Little World," referring to man as a reflection of the universe.</note> this cornerstone has been cast for a foundation, wherefore speaking of the internal man the Prophet says: *look to the rock from which you were hewn.* <note>Isaiah 51:1</note> And hence man is called the temple of the Holy Spirit, and the Tabernacle of Christ, and Christ—or the Word—is the light of men.

We conclude, therefore, that the entire Harmony in both worlds <note>The Macrocosm and the Microcosm.</note> proceeds from the Word, from Wisdom, from Jesus Christ, and from the power of the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The sacred four-letter Hebrew name of God (YHWH), which Fludd associates with the fundamental creative power.</note>, who is the true cornerstone of every thing in the world, in whom, as a firm foundation of any thing, life and being are founded. And we say that his "semiconian" tones <note>original: "toni semiconij." These represent the "semitones" or specific intervals of divine light as it descends into the density of matter.</note> are the various degrees of its immersion in the multitude of matter. And although this true essential light is unique and simple, remaining in itself, yet it is seen to operate variously in the world, according to the greater or lesser appetite for receiving or resistance in suffering of the species or individual it informs, but especially according to the arrangement of the composition and the proportion of that divine light assigned to it by God in its specific creation or generation. For just as common fire, though it is the same and constant in its nature, does not harm a certain thick subject, yet nonetheless devours another, rarefies another, gives out a great noise in another, and also by its action raises one higher, depresses another more, and operates in infinite other ways in this world; so indeed should it be judged regarding the Fire of fires, and the Stone of stones, and regarding this "all in all."

You see here, (just Readers) how happy the foundation of my Harmony of both worlds is, and whether it was unsuccessfully produced (as he himself says)—the foundation of the world, I say: namely, the Word, Christ, Wisdom. You also have the most perfect <term>Diapason</term> <note>The musical interval of an octave, symbolizing the complete cycle of divine emanation.</note> of harmony, or the most perfect outflowing of the emanation of all things into the world, sent forth like an Angel from the mouth of the Almighty; from which all things in the world...

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s vigorous defense of his "World Monochord" and his "History of the Microcosm" against the criticisms of the French mathematician and philosopher Marin Mersenne (referred to here as Marini). Fludd transitions from describing the divine nature of the Word to defending his specific use of musical and mathematical symbols—like his famous interlocking pyramids—to explain the relationship between God and the physical world.</meta>

# On the Contest of Wisdom with Folly

With infallible harmony and proportion, though it is least of all heard by our ears, [the universe] echoes and resounds. And if, according to that contemptuous speech of Marini, you do not wish to buy the history of my Microcosm <note>The "Microcosm" refers to Fludd’s massive work *Utriusque Cosmi Historia*, where he argues that the human body and soul are a miniature reflection of the entire universe.</note> even for a *titivilitium* <note>original: "titiuilitio"; a Latin term for a scrap of wool, meaning something of absolutely no value.</note>, O Readers—in which work I have, as he dreams, more than sufficiently betrayed my ignorance with my inverted and reverted pyramids—then let your will be done in this matter. But truly, I am certain that the things I have written there were produced according to the pattern of truth, even if they do not appear so clearly to everyone. Truly, I have brought these writings into the light with a good effort, primarily for my God, and then afterwards so that they might please your judgment. Therefore, I am resolved, and led by a firm conscience, that everyone should use their own judgment, whether in choosing or rejecting. For this shall be for me: one who has published his works to the public neither led by vain praise, nor nourished by self-love <note>original: "philautia"; a Greek term for excessive self-love or conceit.</note>, nor elevated by Marinian ambition.

<margin>
Hebrews 1:2.
Colossians 2.
Ephesians 2:14.
Colossians 3.
1 John 5.
</margin>
But I have done so because I wished to trumpet the truth to the world and make it known for a certainty to all people, along with the Apostle: That spiritual Christ, or Wisdom, is the brightness of God’s glory and the express image of his substance. And that he himself bears all things by the word of his power, and that in him are hidden all the treasures of Wisdom and knowledge. And that all things exist in him, from him, and through him. And that he is our peace and the Harmony of the world; indeed, he is all in all and the only truth, to whom be praise and glory forever. For his glory alone, I say, and for his honor have I written. And I am certain and secure in this: that although the imaginations of men are uncertain and varied, nevertheless Wisdom, which is truth itself, shall enjoy forever the trophy that she herself has fixed.

---

### Text by Marini. 1626.

> *As if indeed David, striking his harp, moved some celestial ranks and intelligences or divine properties. Truly, I relegate these imaginations to the Mancavists, Hechmanists, Zeruphant-Cabalists, and such merchants of trifles <note>original: "nugiuendulos"; a derogatory term for charlatans or street-hawkers of worthless goods.</note>, among whom Fludd seems to be; for he not only devises the names and orders of geniuses and Angels, but also applies himself to mundane, celestial, and human music, and involves it in fluctuating darkness.*

### Robert’s Examination.

And why should he not primarily move the celestial ranks, since there are no other degrees of Harmony known in the world among men or spirits than the <term>Octave</term> <note>original: "Diapason"</note>, <term>Fifth</term> <note>original: "Diapente"</note>, and <term>Fourth</term> <note>original: "Diatessaron"</note>, although some also add a sixth chord? Have you, out of all your Harmony—whether Greek or Hebrew—understood more degrees in the art of Music besides these same simple ones, or those multiplied within themselves (whereby the 10th sounds the same as the 3rd, and the 17th as the 10th, etc.)? Or do you think there are some symphonic consonances in art, and others in nature? 

But Pythagoras perceived the arithmetic consonances from a variety of natural weights, and Iamblichus <note>A Neoplatonist philosopher who wrote on the mysteries and the soul.</note> acknowledges that the soul is snatched away by the sound of music because it seems to remember having heard similar proportions in its homeland from which it was derived. Does not like rejoice and take delight in its like by a natural inclination? Thus we see the magnet attract iron by a wonderful Harmony of love. And the Sages, taught by experience, also knew that similar things in the lower realms attract and entice similar influences to themselves from the higher realms through symphonic appetite and wonderful love. 

Why then should the concord of heaven not be moved by its like on earth, when we observe in lower things that a string of one nature in one musical instrument, by vibrating through a very great symphony, acts upon a string of another, so that when only one is struck, the other seems to feel it by a marvelous kind of life? Have we not learned from the Wise Man that there is the greatest Harmony among the elements? *While the elements are changed among themselves,* he says, *as the sound of a quality is changed in an organ, yet all things keep their sound.* Furthermore, even if the evil and antipathetic spirit in Saul was calmed at the sounds of David’s harp, why should the good spirits in heaven not be moved, who by natural instinct delight in concord and Harmony? That evil spirits wait for the influences of heaven and are strengthened in their malice by them is proven by the falling sickness <note>original: "epilepsia"</note>, which for that reason is called the disease...

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s heated defense against the French philosopher Marin Mersenne. Having previously discussed the "World Monochord" and his symbolic pyramids, Fludd now addresses Mersenne’s accusations of "trifling" regarding the study of angels and the art of chiromancy (palmistry), while explaining his method of synthesizing human knowledge into his "History of the Macrocosm and Microcosm."</meta>

# On the Contest, Book III.

...the Sacred Page <note>A traditional term for the Bible.</note> calls it the "Lunar disease" <note>This refers to epilepsy or mental illness, which historically was believed to be influenced by the phases of the moon.</note>, insofar as it vexes the patient more during the full and new moon. Hence it is said that the malignant spirit left its possession when Christ rebuked it. Why then should good Spirits, who receive their strength from heaven, not do the same? But now at last he reaches Fludd, always greeting him with the same song of malice: "*I read*," he says, "*such peddlers of trifles* <note>original: "nugiuendulos"</note> *as Fludd seems to be, while he devises not only the names and orders of geniuses and Angels, etc.*" Good God! How many times has this little brother <note>original: "fraterculus"; a disparaging reference to Mersenne’s status as a monk of the Minim order.</note> rolled this same stone—as if, lacking a subject of his own to write about, he implores all those Angels and Demons listed elsewhere by me to his own aid with many repeated outcries? But he himself says that the tones and sounds of David's Harp could not move the levels and intelligences of heaven. How thoughtlessly, then, does he produce these discordant tones of his malevolence to move my spirit and intelligence, if there were no power in sounds or tones? Or why should anyone pour out hymns, or songs—indeed, even prayers—either to the saints or to God himself, if there were no harmonic communication <note>original: "commercium"; implying a reciprocal exchange or connection.</note> between the human mind, its Spirit, and the Angels and saints of heaven? As for the further quibbles he makes against my worldly and human Harmony: since we have, as I hope, said enough in the preceding words, we shall pass over them in silence in this place.

---

### ->C H A P T E R  I V.<-

->*Through which it may be seen with what violence Marin pursues Robert in the investigation of the science of Chiromancy <note>Chiromancy: the practice of palmistry or interpreting the lines of the hand to understand a person's nature or fate.</note>: He challenges him to resolve a question in Chiromancy, though not without the greatest insults.*<-

->*Text of Marin, Column 1744.<-

Therefore, that Robert Fludd seems to me to rave like a heretical Magus, when he says the hand is a table of the person’s birth and of nature, in which the mysteries of birth are carved and outlined in a secret manner; and that fatal things are reported by those characters and similar signs formed by nature, in both good and evil, and are clearly revealed to the learned, and thus instructions from the higher powers are made evident by a super-celestial sharpness.

### ->Robert’s Examination.<-

Most welcome Readers, I pray you attend to those things which must now be said in brief. I would wish, if it pleases you, that you consider with a slightly more mature gaze that our entire effort in writing the technical history of both the Macrocosm <note>The "Macrocosm" refers to the universe as a whole, the "great world."</note> and the Microcosm <note>The "Microcosm" refers to the "little world," the human being, viewed as a reflection of the entire universe.</note> was to reveal to the world in a few words, and in an abbreviated manner, those arts which are frequently used among men. For example, the arts commonly practiced in and around the Macrocosm are **Arithmetic**, **Music**, **Geometry**, **Optics**, **Painting**, **Clock-making**, **Warfare**, **On Motion**, **Geography**, **Astrology**, and **Geomancy** <note>Geomancy: a traditional method of divination using patterns of earth, stones, or dots.</note>. We have written about these succinctly, not as if they were invented by us, but insofar as they are held in public use among worldly people. Although I confess that I have added some things proven by my own experience and very little known to the common people. For a similar reason, in the technical *History of the Microcosm*, I have described all the sciences pertaining to man—as far as they are held in estimation by the world—briefly and succinctly. I did this not to establish their certainty in any other way than to recite them briefly as we received them from authors, whether printed or in manuscript, and all the more because we perceived much certainty and utility to be inherent in them.

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s aggressive rebuttal of Marin Mersenne. Having previously defended his theories on the "World Monochord" and spiritual harmonies, Fludd now turns to the "lesser" sciences like palmistry and astrology, which Mersenne has used to label him a practitioner of magic.</meta>

# On the Contest of Wisdom with Folly

Now, these are the arts of that kind: namely, the Prophetic art, the interpretation of dreams, mystic Geomancy <note>A method of divination that interprets markings on the ground or patterns formed by tossed handfuls of soil/rocks.</note>, the art of memory, Astrology <note>original: "Genethlialogia"; specifically the casting of birth charts.</note>, Physiognomy <note>The practice of assessing a person's character from their outer appearance, especially the face.</note>, and Palmistry. I have, I say, expressed and numbered these sciences among those pertaining to man, just as I did the preceding ones which are familiar to the world and pertain to the world itself. Here, I pray you, observe the disposition of this diligent and troublesome man: having omitted all other sciences of greater weight, or at least barking at them like a dog—knowing only how to bark but not how to bite—he has arrived at a more irrational science, one of weaker value in the common view. He proceeds to command me like a Master, ordering that I predict his fortune from a hand; and as my reward, at the very front of his discourse, he greets me with the title of *heretic-mage*. But we shall soon show what the difference is between me, the *Heretic*, and him, the *Catholic* after his own fashion.

---

### 2. Mersenne’s Text. Cologne, 1744.

> *However, to say something in passing, and lest many authors of disapproved readings impose themselves upon others, I cast an objection at Robert Fludd, Taisnier, and other Palmists <note>original: "Chiroscopis"</note> which they can never dissolve. Yet, unless it is entirely dissolved, Palmistry must be rejected and held as nothing more than a fabulous little tale. Leaving others aside because they have passed away, I challenge that Robert, that armored soldier <note>original: "cataphractarium"; likely mocking Fludd’s perceived arrogance or his status as a "soldier" of his own philosophy.</note> in his own judgment, who believes (or at least pretends) that he is <term>universally</term> <note>original: "καθόλου" (katholou); Mersenne uses the Greek word for "universal" to mock Fludd's claim to all-encompassing knowledge, while also punning on the word "Catholic."</note> learned. I challenge that Fludd, I say, with all his "waves" <note>A pun on Fludd’s name (Latin "fluctibus").</note> and his formal and material pyramids, and with his soul of the world, to pronounce what has happened to that man, what is happening with him now, and what is to happen, whose hands and lines these are. These lines were not made at a whim, but were expressed to the life by an excellent painter accurately looking upon the true, living hands themselves, exactly as they truly are. And lest you perhaps complain, I offer not only the right hand of that God-taught friend of mine, but also the left; and if you reject the right, let the left suffice for you.*

---

## Robert’s Examination.

You object, you say, to Robert Fludd something he can never dissolve; and what is it that you desire to object to Fludd the "armored soldier," a soldier (as you say) in his own judgment? To Robert, I say, whom you claim either believes or pretends that he is <term>universally</term> <note>original: "καθόλου"</note> learned? But Robert confesses that he has never believed nor pretended such a thing about himself—to be learned in the <term>universal</term> arts. But because perhaps someone among your own people has, out of their own grace and not by my merit, judged me so, from this arises your envy toward me: a vain, I say, and useless malice. Is it Fludd whom you provoke with all his "waves" and his formal and material pyramids, and with his soul of the world? But Fludd wonders that you have not been able to bring forth even one reason of validity against his pyramids and his world-soul, as is recorded to your disgrace for all time from the preceding arguments. And yet you believe and pretend that you are that armored soldier in your own judgment, the one who should be called <term>universally</term> learned.

But you provoke Fludd, you say: to do what? To predict the fortune of a ridiculous fool. And what is the reward for this stupid service? Certainly, the title of *heretic-mage*. Excellent! And do you think you shall escape unpunished in this way? You have filled the page—O, vainer than vanity itself!—with vain and ridiculous pictures of deformed hands, and perhaps you seek the fortune of their master; a question for which, if no solution is made, you have decreed that all Palmistry must be abolished. Let it be so. But what then? Do you believe that Fludd, if he wished to undertake this empty business, would satisfy you in this matter—you, who have afflicted him with such great insults? Let Fludd perish in his own "waves" if he should so lightly throw himself into the impetuous "Marine" waves <note>Another pun, this time on Mersenne’s first name, "Marin" (Latin: "Marinus," meaning "of the sea").</note> of a Marine impostor, merely to satisfy the appetite of that man's envy. Furthermore, if it pleased Robert to labor at such a business and successfully bring something forth: would not *Marinus* proclaim to the world, according to the subject of his following text, that Fludd had done so by the power...

Fludd mocks Mersenne’s hostility and explains that even a successful prediction would only result in further accusations of heresy or magic.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s aggressive rebuttal of Marin Mersenne. Having previously defended his theories on the "World Monochord" and spiritual harmonies, Fludd now turns to the "lesser" sciences like palmistry and astrology, which Mersenne has used to label him a practitioner of magic.</meta>

...[he claimed] that he performed that through the protection of his Demons and not by the art of Palmistry, and by this reasoning, Robert would unexpectedly give Marin a just occasion for shouting out. But in truth, I know, and many others besides me who are taught by experience in the observation of hands, acknowledge—despite the profane things Marin brings forth in praise of such an art—that things worthy of admiration have been and long since were predicted through it. But in this, that caviller <note>A person who raises trivial or annoying objections.</note> seems to be very superficial in the matter of Palmistry <note>original: "Chiroscopica"</note>, inasmuch as he seems to ignore that the primary judgment in the hands consists in the vivid color of the lines, since in this art, the paleness of the lines signifies one thing, while their vivid and ruddy appearance signifies another. If, therefore, he had also sent the blood of the subject along with the hands, the resolution of the question would indeed be more probable and certain. Nevertheless, for the confirmation of this science, it will not be difficult to bring to light even the testimony of the Holy Scriptures, if there were need; and we say that this art exists on equal footing with Physiognomy <note>The practice of judging character from physical features.</note>, about whose praises authors have written abundantly.

### 3. Mersenne’s Text. Cologne, in the same place.

*I omit almost infinite things, which you chatter about with those insane people like yourself, which—unless you renounce them—I know not with what Hellebore <note>A plant used in antiquity to treat madness.</note> Hippocrates or even your Paracelsus could cure you. But if you have anything to object or respond to us, I admit nothing until you have unfolded those hands, and have made conjectures about each individual line, or at least some of them according to your principles. So that you may better achieve this, call upon your Astrological and vital Palmistry, or rather your Genius or Demon (for you teach the method of calling him forth), whom even a child still suckling or babbling would mock; add if you wish your Geomancy, ecstasy, and prophetic Oracle. For whatever you finally do, I know you will say nothing truly.*

## Robert’s Examination.

I feel, Marin, that your nature is so violent, insane, and pestiferous, that in the manner of a rabid dog you scatter your foamy saliva upon everyone, and especially upon me, without any respect. But why, I pray, do I "rave with my like"? Or with what insanity do you find me so struggling that Hellebore is required for my cure? What? Have you ever even touched my pulse? Fie, you sham physician <note>original: "spurie medice"</note>, who vainly boast to profess an art entirely unknown to you! But I, who am truly a physician, have been justly forced to fall upon this judgment concerning you: namely, that it is not so difficult to say whether you are truly intoxicated with Lycanthropy <note>The delusion of being a wolf; here used as a metaphor for his rabid behavior.</note> or not. For throughout your whole volume, you seem to rage and babble against me and other honest men in a rabid manner, as if you were seized by a delirium. 

But just now, the reason for this matter seems to come to my mind: perhaps you have been enchanted by the power of that Circean mandrake root called *Morion* <note>Historically identified as Mandragora officinalis or a similar narcotic plant; "Circean" refers to the enchantress Circe.</note>, since a single drachm of it taken in a drink (as you yourself admit) induces madness. For not only in this place, but also almost everywhere through your whole volume, you have conducted yourself with an insane logic and gesture toward men who ought to be placed many degrees above you in both doctrine and character: which indeed is least becoming to a Monastic habit <note>Mersenne was a Minim friar; Fludd is pointing out that his aggressive behavior does not match his religious vows.</note>. I speak of others; for as far as I am concerned, I do not care a straw for your cavils or quarrels, since I feel myself strong enough to repel all your empty injuries. Therefore, I willingly forgive that notable incivility and insolence of yours, praying to God himself that He may convert you into something better—that is, that He may grant you better manners and a more civil or less barbaric condition out of His grace. Indeed, may He inspire you with a deeper Spirit in true Philosophy, so that you might use more solid and sharp reasons and arguments in your refutations and disputes, or at least, may He plug that harsh mouth of yours with the mud of Wisdom, or at least equip the edge of your pen with greater moderation. These things I certainly [wish] faithfully for you, moved not by any malice, but by mere piety.

<sig>o 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: exopto: (I strongly desire)</meta>
