<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s aggressive rebuttal of Marin Mersenne. Having previously defended his theories on the "World Monochord" and spiritual harmonies, Fludd now turns to the "lesser" sciences like palmistry and astrology, which Mersenne has used to label him a practitioner of magic.</meta>

# Of Wisdom with Folly

I long for it: for although your malice toward me is remarkable, which urges you so many times to roll the stone of slander regarding evil magic <note>original: "cacomagia"; the practice of harmful or "black" magic</note> against me; whence also in this text you seem to recount and make known the names of your fictitious, and I know not which, Demons; nevertheless, I, led by a good conscience against those fictions of yours, am fortified as if by a brass wall <note>A classical reference to an unbreakable defense of one's character</note>, and protected on every side (thanks be to God) by the shield of innocence.

### 4. Mersenne’s Text. Column 1744.

> *Take care, however, lest you wander in any way in making conjectures and offering judgment concerning those hands and concerning the one to whom they belong in this art of Palmistry <note>original: "Chiroscopica"; the study of character or fortune through the lines of the hand</note>. For he will remarkably catch your errors and publish them to the whole world, if the life he enjoys still survives.*

## Robert’s Examination.

But what indeed is that horrendous monster, and what kind of man is he among yours, who is powerful enough to strike Robert with such dread or terror that he should guard himself against the danger of him? You are deceived, Marin; for you and the rest of your ilk, however malicious, will perceive that Robert rejects words of that sort, however high-sounding; they will perceive, I say, if I am unjustly and injuriously agitated, what sort of man I am. And I will even say boldly, that neither to you, nor to anyone of the same ilk as you, will I ever give place in any science whatsoever. But as for you, Mersenne, not only I know, but indeed anyone else who is able to judge colors, can clearly discern that in any science whatsoever, you are a man who is entirely superficial, and clearly of no depth; seeing that you, with your boastful speeches, seem to be more than you actually are or ever will be. 

Behold, now you have a true prophecy from your own hands. But we also gather this from the observation of the hands of him who wrote that Babylonian volume <note>Fludd refers to Mersenne's massive work, "Questions on Genesis," as "Babylonian" to imply it is a confused, prideful tower of noise</note> concerning the "most celebrated questions," as he says, on Genesis. A title indeed of great expectation, but weighed with a slightly more mature gaze, it seems to verify that fable of Aesop: *The mountains are in labor, and a ridiculous mouse is born* <note>original: "Parturiunt montes, exit ridiculus mus"; a famous proverb used to describe grand promises that result in trivial outcomes</note>. 

And certainly, as that volume is large enough, so indeed I have dared to assert that two-thirds of it are taken from the notes in the books of other authors; for he himself seems to fill pages full of their opinions under the pretext of refutation, but in the end, he is not accustomed to produce any solid reasons. Indeed, even—and what is worst—he makes use of the doctrine of those whom he accuses of ignorance and folly: among whom I have observed that he has usurped and whispered my very words in many places of his volume, and sometimes full speeches (my name being forgotten); so that I can cite various pages in my *Microcosm* <note>Fludd’s magnum opus, "The History of the Two Worlds," where he discusses the human as a "little world" mirroring the universe</note> (esteemed so little by him) from which he himself received his subject and method, falsely arrogating to himself those things which are mine. Thus also in the beginning of his volume, in his dedicatory letter to God, he imitated me. But to the matter.

### 5. Marin’s Text. in the same place.

> *But if you are willing to cast away your trifles with which your books abound, especially heresy, he will congratulate you from the heart and will speak with you as a friend concerning certain sciences, whether in person with a living voice or from afar by letters; whom I shall ask not to write anything against you until you have satisfied him and surrendered to the truth <note>original: "manus veritati dederit"; literally "given your hands to the truth," an idiom for admitting defeat or yielding</note>, so that when, with the help of the grace of the divine power, you have received yourself among the sons of God and of the Catholic Church, and have abjured your errors, this man may exist as a friend to you, and you may together compose divine praises eternally in the blessed fatherland <note>Heaven</note>; otherwise you will be tortured by eternal flames, since it is equally certain that heretics and those alienated from the Catholic faith, which your ancestors embraced, will be damned if they die stubborn in that heresy, as it is certain that God himself is truthful and his word infallible; wherefore take your conscience and yourself seriously to heart.*

Mersenne, in turn, warns Fludd to renounce his "heretical" scientific views and return to the Catholic Church or face eternal damnation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s fierce intellectual and theological counter-attack against the French mathematician and priest Marin Mersenne. Following his defense of his character against charges of "evil magic," Fludd now pivots to a direct challenge of Mersenne's religious authority, questioning whether Mersenne himself meets the biblical criteria for a true "Catholic" or if he is merely a man of words without spiritual power.</meta>

# Of the Contest, Book III. Robert’s Examination.

As for my heresy (O Marin), let me say the same thing that Augustine said: *I may err, but I refuse to be a heretic* <note>original: "errare possum, hæreticus esse nolo"; a famous sentiment attributed to St. Augustine expressing a willingness to be corrected by the Church despite personal errors.</note>. For if I acknowledge myself a subject of the true Church before God—who is the searcher of hearts and minds—if my whole hope and salvation rest upon the spiritual rock; if I confess with the Patriarch <note>The biblical Patriarch Jacob, who set up a stone at Bethel</note> that the stone he set up as a monument is the house of God, as it was the temple of the Spiritual Rock, which is Christ himself (just as the human body is called a temple of the Holy Spirit); if that stone is universal insofar as Christ is everywhere and fills all things; if, therefore, my hope, my faith, my anchor, or the tried, chosen, and cornerstone—the one and only soul of the whole Catholic <note>Fludd often uses "Catholic" in its literal Greek sense of "Universal," referring to a mystical, world-encompassing spirit, whereas Mersenne uses it to mean the Roman Catholic Church.</note> world—is in this Christ, in this stone, or this rock from which (as the prophet witnesses) every man is carved; and if I believe my salvation and my life are hidden within it, should I not truly be considered a Catholic? 

But if this is heresy, where then will they finally find the truth? Since I acknowledge no particular or limited thing, but this Catholic Rock—the spiritual Rock, I say, most full of the Holy Spirit—and adore it with true worship in my heart. But you assert in many places in your volume that Fludd is a heretic, while you strive to proclaim to the whole world that you yourself are entirely Catholic. So be it, then; let it be that you are such a great Catholic, and may God grant it. 

But Fludd utterly denies that he is a heretic until he perceives you to be such a distinguished Catholic by the signs that truly belong to a Catholic. For where power and virtue are lacking, there follows the suspicion of a true Catholic; for that is a most wretched beginning which has no corresponding praiseworthy effect. Truly, virtue consists in action, and a "real" Catholic holds power in his right hand, as they say. This also seems to be verified by the thread of the Apostle’s discourse: <margin>1 Corinthians 4</margin> *That I may know not the speech of those who are puffed up, but their power.* <note>original: "Ut agnoscam nō sermonem eorum, qui inflati sunt sed potentiam siue virtutem."</note>

If, therefore, you are such a Catholic who can demonstrate your truth not with words, but with deeds and power, confirmed by the signs of the Catholic faith (for it is your office and profession, if you are a true Doctor, to preach and teach the kingdom of God), gird up your loins now and show forth an act of your virtue which has been hidden until this day. For the kingdom of God consists not in talk or writings, but in virtue and power. If you can actually reveal the signs of the true Catholic faith to the world, O Marin, behold, you will find Robert most obedient to all your commands! 

But if you have not been at all practiced in the true signs of faith, I cannot place any certain faith in you—nor indeed (to speak with the Apostle) in an Angel from heaven preaching another Gospel. It is necessary, therefore, that I first bring you to the touchstone <note>original: "Lydio lapidi"; literally a "Lydian stone," used since antiquity to test the purity of gold. Fludd uses it here as a metaphor for a spiritual test.</note> to clarify this doubt, so that by its examination we may see whether you are as much of a Catholic as you strive to proclaim and boast yourself to be to the world. In these words, the Savior of the world addressed the Apostles after his resurrection:

> *Go into the whole world and preach the Gospel to every creature. He who believes and is baptized will be saved; but he who does not believe will be condemned. And these are the signs that will follow those who believe:*
> 1. *In my name they will cast out demons.*
> 2. *They will speak with new tongues.*
> 3. *They will pick up serpents.*
> 4. *If they drink anything deadly, it will not hurt them.*
> 5. *They will lay hands on the sick, and they will recover, etc.* <note>A translation of Mark 16:15-18.</note>

Indeed, that Word promised true believers that they would perform not only these things, but even much greater ones, and bring them to a happy conclusion. Behold, therefore, here is that touchstone to whose examination you are reduced (O boastful Mersenne), so that it may appear to the whole world whether you are truly a real, or rather a False Catholic. For the faith of the Church (says the Apostle) is not in the wisdom of men, but in the power of God. Whoever, therefore, is destitute of essential faith, cannot be of the Catholic Church.

<summary>Robert Fludd challenges Marin Mersenne to prove his religious authority not through insults, but through the performance of biblical miracles. Fludd argues that true "Catholicism" is defined by spiritual power and virtue rather than mere institutional belonging or academic argument.</summary>
<keywords>Robert Fludd, Marin Mersenne, heresy, Catholic faith, spiritual power, touchstone, biblical signs, miracles, 1 Corinthians 4, Mark 16</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s theological defense against Marin Mersenne. In the previous page, Fludd defined his "Catholicism" not as an institutional membership, but as a mystical union with the "Spiritual Rock" of Christ. Here, he shifts from defining his own faith to scrutinizing Mersenne’s, using the biblical standard that "faith without works is dead" to challenge Mersenne’s spiritual authority.</meta>

# Of Wisdom with Folly

...it can be, and although some things seem to be imbued with faith: yet the source of that faith is from human Wisdom, which is of a hypocritical nature—appearing to be such when in truth it is not. Therefore, the Apostle seems to distinguish this kind of adulterated faith from that which arises from the divine source in these words, and especially makes this sort of preparation for his division: *the proof of your faith,* he says, *will be found much more precious than gold which is tested by fire, etc.* <note>original: "fidei... vestræ probatio, multò prætiosior inuenietur auro quod per ignē probatur"; a reference to 1 Peter 1:7. Fludd argues that true faith must survive a "fiery" trial to be proven genuine.</note>
<margin>1 Peter 1</margin>
By these words he argues that there must be a fiery testing by which truth is distinguished from fiction; then Saint James continues thus: *Faith works together with good works, and by works faith is made perfect:* for faith, he says, *cannot be shown without works, etc.* From this it is clear that true and living faith consists in virtue and power: by which it is concluded that the faith which is destitute of these is false, spurious, and dead, which indeed he confirms elsewhere: *Faith, if it does not have works, is dead in itself: wherefore also elsewhere in the same chapter, If anyone says he has faith, but does not have works, it profits him nothing, etc.* From this it follows that your faith will have profited little toward Salvation unless it is adorned and accompanied by the appropriate works of faith. But he also confirms this in the same chapter, saying that *Faith will not be able to save him if it does not have works.* But
<margin>
2 Thessalonians 1
1 Corinthians 13
</margin>
*the work of faith* (says Saint Paul) *is fulfilled in power, so that the name of our Lord Jesus Christ may be glorified in us, and we in him:* Hence he says elsewhere: *By faith mountains are moved, but without charity, it is nothing.* And the Savior says: *through faith, nothing is impossible.*

What then do you say, O Marin? <note>Marin Mersenne (1588–1648), the French polymath and priest Fludd is debating.</note> You see that I do not play at your manner, you who toy with fantastical lines in the hand, but I deal with you in more serious matters, by which we may discern whether you are truly such a great Catholic. If, therefore, you were a true Catholic, and were adorned with true faith, and if you were enriched by divine virtue as the companion of faith, then that could be known and uncovered in action and works. As the Apostles teach us: which works the Evangelist Mark specifically proposes in the place cited before. Come then, Marin, and gird your loins with the belt of your confidence and the zone of truth: In the name of Jesus, cast out demons; speak in new tongues; take up the serpents infesting the world by your virtue; drink poison, for it cannot harm so great a Catholic; lay your hands upon the sick, so that by your confidence you may restore them to their former health. Why not, since you are such a great Catholic? But truly, for Catholics to lack faith is impossible. But (to justly hurl your own words back at you) whatever you may have spoken, I know that you will produce none of the aforementioned signs, and consequently, I will not give a flock of wool <note>original: "ne floccum dabo"; a Latin idiom meaning "I won't give a straw" or "I don't care at all."</note> for that feigned faith of yours. Since this is how it stands, what, I pray, is the difference between you, a Catholic in name, and me, a "heretic" so falsely and repeatedly called by you? O Marin, attend to the words
<margin>Matthew 7:5</margin>
of our Savior saying: *Hypocrite, take the beam out of your own eye, so that you may see better to extract the speck that is in your brother’s eye, etc.* Do not be, therefore, if you are wise, so vain in the future as to proclaim others as heretics as if with the blast of a trumpet, unless you have been called into the rank of worthy true Catholics by divine permission; for if you are not endowed with true faith, the sentence of the scriptures is that you shall be damned. Therefore, it matters nothing whether one is a Catholic in your way or a heretic in mine, as you say, since both of us—if destitute of true faith—cannot endure or tolerate the final punishment, which is damnation: whence our only hope is in Jesus Christ our Savior, whose task alone it is to bring salvation to both you and me by his blood and to snatch us from the jaws of damnation, without the possession of whose mystery (nonetheless) the way to salvation will be arduous—I will not say impossible, insofar as nothing is impossible for God—and consequently, it is not likely that you can produce the effects of true faith.

We conclude, therefore, that if you, Mersenne, can respond directly to this doubt, we shall also not 
deign to refuse to give a resolution to the question of <term>Chiroscopia</term> <note>From the Greek 'cheir' (hand) and 'skopein' (to look); refers here to chiromancy or palmistry, which Mersenne apparently used as an example or challenge to Fludd.</note> proposed by you, even though it is 
altogether vain and frivolous.

Fludd dismisses Mersenne’s accusations of heresy, asserting that salvation depends on Christ's mystery rather than sectarian labels or frivolous studies like palmistry.</summary>

He begins Book Four by directly quoting his adversary, Marin Mersenne, and then mocking Mersenne’s lack of philosophical depth in attempting to refute Fludd’s interpretation of the Hebrew letters in the Book of Genesis.</meta>

# BOOK FOUR
## In which
## ROBERT FLUDD
## DESCENDS INTO THE DEFENSE OF HIS CABALA <note>The "Cabala" (or Kabbalah) refers to a tradition of Jewish mysticism that Fludd adapted into a Christian Hermetic framework to explain the structure of the universe.</note> AGAINST THE EMPTY OPPOSITIONS OF MARIN MERSENNE and his trifling quibbles, extirpating them at their roots, and denouncing them as stupid and futile.

---

### CHAPTER I.
*Which examines Marin Mersenne’s censure of the Cabalistic exposition of the holy letters.*

*1. Marin’s Text from Column 716.*

<margin>P.</margin>
<image-desc>Decorative initial letter A with ornate floral and foliage patterns filling the square background.</image-desc>
Before I put an end to this question, several things must be noted concerning what Robert Fludd has written in *Bereshit* <note>original: "בראשית" (Bereshit); the first word of the Hebrew Bible, meaning "In the beginning."</note> in Volume 2 of the *History of the Preternatural World* in the prelude. There, he says that the Cabalistic reason for the order of the whole creation of the world can be investigated through a diligent observation of the characters <note>letters</note> of that word. He claims that the letter *Resh* <note>original: "ר" (Resh)</note> denotes the empty, the void, and the darkness, as it is a symbol of poverty and want.
<margin>n.</margin>
He argues that this void was filled by the bright presence of Jehovah, and that without that bright and fiery Spirit illuminating the waters, it remained empty. He says that *Aleph* <note>original: "א" (Aleph)</note> is the middle and the beginning by which the empty earth—the squalid poverty of the *Resh* or the dark abyss itself—is removed. For he says that God was *Aleph*, dark with respect to us when he reflected his heat into himself;
<margin>n.</margin>
but *Aleph* <note>original: "א"</note> becomes manifest when he wished to break out, as if from the *Resh*, and reveal himself. For this reason, *Bereshit* <note>In the beginning</note> is followed by *Elohim* <note>original: "אלהים" (God)</note>, as if he were appearing out of the darkness or a cloud.

->Robert's Examination.<-

<margin>P.</margin>
Robert freely confesses and acknowledges that he spoke in exactly this sense. But how does Marin respond to this? Or what part of it did he even dare to attack? It is not enough to repeat the opponent's writings word for word; one must also bring to light reasons fitting for a good Philosopher, by which those things that deserve to be refuted can be correctly disproved and rejected. There is indeed no doubt (diligent readers) that this "good Monk," <note>Fludd is speaking sarcastically of Mersenne, who was a member of the Order of Minims.</note> even after inspecting the matter with sharp eyes,
<margin>n.</margin>
will produce no greater fruit in his refutation and condemnation of my Cabala than he did in his attacks on my *Physics* and *Worldly Harmony*. You can see for yourselves in the first part of this work what the nature of those attacks was: namely, they were entirely destitute of philosophical reason, though full of careless words. Let us proceed, then, so that we may more clearly perceive the "virtue" of this arrogant man in his refutation of the aforementioned Cabala.

*2. Marin’s text in the same place.*

He adds many other things, proposing to dictate in this book of three parts concerning the three primary and uncreated Metaphysical principles, which he calls archetypal, supersubstantial elements and mystical characters, appropriated to the name of Jehovah alone. These are *YHWH* <note>original: "יהוה" (the Tetragrammaton)</note>, which signify Light, Spirit, and Fire. He then adds three secondary Physical principles according to the pattern of the characters of *YHWH*. Finally, he introduces three Microcosmic principles: body, Spirit, and soul.

Fludd argues that the very letters of the word "Bereshit" (In the Beginning) reveal the mechanics of creation, moving from darkness and poverty to divine light.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the heated debate between the English physician and mystic Robert Fludd and the French polymath Marin Mersenne. Fludd is responding to Mersenne’s critiques of his "Cabalistic" interpretation of the Bible. The previous page introduced Fludd's specific use of Hebrew letters—such as Resh—to explain the creation of the world out of the void.</meta>

<header>Of Wisdom with Folly <note>original: "De Sophiæ cum Moria"; This is the running head for Fludd's work "Sophia cum Moria Certamen" (The Contest of Wisdom with Folly).</note></header>
<meta>catchword: Robert</meta>

# Robert’s examination.

He speaks excellently: for what has been reported by him in this place, we recognize as having been collected from our own volume. He delights in repeating the words of others like a parrot. But what is this toward a refutation or a correction? Shall we hear of the fault? Who or where is the error? Is it enough, forsooth, to measure the words of other authors against schoolboys? Let us therefore approach his "Herculean" virtue in his refutation of the Fluddian Cabala <note>Fludd is being sarcastic, mocking Mersenne's supposedly "mighty" effort to disprove his work.</note>.

# CHAPTER II.

## In which the vain and empty judgment of Mersenne on the Fluddian Cabala is laid open, and its validity is rendered plainly crippled and of no importance to the learned.

### 3. Marin’s Text in the same place.

*Regarding all these things, I deliver this judgment, lest anyone be deceived by Robertine speculations, or waste time in vain. What he holds concerning the Cabala in this prelude and throughout the book either contains only what theologians and philosophers commonly teach and individual Catholics know, or it proposes falsehoods. If the first, there is no reason we should be detained by reading it, because theologians and any simple commentary on Genesis clearly and distinctly opens up what he proposes obscurely and confusedly through characters <note>original: "characteribus"; here meaning the mystical use of letters or symbols.</note>—not to say he infects and perverts them—which characters he does not understand, or at least establishes and explains without sufficient foundation. Add that there are no such characters as he presents, as has been sufficiently said elsewhere, since we are overturning the Jewish Cabala from its foundations.*

# Robert’s examination.

In this text of his, three things come to light worthy of observation: namely, the bestial judgment of the author—bestial, I say, as being destitute of reason; his ignorance and uncertainty regarding Robert’s Cabalistic science; and finally, the denial of Cabalistic characters. Concerning these three in their order.

<margin>Text.</margin>
And in the first place he shows his judgment concerning Robert’s Cabala by warning the literate without any other reason given, *“lest any of them be deceived by Robertine speculations or waste time in vain.”* Is it thus, Marin, that you approach judgment after the manner of an "ass at the lyre" <note>An ancient proverb (Asinus ad lyram) referring to someone who has no appreciation or understanding of the art they are judging.</note> or like the horse Bayard <note>original: "equi Bagardi"; a reference to 'Blind Bayard,' a legendary horse used in proverbs to describe someone who acts boldly but is completely blind to the truth.</note> (as the proverb goes), judging colors in your drunken fashion like a blind man, with the cause not at all weighed or driven by solid arguments of either Philosophy or Theosophy? Is this the virtue of your Philosophy and Theosophy, by which you boast so triumphantly to refute so many learned men named in the title or frontispiece of that discourse of ours? Do you believe anyone, unless they are a fool and buried in ignorance, will give you any credit in a matter of such importance, unless you attack these refutations of yours with stronger arguments? As if, in a womanly fashion <note>Fludd employs a common early modern trope suggesting that arguing "a thing is bad simply because it is bad" is emotional rather than logical.</note>, you prove a thing to be bad simply because it is bad? Is this not a bestial and plainly irrational progression toward judgment—namely, to condemn a thing because it is unknown and ignored by you, as is clearly evident from your writings? Which is also very easily gathered from the following portion of your text, in which you argue your own ignorance and uncertainty regarding Fludd’s Cabala, and that in express words. For the text is as follows:

<margin>Text.</margin>
> *IF THE FIRST (that is, if it be true) there is no reason we should be detained by reading, because theologians and any simple commentary on Genesis clearly and distinctly opens up what he proposes obscurely and confusedly through characters, not to say he infects and perverts them—which characters he does not understand, or at least establishes and explains without sufficient foundation, etc.* These are your words, Marin.

<summary>Robert Fludd defends his mystical "Fluddian Cabala" by mocking Marin Mersenne's dismissal of it, accusing Mersenne of being an irrational and "blind" critic who lacks the philosophical depth to understand his work. Fludd argues that Mersenne's judgment is based on ignorance rather than solid theological or philosophical arguments.</summary>

<keywords>Robert Fludd, Marin Mersenne, Cabala, Genesis, refutation, characters, symbols, theology, philosophy, blind bayard, ass at the lyre</keywords>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s rebuttal against Marin Mersenne. Fludd is responding to Mersenne’s claim that Fludd's use of "characters" (mystical symbols and Hebrew letters) is unnecessarily confusing compared to standard biblical commentaries. Fludd turns this argument back on Mersenne, accusing him of admitted ignorance regarding the Cabala.</meta>

<page-num>115</page-num>
<header>On the Contest, Book IV. Robert.</header>

By which things they are permitted to see—what you yourself do not know—what he intends through that Cabala of his; and yet you, led by a corrupted conscience, hasten toward your rash and intoxicated judgment. But what is most ridiculous in this part of your text is when he says: *Theologians and some simple commentary on Genesis clearly and distinctly open up what he proposes obscurely and confusedly through characters, etc.* From which it follows that "some simple commentary on Genesis" is far more sagacious and learned than Marin, since he himself will do more than you, Marin, have accomplished in your confused and undigested commentary. Indeed, if some simple commentator understands the mysteries of my characters so easily, why is it that such a great Doctor as you are understands nothing of this, since you seem uncertain whether Fludd’s Cabala is true or false? Furthermore, it is necessary (as the Proverb goes) that a liar have a good memory.

For here he acknowledges it to be possible that the Cabalistic intention of our characters could tend toward Mosaic doctrine. If that is so, then he seems in this place to prove and confirm my Physical doctrine—which was previously condemned by him for no just preceding reason. For ר **Resh** denotes darkness; ש **Shin**, light flashing out of the darkness; and א **Aleph**, the lucid source from which it passed. For according to all those more skilled in the Cabala (as is found in Reuchlin <note>Johannes Reuchlin (1455–1522) was a pioneering German humanist and a key figure in Christian Cabala.</note>), ר **Resh** signifies obscurity, squalor, and emptiness; ש **Shin** signifies light, splendor, and clarity emanating from a lucid source; and א **Aleph** signifies unity and God the Father Himself, from whom they argue all things proceed. They spoke of this character insofar as He contracted the emissions of holy rays into Himself, and then by this character the Cabalists (whom he here attempts so falsely to condemn) understood the "hidden God," of whom Hermes Trismegistus <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure associated with the *Hermetica*, texts on philosophy, magic, and alchemy.</note> speaks thus: *The Monad generates the Monad <margin>Psalm 18:11.</margin> and reflected the heat into itself.* And the royal Psalmist says: *He made darkness his secret place.* But truly, א **Aleph** emitting its own emanation (which the Holy Scriptures call Wisdom itself and the Word) is called the "lucid Aleph" by the Cabalists, not without reason, inasmuch as it illuminates all things in the world with its rays (as is clearly indicated by Moses). Hence, therefore, you have the true principles or foundations of the Mosaic Philosophy, concerning which I shall speak more broadly and in a fuller style *below*.

->You see here, Mersenne, how we have the foundations of my Philosophy and consequently of the Worldly Harmony confirmed by your own mouth.<-

Finally, from these things it is clearly indicated, for the purpose of refuting your second clause, that I have not established the Cabala, and consequently my Philosophy, and exposed it to the public without a sufficient foundation.

Then you proceed in your usual manner of fabricating, saying: <margin>Text.</margin> *Lest I say he infects and perverts those characters he does not understand, or at least established without sufficient foundation, etc.* To which I reply thus: Truly, I would not wish for you to inflict a sign of ignorance upon me for this reason—namely, that you yourself have never found truth and desirable fruit in the true Cabala. Just as you confessed in open speech in your marginal annotation on a certain Problem of "the Venetian" <note>This likely refers to Francesco Giorgio (Venetus), an Italian Franciscan friar and author of *De Harmonia Mundi*, whose work influenced Fludd and was criticized by Mersenne.</note>; these are your words:

<margin>Column 282.</margin>
> *If anyone is wise, I advise him not to spend good hours in the reading of the Cabalists, unless he wishes to waste them; I speak from experience, for if it happens that they join anything good with trifles and fables, we have it more clearly, briefly, and subtly deduced in Philosophy and Theology, etc.*

These are your words, Mersenne, in which you do not blush to acknowledge your ignorance in that very science in open words. Is an art (true and constant in itself) therefore to be condemned because the practitioner himself is a fake and perhaps unworthy of such great knowledge?

<meta>The catchword "Falso" (Falsely) indicates the first word of the next page, continuing Fludd's critique of Mersenne's judgment.</meta>

<summary>Robert Fludd defends his use of Hebrew letters (Resh, Shin, Aleph) as foundational symbols for his "Mosaic Philosophy," representing the emergence of light from darkness. He mocks Marin Mersenne for criticizing the Cabala while simultaneously admitting in other writings that he does not understand it and considers it a waste of time.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s vigorous defense against the attacks of Marin Mersenne. On the previous page, Fludd pointed out Mersenne’s admitted ignorance of the Cabala, arguing that if even a "simple commentator" can understand Fludd’s symbols, Mersenne’s inability to do so reveals his own lack of scholarly depth.</meta>

<page-num>116</page-num>
<header>On the Contest of Wisdom with Folly</header>
<meta>catchword: Robert’s</meta>

Therefore, you have set out to refute the Cabalists <note>Practitioners of Cabala, a Jewish mystical tradition that Robert Fludd and other Renaissance thinkers adapted into a Christian framework to understand the mysteries of God and nature.</note> and the Rabbis falsely and in an impious manner, since you have never attained a deep understanding of their knowledge or intentions. You have behaved unworthily toward that learned man, Francesco Giorgio of Venice <note>original: "Georgium Venetum." Francesco Giorgio (1466–1540) was a Franciscan friar and author of *De Harmonia Mundi*, a work that deeply influenced Fludd’s views on the musical and mathematical proportions of the universe.</note>, whose opinions you profess to refute and correct, even though you do not seem to understand a single iota—to speak truly—of his meaning, his knowledge of the Cabala, or indeed of his "World Harmony" itself. Nor will you ever reach the profound understanding of his "key" unless you turn your malevolent disposition toward all those who have served scholarship well into something better.

->Marin’s text in the same place.<-

*Therefore, there is no reason for him to boast about those frivolous inventions; if anyone applauds them, I shall not hesitate at all to judge that person as being idle and far too addicted to the Cabalists.*

->Robert’s examination.<-

Robert does not boast in his works, O Marin, as you suppose; nor does he, after your fashion, belittle the works of others. But I would like you to prove—and with a more accurate core of your own knowledge—that the works he has published, whether Physical or Cabalistic, are frivolous and mere trifles. In truth, you do not know how to prove anything against them using true arguments or the strength of reason. Instead, you have become quite expert at condemning and belittling through slanders and defamations in a "Cynic" manner <note>Referring to the Cynic school of philosophy, known in Fludd's time for biting, snarling criticism.</note>, giving no evident reason. The evidence of this is everywhere; experience teaches it.

->Marin’s text in the same place.<-

*If only that man Fludd and people of his kind would apply their industry to illustrating or restoring certain arts or sciences, and not waste it on trifles <note>original: "titiuillitiis." This term refers to "Titivillus," a demon in medieval folklore said to collect errors in scribal work or speech. Here it means "nonsense" or "useless scraps."</note>; they would surely earn the gratitude of the entire Christian Commonwealth, whereas now they provoke either laughter or tears.*

->Robert’s examination.<-

How beautiful these words are! As if our works and labor should have been devoted to him. Here we understand the man’s mindset. But could anyone spend their effort and "oil" <note>An idiom for late-night study or "burning the midnight oil."</note> better or with greater utility than in correcting and purging the errors of the Heathen <note>original: "Ethnicorum." This refers to non-Christian, particularly ancient Greek and Roman, philosophers whose ideas Fludd felt had corrupted Christian thought.</note> philosophers that have infested the Christian world for so many centuries—not by his own invention, but by the testimony and authority of Holy Scripture—and by clearly dragging them out from their blind and dark corners? Would such a work and a business of such great momentum seem worthy of laughter or tears to any wise man? If he means the foolish and the envious, certainly laughter is appropriate for such people; for according to that old verse:

> By much laughter, you may recognize a fool.

But let us proceed in our examination; for we eagerly await the strength of this "Herculean" man’s refutation.

---

### CHAPTER III.

->*In which the exceptional virtue of that boastful Monk is seen in his refutation of Fludd’s Cabala.*<-

->Marin’s Text. Column in the same place & 717.<-

He says in vain that we are ignorant of these characters <note>Mystical symbols, often incorporating Hebrew letters or geometric shapes, used in Fludd's diagrams.</note> and do not understand them: for example, what he himself intends by the "Olympic Spirits" and the "personal Genius" of each individual, which he teaches in his *History of the Microcosm*, book 3, part 4, section 2, treatise 1, from page 85 to 116. For it is clear enough from which parent he drank these magical trifles: namely, from Agrippa <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535), a famous German polymath and occult writer.</note>, Pietro d'Abano <note>original: "Abbano." Pietro d’Abano (c. 1257–1315) was an Italian philosopher and astrologer often accused of magic.</note>, or similar monsters. But whether he is a Magician or not, I do not judge the man’s soul—it is God who searches the reins and the hearts—but as an example to others and for the edification of Christians, he ought to be punished with no less a penalty than a Magician, etc.

Note here (good Reader) that in his marginal note pertaining to this passage, we find these words: *The folly and errors of Robert.* But to the matter at hand.

<summary>Robert Fludd defends his "Mosaical" philosophy against Marin Mersenne, accusing the monk of failing to understand the Christian Cabala of Francesco Giorgio and relying on insults rather than reason. Mersenne counters by labeling Fludd’s interest in planetary spirits and occult authors like Agrippa as "magical trifles" deserving of punishment.</summary>

<header>On the Contest, Book V. Robert’s examination.</header>

We indeed expected a *Philosophical Refutation*, but look! In its place you have a spiteful dog returning to its vomit, and Marin returning to his malicious disposition, swelling with slanders. We expected (I say) Philosophical reasons, fitting for such a famous man, by which he might demonstrate that this Cabala of ours is so useless to the World (as he says). But alas, in the manner of a Pythoness <note>A priestess of Apollo at Delphi, often used by Christian writers to denote one possessed by a demonic or frenzied spirit rather than divine inspiration.</note> he seems to rave without any piety. Perhaps one of Fludd’s *Olympic Demons* (as he is so fond of repeatedly calling me a Magician) has stealthily crept into his brain; otherwise, what is it that so vehemently vexes and disturbs the man, causing him to wander from human reason into such a beastly sense? *But Fludd (he says), whether he be a Magician or not, is to be punished with no less a penalty than a Magician, for the example of others and the edification of Christians.* O unjust Judge! Unworthy Monk! Who, when he ought to have imitated Christ by office, does not blush to profess himself the son of the Devil at first sight. *Let him be punished (he says), whether he acted justly or unjustly.* Thus those wickedest Jews cried out against the innocent Christ (the cause having been minimally weighed): *Let him be crucified, let him be crucified.* *Judge not (says Christ), for with what judgment you judge, you shall be judged.* <note>Matthew 7:1-2</note> But to the purpose:

As for the first portion of this text, where he asserts that Robert claimed he and others of his ilk were ignorant of their own characters: I say indeed, and say again, that I do not hesitate at all in that statement of mine. For as I truly think, and perceive now without any further risk of doubt from his own writings, Marin himself, however puffed up with self-love <term>philautia: a Greek term for excessive self-love or conceit</term>, has by no means understood the true characters of the faithful Cabalists. And nevertheless, he boasts that he has refuted and abolished the Cabalistic letters or characters (which are truly divine and eternally lasting), indeed, crying out with a profane mouth that *there are no such characters*, etc. He hardly understands that these were the primordial Elements of created things, called (as they rightly will it) *hylementa* <note>A portmanteau of the Greek "hyle" (matter) and "elementa" (elements), suggesting the material-spiritual building blocks of the universe.</note>—that is, fiery, living, and truly formal characters, depicted and engraved upon the obscure face or surface of the *hyle* <note>The primal, unformed matter of the universe.</note> or of the shapeless darkness by the finger of the living God himself, and brought forth from His mouth in a mystical manner. This the those more skilled in the true Cabala seem to confirm, affirming that God wrote His law in a Fiery Globe, by dark fire upon white fire <note>A traditional Cabalistic description of the Torah's spiritual essence: "black fire on white fire."</note>. Therefore, the 22 Hebrew letters were formed in the image of these characters; yet they are as far distant in nature and disposition from those formal and fiery ones (by means of which the World and all things in the World were originally forged) as a shadow from the truth, darkness from light, body from soul, a scabbard from a sword, and—to speak with the Apostle—*the letter from the Spirit: for the former kills, but the latter gives life.* <note>2 Corinthians 3:6</note> And this is the reason why Mersenne tries to strip all power from the external characters of the Hebrews: because he himself, occupied only with them in his studies, has lost his labor and oil <note>original: "operam suam & oleum perdidit." A Latin idiom for wasting time and effort.</note>; namely, because having neglected the Center, he deals with the Circumference and externals—he works in the dung-heap (I say) with the pigs; he does not even think of the gem hidden within it.

Finally, what more should be said at this time about men of this sort, whose delight and pleasure consists in quarrels, insults, contentions, and fictions, except that they should attend a little more diligently to the teachings of the Royal Prophet [King David], and incline their ears to him who sings to this effect: *Who is that man who delights in life, loves days, and desires to enjoy good? Keep your tongue from evil, and do good: seek peace and pursue it.* <note>Psalm 34:12-14</note> For according to the Apostle's sentence: *Wisdom contradicting the truth is not that which descends from above from the Father of lights, but is earthly, sensual, and devilish. But that which is from above is first pure, then peaceable, modest, easy to be entreated, full of mercy and good fruits, not judging, without hypocrisy.* <note>James 3:15-17</note> Therefore, whether the wisdom of Mersenne be worldly (which the Apostle calls "foolishness") or divine (which he calls with Solomon "the power of God"), let the work...

<sig>P 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: work (referring to "oper-" at the end of the page)</meta>

<summary>Robert Fludd defends his Cabalistic philosophy against Marin Mersenne, accusing the monk of being a malicious judge and arguing that Mersenne only understands the "dead letter" of Hebrew characters while ignoring their "fiery" spiritual essence. Fludd contrasts Mersenne’s aggressive, "earthly" wisdom with the peaceful, divine wisdom described in the scriptures.</summary>

--- Page 127 ---
<meta>This page concludes Robert Fludd’s heated rebuttal against the French priest and mathematician Marin Mersenne. Following the previous page’s critique of Mersenne's "beastly" reasoning, Fludd now turns to scripture to categorize Mersenne’s behavior as fundamentally un-Christian.</meta>

# On [the Contest of] Wisdom with Folly

...his own effects seem to denote. But far be it from me that I should judge a brother; rather, I humbly commend both my own cause and that of Mersenne himself to Eternal Wisdom, as she is the Judge of Judges.

Nevertheless, I ask you (O learned judges of this world) to seriously consider those words brought forth by Solomon: and I entreat you to compare them, point by point, to each of the actions and conditions of Marin Mersenne enumerated in his Volume; so that it may afterward be in your power to render a just censure of him. <margin>Proverbs 16</margin> <note>While the margin says Proverbs 16, the text Fludd quotes is actually from Proverbs 6:16-19.</note> These are the words of the Wise Man: *These six things Jehovah hates: and the seventh his soul detests: Haughty eyes, a lying tongue, hands that shed innocent blood, a heart that devises wicked imaginations, one who forges lies: a false witness, and finally, one who sows discord among brothers.* If, therefore, you bring Marin Mersenne to this touchstone <note>original: "lapidem Lydium," a stone used to test the purity of gold, here used as a metaphor for a moral test.</note>, you will undoubtedly find him depicted in his own true colors. <margin>Pride</margin> For, led by pride and self-love <note>original: "Philautia," a Greek-derived term common in the Renaissance for excessive self-love or narcissism.</note>, he considers himself to be preferred above all others in science and learning. Thus, he delights in bringing into question, examining, judging, and refuting in his boastful manner not only the most famous philosophers and mathematicians—those most skilled in astrology and other sciences—and physicians most expert in their art, and Cabalists and Rabbis most profound in abstruse mysteries and elevated far beyond his grasp, but even Kings and Monarchs themselves, anointed by God. Whether, therefore, in this condition of his, he is abominable to God or not, I leave to you: it is not my place to judge a brother, but to pray from the heart that God, the Greatest and Best, might convert him for the better.

<margin>A deceitful tongue</margin> I wish also that his tongue were not deceitful, not to say lying; as it is one which brings forth fictions and falsehoods not only concerning me—sometimes calling me an Atheist and sometimes a heretical Magician <note>original: "Hæreticomagum," a compound word meaning a practitioner of forbidden magic who is also a religious heretic.</note>, and falsely imposing various crimes and outrages upon others. <margin>A bloodthirsty nature</margin> From this tongue, he has spoken unjustly against good men, according to the wicked will of his master. That his hands are also prone to shedding innocent blood is proved by that passage of his regarding me:

> *Whether (he says) Fludd be a Magician or not, he should nevertheless be afflicted with no less a punishment than a Magician, as an example to others.*

Thus he speaks: may God judge him, and not I, who am indeed unworthy to take the place of a Judge. Furthermore, he says to the men who are Alchemists:

> *Woe to those scoundrels! I wish a millstone were hung about their necks and they were drowned in the depths of the ocean.*

From these words, his bloodthirsty disposition clearly reveals itself. Finally, he calls almost all the most skilled men "insane," and declares that they should be given over to flames, waves, breaking wheels, and the most severe, unheard-of punishments as if he were a judge. Therefore, behold the man, pointed out as if by the finger of Solomon. <margin>Wicked imaginations</margin> His mind fabricates wicked thoughts: for not only does he maliciously rail against all the learned, but also, in many places of his Volume, led by a lying spirit, he has judged them iniquitously—fabricating things against them by wickedly twisting their meaning. <margin>A false witness</margin> That he is also a false witness is evident in this: that he has falsely suggested the crime of Atheism and heresy against very many who are, in truth, more sincere and faithful servants of the living God than Mersenne is or can be, unless he changes his ways. <margin>He forges lies</margin> Furthermore, he does not hesitate to falsely and injuriously accuse—indeed, to proclaim to the world—the Abbot Trithemius, Ficino, Bacon, Agrippa, and myself, along with many others, as evil magicians <note>original: "Cacomagis."</note> in several parts of his work: <margin>Col. 1151</margin> and what is more, he seems to spare neither Thomas Aquinas nor Albertus Magnus. <margin>Discord among brothers</margin> Finally, he sows discord among brothers, insofar as he attempts with all diligence to render men—eminent in knowledge both from the Roman religion and from other sects of the Christian religion—abominable to their brothers, as is clearly explained in the Catalog of Authors prefixed to this Treatise. These things having been considered with more mature reflection by you (just Readers), I would not dare to judge whether this man is, in his qualities, lovable or abominable to God; much less do I desire to condemn anyone. But I humbly commend myself, along with him, first to our God, and then to your more merciful Censure: praying God that He may preserve you safe and unharmed from the wretched lips of the malevolent and from viperous tongues.

->T H E  E N D.<-

<summary>This page concludes the alphabetical index of Robert Fludd’s work, summarizing his key philosophical, alchemical, and theological arguments while continuing his sharp rebuttal of Marin Mersenne. It highlights Fludd's synthesis of ancient wisdom (Pythagoras, Hermes Trismegistus) and Christian mysticism.</summary>
<keywords>Microcosm, Marin Mersenne, Robert Fludd, Alchemical Medicine, Archangels, World Soul, Monochord, Harmony of the Spheres, Hermes Trismegistus, Human Physiology</keywords>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page serves as the title page for the "Summum Bonum" (The Highest Good), a work published in 1629. It follows the index of Robert Fludd's "Certamen Sophiae cum Moria," continuing the intense intellectual and theological battle between Fludd (writing here under the pseudonym Joachim Frizius) and the French scholar Marin Mersenne. While the previous page indexed Fludd's general theories, this volume specifically defends the Rosicrucian Order and the "true" occult sciences against Mersenne’s accusations of magic and heresy.</meta>

<margin>
Compare among the Pietists <note>A movement within Lutheranism that emphasized individual piety</note> in octavo size, number 147, letter C: the *Apologetic Treatise* defending the integrity of the Society of the Rosy Cross.

Georg Serpilius, *On Rare Books*, Part II, page 237.

Johannes Vogt, *Catalog of Rare Books*, page 88.
</margin>

# THE HIGHEST
# GOOD,

->Which is<-

The True Subject of True { **MAGIC, KABBALAH, ALCHEMY** }
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;{ **and of the True Brothers of the Rosy Cross** }

*Published in praise of the aforementioned sciences, and to the disgrace of the notorious slanderer, Brother Marin Mersenne,*

->BY JOACHIM FRIZIUS.<-
<note>Joachim Frizius is a pseudonym for Robert Fludd, an English physician and mystical philosopher.</note>

<image-desc>A central woodcut emblem depicting a large, multi-petaled rose on a thorny stem with leaves. Arched above the rose is the Latin motto "THE ROSE GIVES HONEY TO THE BEES" <note>original: "DAT ROSA MEL APIBUS"</note>. Two bees are shown in flight, approaching the rose from the right. The background features a detailed landscape: on the left, a spiderweb is suspended between two wooden posts <note>The spider, representing the "slanderer" Mersenne, produces venom, while the bees, representing the true seekers or Rosicrucians, extract honey from the same source</note>; on the right, a row of three traditional beehives (skeps) rests on a wooden bench. In the far background, rolling hills and a shoreline are visible.</image-desc>

->IN THE YEAR 1629.<-

<summary>This is the title page of Robert Fludd's (writing as Joachim Frizius) "Summum Bonum," a 1629 defense of Rosicrucianism and the occult sciences against the criticisms of Marin Mersenne. The central imagery of the rose and bees serves as a metaphor for extracting spiritual truth from nature, contrasted against the "poison" of his detractors.</summary>

<keywords>Highest Good, Magic, Kabbalah, Alchemy, Rosicrucians, Marin Mersenne, Robert Fludd, Rose and Bees, Hermeticism, 17th-century polemics</keywords>
